The fake eyes keep a camera that can't move across a map narrower than the view, and on a map whose minimap picture is taller than its window show other players' dots only in the rows the window scrolls to. 16 scenarios are on West Rocky Mountain II (`*_rocky_*`) and 17 on Tree Dungeon (`*_tree_*`, three of them a Lie Detector during the hunt loop: On the Lie Detector): In game, on each.

**Its monsters** (`sim/world.py`) are as the game data has them where it has them (`Mob/<id>.img`, cached in `gamedata/data/out/mobs`; without it, the flat values from before):
- **A touch takes HP by the monster's touch damage:** 0.68 of its PADamage (snail 3, slime 13, pig 15, Orange Mushroom 17) of her 642 HP (her HP bar), give or take 70%. That is 1.6% on average for a pig, 1.4% for a slime, 1.8% for an Orange Mushroom and 0.3% for a snail, where every touch took 1.4%. It's fitted to `lab/hits.jsonl`: 100 hits on the grounds where only pigs and slimes spawn (the basin and 21) took 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 px of the 147 px bar 30, 32, 28, 9 and 1 times (2.19 px, 9.6 HP, on average), and the fit has 26, 33, 30, 9 and 0 (and 2 too small to show). How the game takes her defence off isn't known, and no touch by a snail or an Orange Mushroom alone was logged.
- **She evades a touch with a chance inversely proportional to the monster's accuracy** (acc: snail 33, slime 53, pig 54, Orange Mushroom 62; the form MapleStory's dodge rule is usually given in, unmeasured), fitted to the 13 touches logged hopping over pigs (7 MISS): a pig's half the time, a slime's 51%, an Orange Mushroom's 44%, a snail's 82%, where every kind's was half. In the harness she evades 50.5% (loop) and 52.5% (tour) of all touches.
- **A monster touches her by the box of the frame it shows** (its lt/rb in the game data: none of this map's monsters has an attack of its own, so the client hurts by the body), walking, standing, or flinching for 0.6 s after a hit (its hit1 frame), turned the way it faces. Before, every monster was its kind's half width either side and as tall as its first walking frame. A walking slime is in the air for 400 of its 1,050 ms, 10-17 units up and up to 66 tall (40-44 on the ground), so a hop over it has to clear that; a walking pig is 4 units up and 47 tall for 300 of its 500 ms; an Orange Mushroom reaches 36 behind it and 27 in front standing, and 67 tall for 120 ms of its walk. An attack still picks its target by the old bodies (`World.top`: each kind as tall as it walks, as the decisions' `reach.body_of` has it): by its frames a pig standing 60 below her (38-40 tall) would be picked from the ground only from 171 units sideways, not 151, and by a jump's area (its floor 19 below her feet, `reach.AREA_DOWN`) not at all, where in game pigs 60 below were hit (see Found offline).
- What the touch damage and evasion changed: of the golden traces 26 changed, every one with a touch in it, from its first touch's HP (on one, a snail's touch now evaded). `nav_monsters_staircase` moved to seed 1 (explained there). Harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes, against the code before: the hunt loop 123.3 -> 125.0 EXP a minute (better on 9 of 20 seeds, the worst 12% down), drops picked up 88.1% -> 86.3%, HP lost 2.55 -> 2.72 bars (181 touches hurt her, 182 before: the basin's pigs and slimes take a little more each); the tour 88.6 -> 89.6 (better on 13 of 20, the worst 24% down), drops 91.7% -> 93.8%, HP lost 2.52 -> 2.49 bars (168 touches hurt her, 180 before).
- What the frames changed: of the golden traces 28 changed, each first at a touch 5-95 ms earlier or later than before, or by another monster. Harness, as above: the hunt loop 125.0 -> 119.7 EXP a minute (better on 9 of 20 seeds), the tour 89.6 -> 87.8 (better on 9 of 20, the worst 20% down). On the loop's seed 19 a pig touched her as she dropped through 33 by the basin's right end, and the knock carried her past the end of 36, through the cliff below 35 (another foothold group's wall, which doesn't stop her in the air) and out of the map: she fell for the rest of the run (29 EXP a minute, 106 before). Without that seed the loop went 126.0 -> 124.4 and the tour 89.8 -> 87.5, with 1% and 4% more touches, drops picked up 85.7% -> 87.2% and 94.1% -> 93.4%, and as much HP lost (2.77 and 2.55 bars).
- **A hit pushes the monster back and stands it while it flinches** (`world.MOB_KNOCKBACK`): a star or a swipe that doesn't kill pushes it 18 units away from her over 0.25 s, and it stands in its hit1 frame until 0.6 s after the hit (that frame's delay), then walks or stands as it would. In the game data `pushed` is 1 for all four kinds: any damage pushes them. The distance is one measurement: a slime walking at her, swiped on the basin floor (`lab/recordings/do_attack2`, static camera, 30 fps), went 13 half-px (18 units) back over about 0.25 s and stood about 0.6 s, then walked at her again. A pig hit in `fight1` lost about 80 units of its walk (the camera moved, so that's no distance, but it fits a stop of 0.45-0.6 s and a short push). Pushed from where its walk turns, a monster stays on its platform, so none is pushed off an end: pigs have been seen on platforms where none spawn (one on 26), but how they get there isn't known. Before, a hit didn't slow a monster at all.
- What the push changed: of the golden traces 28 changed, each from a hit that didn't kill (a monster 18 units further off at the next star or touch), or from the start on the four that moved seed. `nav_fight_basin` (seed 2), `nav_fight_across` (10) and `hunt_kill` (1) moved seed (each explained there). Harness, as above: the hunt loop 119.7 -> 131.3 EXP a minute (better on 16 of 20 seeds, the worst 13% down), 359 -> 283 touches a run, HP lost 2.66 -> 2.01 bars, drops picked up 86.2% -> 90.1%; the tour 87.8 -> 93.8 (better on 15 of 20, the worst 6% down), 367 -> 317 touches, HP lost 2.49 -> 2.20 bars, drops 93.0% -> 94.2%. A monster she kept shooting hardly came nearer: shot every 0.85 s, it walked 0.25 s of it (17.5 units at the 70 units/s the simulator's monsters walked then) and was pushed back 18. They walk as fast as the game data says now (125 units/s for a pig: see Localization), so a pig shot every 0.85 s gains 13 units a hit.
- **All of it together**, against the simulator before (harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes): the hunt loop 123.3 -> 131.3 EXP a minute (better on 17 of 20 seeds, the worst 7% down), HP lost 2.55 -> 2.01 bars (182 -> 135 touches that hurt), drops picked up 88.1% -> 90.1%; the tour 88.6 -> 93.8 (better on 17 of 20, the worst 24% down), HP lost 2.52 -> 2.20 bars (180 -> 149), drops 91.7% -> 94.2%. She evades 52.5% (loop) and 53.1% (tour) of all touches. The world got easier, not harder: the push outweighs the rest. Without it (the frames, damage and evasion alone) it got a little harder: 119.7 and 87.8 EXP a minute.

- `python -m gamelab sim tour_run --speed 4` runs a scenario and records it for the mini-map: open `python -m gamelab minimap` first to watch it as it happens.
- `python -m gamelab.analysis.physics_check` replays her recorded movement (18 recordings, or the 6 kept as test fixtures) through the physics models and prints how far each is off (see Motion planning).
- `python -m gamelab.sim.golden check` reruns every scenario and compares it with the golden trace recorded earlier: every key plan, log line and result. A refactor that leaves those byte-identical hasn't changed what she does. `record` rewrites the traces (after an intended change). The traces hold map data, so they stay in `lab/golden` (git-ignored): a branch worked on in a worktree of its own re-records them in its own `lab`, so once it's merged, `record` is run in the main checkout too (with the feed's searches off the frame loop, the route track and the other players together, 32 of master's 86: the other players' and her EXP number's 27 and the five `feed_*`; Found offline).

The move into the layers above was checked this way: all 50 traces stayed identical, apart from two counters that were never read (`Report.out_of_reach`, `Hunter.skipped`), which were removed.

**Comparing policies** (`python -m gamelab.sim.harness`): `run POLICY` hunts 20 simulated minutes on each of 10 world seeds (`tour`, `loop`, or `loop-avoid`), and `compare BASE CAND` applies the bar seed by seed: more EXP a minute on 8 of 10 seeds and none more than 10% worse, at least the drops share, no more HP lost, no death, no fall out of the map. What a run gave is read from the simulated world, not from her own reports. Preset `game` adds the game's errors (27.5% of stars miss, hit monsters hidden for 1-3 s, 10% of looks miss a monster, her lags spread as measured, 60% of taps of 50 ms or less lost), arrows pressed while an attack plays swallowed as in game (`Realism.lock_arrows`: pressed less than 0.72 s after the key, seen only 0.77 s after it if still held, else never; the noisy scenarios have it too), 60% of spawns filled, another player killing a monster every 25 s (hunting one place at a time, its dot on the minimap: see Other players), and 60 potions (not monsters' poses read wrong, `Noise.pose_wrong`, which no preset has yet); `game-drops` has the drops seen in game, `game-air` jump throws missing a snail close by as often as in game (`Noise.air_snail`), `game-sparse` 15% of the spawns filled (the coverage test's world: Route coverage), each `game` otherwise, the arrows swallowed too; `plain` is the simulator's own crowded world; `--think 1` also charges the stack's real computing time to the clock (those runs aren't repeatable). `--costs measured` makes the simulator's looks as fast as they're measured after the profiling pass of Where the time goes (a look placing her 20 ms with its frame's wait and copy, her band 24 more, the whole screen 84, a strip of drops 8; as step 5 measured them, 24, 32, 122 and 8), not the 100 ms they were coded at; `--feed` takes her looks and glances from a continuous feed; `--ways 3` has her trips choose among 3 ways (Route diversity). `rests` compares trips to rest at low HP with and without `--rest-avoid`. Each run also counts her trips from the stack's log (`nav`: plans cut short by a hit and off the path, replans, misses and the falls among them, her glances), and `compare` shows their totals. Runs are saved in `lab/harness` (git-ignored), each with a tally of her moves for the coverage report (3-7 KB a run).

On the new maps: `--map 10004021` (West Rocky Mountain II) and `--map 10002073` (Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II) hunt there with her as she is there, from their own starting places, saved as `POLICY-PRESET-MAP`; `compare` refuses runs of two maps. `--world NAME` (again for another) switches on what isn't known: `zero-walls`, `client-sides`, `client-reach`, `touch-share` (a touch a share of PADamage, not the formula), `no-evade`, `red-potion` (her potions heal a Red Potion's 100 HP, not 30% of her HP), `client-body` (her body for the world's touches the client's, 22 either side and 65 tall as the stack plans by, `motion/bodies.py`, not the simulator's 15 x 55), `near-misses` (the detector's misses near her as live on 2026-10-25, `world.NEAR_SEEN`: Cutting her touches), and how she plays: `lucky-seven` (her attacks as Lucky Seven's thrown live, `tour --loop --skill`: 3 of the simulator's stars a cast, `world.LUCKY_SEVEN`, so a Horny Mushroom dies in about 4 throws and a Green in 3, as live, not 12 and 7), saved as suffixes. Each run also keeps the seconds she was hunting (`alive_s`: rates a minute alive, where a death would drag a rate over the horizon down), the touches by a monster just back at its spawn point (`fresh_hurt`: within `world.FRESH_S`, 1.5 s, of its respawn) the time the hunt loop waited for respawns and the HP lost then (`wait_s`, `wait_hp_lost`: its rules 'wait' and 'lull loot'), and the time she was on the move and the touches that hurt and HP lost then (`trip_s`, `trip_hurt`, `trip_hp_lost`: a chain's or a lone move's start to its end), and from 2026-10-26 on her stays for drops (`pile_s`, `pile_hurt`, `pile_hp_lost`), off her trips (`stand_hurt`, `stand_hp_lost`), her knocks off a platform (`knock_falls`, `knock_fall_dy`) and her pet's potions (`pet_potions`; her MP items `mp_potions`). `--grounds 23` (saved as `-g23`) has the policy hunt only those grounds, as the live hunt loop of 2026-10-24 hunted 23 alone. Presets `crowd`, `crowd-17` and `crowd-slow` are Tree Dungeon's alone: the other players the crowding study saw, camping (`harness.TREE_CAMPS`), every spawn filled, respawns at 8.8 s, 17 s (the live median on 23) or 30 s, other players' dots under hers unseen. `ropes` counts the hunt loop's waits on a rope. The policy `loop-hp50` is the hunt loop with `--low-hp 0.5`. A run that goes through a portal keeps `left_map_at` and fails the bar as a fall does. The coverage report reads either map's runs (`coverage RUN --map M`; the first map's own questions, platform 12 and the rope hops, only there).

**Soak runs** (`python -m gamelab.sim.soak`): the stack kept going for minutes on saved frames or in the simulator, to see whether its memory grows (see Memory and disk).

**The stack's own computing** (`python -m gamelab.sim.latency loop|tour [--seeds 5] [--minutes 10] [--profile FILE]`): the harness's hunts, with every stretch of the stack's computing between two of its calls to the game (a look, a key plan, a sleep) timed with the real clock, by the call that ended it, and not charged to the fake clock, so the runs are the harness's own. What a look's answer waits for before her next keys go out is the "before a key plan" row; in game it comes on top of the look itself (see Where the time goes).

Tests can't press keys, page anyone or write under `gamelab/lab` (`tests/conftest.py` stops them), and the simulator refuses to run unless its fakes are in place. The tests, the golden traces, the harness and the simulator's latency and soak runs take her stats as measured (SPEED 119.2, JUMP 100), not as `config.toml` `[character]` has them (`physics.FIXED_STATS`), so editing those for her gear changes nothing they check: with SPEED 125 in the file, 16 tests failed and every golden trace changed.

## Route coverage

`python -m gamelab coverage RUN... [--live]` (`analysis/coverage.py`; offline, no keys) reads saved harness runs and, with `--live`, the live telemetry (`lab/telemetry`, but the simulator's files), and says for each move of the route graph (its kind, platforms and where it starts: `jump 32->29 @536`) how often it was planned (every route record, replans too), taken (set off on, in a chain or made alone) and missed (she ended elsewhere, not from a hit), with where she ended instead (a chain hit on the way that still ended where it was to made every move; one a hit left elsewhere is a hit's); for each platform her visits and time (look to look, 5 s at most); the trips by how they ended, those that timed out before she set off apart; the tour's stops (its sweep's: the hunt loop's 'tour' records are its grounds) and the hunt loop's grounds. Which move of a chain missed is read from where she was seen standing in between: her looks and glances on a platform's surface (within 6 units) twice in a row, against the platforms the chain goes through. Each harness run keeps a tally of its own telemetry for it (`analysis.coverage.tally`, 3-7 KB a run; runs saved before 2026-10-21's evening have none). `--text FILE` writes the report, and each set of runs gets a picture of the map (`lab/coverage_<map>.png`, then `-2`, `-3`: taken in green, thicker the more often; planned but never taken in yellow; never planned in red).

For each move never taken: the least gap from the best route through it to the route chosen, by the planner's own times (no random weights, no penalties), on the plans that could have made it (but those that kept a ground off or avoided a monster); on how many of them it was within 35% of the chosen (the most the navigator's weights, 1 to 1.35 a move a trip, can make up); and why it lost: **replanned** (planned, but replanned before she got to it, or the trip over), **weights** (cheaper than the route chosen: the weights drew the other), **penalty** (cheaper, but its route went onto a ground the trip kept off, or through a move a monster was in the way of), **ground** (the quickest way between two places on the map, but no trip went between them), or **cost** (the quickest between no two places: from every 60 units along each platform to every other platform). A move flow can't chain from where it starts (no takeoff passing `Flow.sure_takeoff`'s room) is named too: none on this map. Platform 12 and 23 -> 17 by the two rope hops get their own lines, with how often the weights would have sent her that way (`coverage.drawn`: the planner's own search under 40 draws of the weights a plan).

The live telemetry so far was all planned on older graphs, and its routes don't say where they were planned from: each route's moves are put on today's of the same kind and platforms that start nearest (1,941 of them more than 3 units off, the takeoffs since placed for room), its start is the look before it, its misses are read from the navigator's log lines ("ended on ... instead of"), and a move cheaper on today's graph than the route chosen then reads **then** (not in that graph, or the weights). From now on the navigator records both (Control: A route, what it records).

What it found (2026-10-21; the harness's preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, and the 72 live files of 2026-10-19 to 21; the same harness runs as the branch's start, seed for seed: the tally only reads the telemetry the run captured anyway):
- **The graph:** 204 moves. 13 are the quickest way between no two places on the map: the 6 rope climb-downs, all 3 rope jumps (the hops 23 -> 17, 6 -> 16 by 798's rope, 3 -> 9 by 723's), and 4 that another move beats by 0.07-0.40 s. Flow chains every other move from where it starts (no takeoff fails `Flow.sure_takeoff`'s room); the 10 climb-downs and rope jumps the mover makes alone.
- **Used:** the tour planned 104 of the 204 and took 98 (478 moves, 11 missed, 42 a hit's); the hunt loop 51 and 45 (166, 3, 26). Live, 121 of today's moves were taken (1,412 moves, 88 missed: the jump 32 -> 29 26 times of 83, into the basin; 29 -> 28 8 of 73; 16 -> 14 7 of 56, onto 25; 19 -> 18 7 of 66, onto 24 and 26). 55 moves weren't taken in any of the three.
- **The simulator's hunts hardly leave the basin.** The hunt loop spends 87.5% of its time on 36 and takes up 36 as its ground 12 times in 10 runs, 4 twice, 2 and 17 once; over 20 seeds no move of a route got her onto 2, 7, 12, 20 or 21 (she was on 12 and 21 only where two runs start, `harness.START_AT`, and on 2 after jump throws from 4). Live, 17.4% of the time was on 36, and the loop's grounds were 4 (24 times), 36 (20), 17 (16) and 1 (6). The tour spends 51.5% on 36, and the escort's fights there eat its trips: 59 of its 246 trips timed out, 28 of them before she set off (no route planned: bursts on the way and fights in her way until the stop's 90 s ran out), so its sweep's far stops come late or never (over 30 seeds it got onto 7 on 2 and 35 on 3). Live, 8 of 573 trips timed out. Whether the game's basin is as busy as the simulator's (60% of its 8 spawns filled, 8 s respawns) was for the respawns' measurement: it isn't. In game the basin had a monster at 12% of its spawn points while in view, and none 45% of the time, against 27% and 7% in the simulator by the same report, and a platform she emptied was mostly still empty 12.56 s later (80%), where in the simulator about half had a monster back within 5 s (Respawns and drop life).
- **Never taken, by why:** the tour's 106: ground 72, penalty 18 (its spots' trips keep off the ground they shoot), cost 10, replanned 6; the loop's 159: ground 137, cost 13, replanned 6, weights 3. Most moves lose to where the trips go, not to their cost.
- **Platform 12** (2 pigs and an Orange Mushroom): the tour stopped at it 6 times of 70 (7 moves onto it, 1.1% of its time); the hunt loop never made it a ground, in the simulator or live (live, 2 visits and one move onto it in 3 days). Through 12 on the way elsewhere was slower for every trip the runs made: the tour's at best +3.1 s (+149%), none within 35%; the loop's +0.8 s (+15%), 3 of 133 plans within 35%; and the weights would have sent her through it on none. On today's graph the live trips' ends had a route through 12 within 35% of the one chosen on 205 of 684 plans, but the weights would send her through it on about 1 of them: each move's weight is drawn on its own, so over the long left side they average out (as found before: Found offline, Ropes in mid-air, route variety). So in the harness it's the ground choice: hardly a trip goes where a way through 12 is close (none of the tour's 219 plans, 3 of the loop's 133), and she goes onto 12 only to hunt 12 itself, which the hunt loop never does. Live it's the weights: a way through 12 was within reach on 205 plans, and the weights almost never draw it (Route diversity: why her routes were fixed).
- **23 -> 17 by two rope hops** (`rope_jump 23->17 @959`): in the graph since the ropes in mid-air (2026-10-21, 09:40), after the last live run (01:52), so live it wasn't there to take, nor the climb past 23's end. In the harness it was never planned: 4.49 s with its risk (0.5 s, a guess), against 3.15 for the climb past 23's end, +1.1 s (+29%) from where the quickest routes leave 23 (x 903), so it's the quickest way nowhere (cost). The weights make that up only when the climb's draw is 1.28 times the hops' or more: on 1.2% of the trips from 23 at x 903 (2.5% from x 866, by its left end; 0.2% from 959). Over the tour's 226 plans that could have made it, the weights would have sent her by the hops on 0.6 (the climb was taken 24 times; in the loop 0.0 and 3); the live trips' ends, on today's graph, 1.2 of 685. That it was never taken in these runs is what its cost predicts: to be taken it needs a lower cost or a diversity setting (Route diversity: `--ways 3`, which takes them on about 1 in 10 of the trips from there, from x 930 or less), or `nav 17 --avoid climb`.
- **The regression test** (`tests/test_coverage.py`): in the harness's own world it's the fights in the basin that decide which platforms she gets to, so the test runs in preset `game-sparse` (`game` with 15% of the spawns filled): the tour and the hunt loop on seeds 1-5, 10 minutes each, between them get her onto every platform monsters spawn on (16), each by 2 runs of the 10 or more (12 by 2, 35 by 3). Of the first 16 seeds, every 4 in a row do (13 of 13), every 3 in a row 13 of 14. 9.5 s, the runs in worker processes as the harness's.

To check in game: the first live run with the new records, by `coverage --live`: its routes should count as planned on today's graph (none "older"), its misses fall on the moves her dot shows (the verdicts the navigator's own now), and a chain hit on the way that still got her there count as made. The hops' own check stays as it was (Found offline: Ropes in mid-air).

## Respawns and drop life

`python -m gamelab respawn [FILES...] [--text FILE]` (`analysis/respawn.py`; offline, no keys) reads the live telemetry (`lab/telemetry`, but the simulator's files), or the files given, and measures three things, each time as a survival estimate over censored observations (Kaplan-Meier; its median's 95% interval Brookmeyer and Crowley's, the share left at 5 s to 180 s with its interval on the log-log scale; a time is given only where 5 or more were still followed):
- **Respawn:** from a kill (her 'kill' events, and the deaths the continuous feed saw, 'died': `watch`'s, hers and others', and from late on 2026-10-21 a hunt's with `--feed`, others' only) that empties its platform until a monster is seen there again. An episode starts only if the kill's monster was seen there in the 3 s before (a kill placed on the wrong monster would start one on a platform empty long since) and the looks after it, from 1.5 s on (a kill is seen dying for up to 1.26 s), see the platform empty twice, 0.5 s apart. A monster seen there then (or another kill there) ends it at the midpoint between that look and the last that saw the platform empty, if they're 2 s apart at most. A longer gap between the looks covering the platform, whether the next sees a monster or not (she was away: a monster may have come back and been killed by someone else meanwhile, and the telemetry has others' kills only from the feed), the end of the run or 180 s censors it at the last empty look. (The report first went on with an episode across a gap when the look after it saw the platform empty again, as if it had been empty all along, though the method said otherwise: 34 of the 77 live episodes spanned such a gap, of up to 111 s, and its "past a minute" rested on that unwatched time. Found in review on 2026-10-21.) One seen first with an HP bar is one she had hurt, hidden for a while under its damage numbers, and drops the episode. 'Lone' episodes had no other kill on the platform in the 15 s before: with a respawn per spawn point, an earlier kill's monster coming back first cuts the time short.
- **Drop life:** each drop, told apart by its kind and where it lies (within 8 units: from one look to the next within a second, 76% of the live drops seen again were within a unit of where they'd been, 89% within two), from the kill of hers it lies by (within 60 units and 20 s before it was first seen; else from when it was first seen, its age then unknown) until two looks 1.5 s apart that looked for drops where it lies, with her standing, her inventory not full and no other drop over it (drops drawn over one another are found as one), see it gone: an event if she wasn't by it since it was last seen (within 25 units, or her walk from one look to the next over it: the looter walks over a row of piles pressing pick-up with no look in between), else picked up (censored). It's followed only until the next kill by it (another kill's drops land where it lies, and one of its kind there would be taken for it), and what's then seen where it lay is neither it nor a new drop until the spot is seen empty. Other players take drops too, once they're no longer the killer's, and nothing in the telemetry tells that from a drop vanishing: the estimate is a lower bound of the game's drop life.
- **How full:** for each platform monsters spawn on, over the looks that covered it (each counted until the next look, 1 s at most), the monsters seen on it against its spawn points, and how much of the time none was. Detection misses a monster now and then, and her kills keep down the ones she hunts.

A look sees a platform empty only if it searched for monsters with her standing (from mid-jump the band and the screen are up to 77 units higher than her platform puts them) and all of the platform in the area searched: her band (140 above her feet to 75 below, a monster's body 60 tall held in it), or the whole screen above the bottom bar and clear of the minimap, less 40 units at the screen's sides. A monster seen on it in any look that searched with her standing is one there. **From 2026-10-21's evening each scene record says so itself:** whether the look searched for monsters (`searched`) and on the whole screen (`wide`), where it looked for drops (`looked`: platform, map x from, to), the map the screen showed above the bottom bar (`view`) and whether she stood (her `grounded`); `watch`'s records carry where its last search for drops looked. That's about 90 bytes a look (16-23% more per scene record in the simulator's runs; about 0.7 MB an hour of hunting on its 4.2). Before, the report infers them: a look searched if it saw a monster, if the hunt loop's 'monsters' record or a fight's 'stance' followed it, in a probe's run off a trip (a probe looks for minutes, every look searching), or in `watch`; it was wide if it saw a monster off her band or drops off her platform, in a probe that looks wide, or in `watch`; she stood if her name tag was seen on her platform's surface, not within 1.3 s of a fight's jump (in the live hunt loops, of the looks after a jump stance whose look before saw 2 drops or more on her platform, 104 of 130 saw none 0.9-1.1 s after it, 13 of 23 at 1.2 s: in the air); the hunt loop's own looks that saw a drop on her platform looked for drops all along it. The rest (the navigator's looks for her landing, the looter's, the rests') say nothing, and the screen is put by the camera's model (`pose.Camera`).

**The game's own data** has no respawn time for this map: its 48 spawn points (life entries: 9 snails, 12 slimes, 18 pigs, 9 Orange Mushrooms) all have `mobTime` 0, its info `mobRate` 1.0 and no `createMobInterval`. No map of the 306 in the client's data has `createMobInterval`; of the spawn points of the 196 with monsters, 4,794 have `mobTime` 0, 2,512 none, and 341 a time of 1 s to 3 hours (timed spawns). The respawn's timing is the server's.

**MeowDB's stated model** for MapleStory Classic (its pages of maps 010003111, The Murky Swamp II, and 010000012, this one, read 2026-10-21): one respawn tick every 7.56 s for every map the server has loaded; after a monster dies its spawn point waits a cooldown (5 s, or for timed spawns the map's `mobTime`) and the monster comes back on the next tick. A map holds at most 75% of its spawn points' monsters with one player there, 5% more for each other player, all of them from 6: here about 36 of 48 solo. The map's mob rate isn't applied to those figures, which it gives as its reading of the closed beta. By it a spawn point's monster is back 5-12.56 s after it dies (8.8 s on average), while the map is under its cap.

What it found (2026-10-21; the 72 live files of 2026-10-19 to 21: 14,869 looks placing her, 469 kills of hers, every look's coverage inferred; no other player's kill is in them, the one `watch` file being a minute long):
- **A platform she empties mostly stays empty while she watches it.** 77 episodes, each followed while the looks covering its platform came 2 s apart at most: 12 seen back, all within 10.8 s (2.5-10.8 s after the kill). Not back by 5 s: 90% (95% 81-95%, 55 still followed); by 8 s: 83% (71-90%, 39); by 12.56 s: 80% (67-88%, 23); by 30 s: 80% (11 followed); fewer than 5 were watched to 60 s, the longest 79 s. The 36 lone ones: 91% not back by 12.56 s (70-98%, 13 followed). The median isn't reached. How much that leans on the 2 s: censored only at gaps over 5 s, 83% weren't back by 12.56 s (34 followed) and 80% by 60 s (5); taking the unwatched time as empty, as the report first did, 83% by 12.56 s (46) and 81% by 60 s (15). So about four in five aren't back by 12.56 s, whichever way; that they stay empty past a minute only the unwatched time said. By platform: 4 (shot from 3) 6 of 20 back (2.5-10.8 s), followed to 70 s; the basin 3 of 14 (2.5-4.0 s), the rest followed only to 13.4 s, as she left; 3 (one snail; she stood on it, shooting 4) 1 of 15 (7.3 s), followed only to 12.9 s; 18 and 21 none of 7 each, followed to 79 and 71 s; 17 1 of 6; 20 1 of 4; 22 none of 3. Of the 469 kills, 350 didn't empty their platform, 26 had it out of view after, 9 had no monster of theirs seen there just before, 7 were a hurt one coming out from under its damage numbers. Whether she stood by the platform's spawn points can't say why: 69 episodes had her within 150 units of one at the kill (12 back), 8 didn't (none back, followed to 49 s).
- **The simulator refills within seconds** (the same report on 8 runs of preset `game`, the hunt loop and the tour on seeds 1-4, 10 minutes each, the records saying what each look searched): 22 episodes, 11 seen back at 3.0-8.8 s, the other 11 followed only 2.5-6.3 s (she left, or a gap in the looks): 52% not back by 5 s (95% 26-72%, 6 still followed), against the game's 90% (81-95%); on the basin 6 of 10 back at 3.0-4.2 s (an earlier kill's monster comes back first), on 31 (one snail's) 4 of 9 at 3.8-8.8 s, the sooner ones after a kill whose event came seconds after the death (a kill's event comes at the look that saw her EXP go up: in the simulator 0.53 s at the median, 1.8 s at the 90th percentile, and a quarter of its kill events had no death of their kind there in the 5 s before). Read as the older telemetry is (everything inferred), the same runs give the same picture (21 episodes, 12 seen back at 2.6-8.8 s, the rest followed only to 5.2 s; the median 3.8 s). `tests/test_respawn.py` has a pig alone in the basin back 7.6 s after its kill's event, 8 s after it died. (Before the gap rule: 14 of 22 back. The messages of commits 299e9e7 and 4906f4d have every one back by 8.8 s: 8 were followed only to 2.5-6.3 s.)
- **So neither the simulator's 8 s at the spawn point nor MeowDB's 5-12.56 s fits the game's platforms after she empties them**, as far as 12.56 s: by MeowDB's model every spawn point's monster would be back by then, while the map is under its cap. MeowDB's cap would fit: with the map at it, a respawn goes to some free spawn point of the map, not the one she freed (her platform's chance a few in the dozens free), and other players' kills elsewhere bring monsters to platforms nobody hunts. That isn't settled, and how full the platforms were (below) would put the cap well under 75%.
- **How full:** 26% of the spawn points of the platforms in view had a monster, over 4,523 s of platforms covered (her kills included). The basin 12% (0.99 monsters of 8, none 45% of the time; the simulator's, by the same report, 27% and 7%), 12 17% (0.52 of 3, none 72%: a run of the probe top-60 watched it from 13 for 5 minutes and never had one in range), 4 22%, 17 19%, 18 28%, 21 35%, 2 40%; the one-snail platforms 20 93%, 33 72%, 22 64%, 31 45%, and 3 8% (she stood on it). The simulator's preset `game` fills 60% of the spawn points, and its monsters come back where they were.
- **Drop life isn't settled: longer than a minute.** 337 drops followed (235 hers, 102 found), 10 seen gone without her by them (hers at 4-10 s, found at 0-77 s); 141 lost from view, 139 followed only until a kill by them, 47 picked up. Still lying at 60 s: 95% (89-97%, 7 followed that long), and none was followed past 88 s: she picks hers up within seconds, and where she hunts the next kill lands by the rest. The early ones gone may be other players' takes, or the detector's. In the simulator, where every drop lies 180 s, the same report saw 21 of 481 drops gone, at 2-187 s, and the basin's kills cut most short (316): a hunt's telemetry can't measure it.
- **The constants stay:** `hunt.RESPAWN` and `world.RESPAWN` 8 s, `hunt.DROP_LIFE` 90 s (`looting.DROPS_LAST`) and `world.DROP_LIFE` 180 s, their comments saying what was measured. RESPAWN's value isn't measured (the median isn't reached) and neither is the game's way (a delay, or a cap): another value would be another guess, and in the simulator, whose monsters do come back in 8 s, any shorter wait would only lose. It decides a fair share of the hunt loop: in the live hunt loops (4,135 s), rule 8 (lull loot, a kill due back) held 6.2% of the time and rule 9 (wait) 5.3%, and 20 of their 43 departures from a ground were "nothing left here, and nothing due back", RESPAWN + 2 s and the 3 s to confirm after her last kill there. The drop life is over a minute, as both values are.

To check in game (the report runs on any telemetry): the next hunt loop or tour with `--feed` (`tour --loop --minutes 20 --feed`), by `respawn` on its file, now with every look saying what it searched and the feed's deaths of others' kills recorded ('died'): whether a platform she empties, watched without a gap, stays empty past 12.56 s as here (80% not back), or has a monster back within it (MeowDB's respawn at a spawn point); and how many of the platforms she empties someone else empties first (others' kills on them). If it stays empty, RESPAWN's wait (rules 8 and 9, and the fast exit's "due back") isn't worth its time in game, and the simulator needs the game's refill (MeowDB's cap, say) before the harness can weigh a shorter one. A hunt watches a platform only while she's near it, so for longer: `watch --minutes 20` (it sends no keys) while she, played by hand, clears a platform in full view (12 from 13, or 21 from 22) and stands still 3 minutes, twice, and `respawn` on its file: the feed records every death ('died', hers and others'), so each clearing is an episode watched throughout, and the other platforms in view say whether monsters come back elsewhere meanwhile (a cap). In the same watch, one kill's drops left lying on a quiet platform in view (a snail's on 22 or 20) and not picked up for 4 minutes: when they go is the drop life, which no hunt has left a drop long enough to show.

## Route diversity

With `--ways N` (`nav`, `tour`, `tour --loop`, and the harness's `run` and `coverage`; `route.ROUTES`, 1 by default) each trip chooses among the N best distinct ways to where it's going, not only the quickest. It still weighs every move's cost by a random 1 to 1.35 (`route.NOISE`, kept through its replans), so near-equal variants take turns (the basin's two ropes, a drop or a walk-off, which takeoff). Then, at its first plan:
- **The ways** (`Planner.alternatives`): the quickest, then the best route through each other move (to where it starts, the move, and on from where it lands: one search out from her and one back from where she's going, as road maps find alternatives through a via node), cheapest first, none more than 35% dearer than the quickest (`route.STRETCH`), none onto a platform twice, and none through a move of the quickest (with no random weights a tie gave one, from 0 at x 1290 to 18 through walk_off 0->1 at no cost more, and `through` that move gave back the quickest itself: 7 of 978 ways offered on random trips, none with the weights). A way is the platforms it goes through and the rope each move takes: another takeoff, or a jump for a walk-off between the same two platforms, is the same way, which the weights vary. From 23 by its right end (x 903) to 17 the planner offers the climb past the end at 1040's rope (3.79 s) and the two rope hops off 959's (4.87 s, +29%); from 19 to 3, the way by 18, 16, 14 and 6 (13.0 s) and the one up the map's left side through 12 (16.0 s, +23%).
- **The choice** (`route.chances`, `choose`): a softmax over their times, a way dearer than the quickest by 10% of its time weighing 1/e of it (`route.TEMPERATURE`). By their times alone the hops are taken 5.5% of the time from x 903, and 10% with the weights drawn as well; the way through 12 from 19, 9%. A way through a move she hasn't set off on for 2 minutes (`route.STALE_S`; the navigator's memory, since it began), one the quickest doesn't make, has a chance of 20% at least (`route.FLOOR`; such ways get half the chances at most between them).
- **Kept to:** replanned, the trip goes the way it chose (`Planner.through` its move) until she has set off on that move, unless from where she is that costs more than the quickest by more than any way offered could (35% of the quickest's time when it chose: a share of what's left dropped the hops at the end of a trip from 35 in the first runs), she is past the move (on a platform the way reaches from it on: she got there another way), or the way from there would take her back (onto a platform twice, or onto one she has set off from since it chose that the quickest from there doesn't go onto: knocked back below one, the quickest goes over it again too). Without those two (found in review on 2026-10-21), a trip from 34 to 2 that chose the hops and got onto 17 by the climb past 23's end instead was sent from 17 down to 23 to hop back up, and from 14 walked back off onto 17 for it; replanned at the landings of the quickest route on random trips that chose another way, 186 of 1,240 replans that kept the way went back onto a platform she had been on, none of 889 now. In the harness neither changed a run (the 80 runs with 3 ways below): only 29 replans there kept a way chosen, none past its move or going back; they're for the misses and knocks of the game. The hunt loop's objectives keep their trips' choice, as they keep their weights. A chain the next one is planned on the move from counts its moves as made: before that, from 9, having walked off 5 on the way through 12 from 19 to 3, she jumped back onto 5 to walk off it again (14.2 s flown, 17.1 with the loop).
- **Not in a hurry:** a trip with a deadline goes the quickest way (`go(vary=False)`): the tour's trips for drops and to rest, the hunt loop's for piles.
- The route record of a trip's first plan has the ways it chose among, each way's move, cost and chance, and which it took (`ways`, `chose`); the log says which when it isn't the quickest ("of 3 ways, the one through drop 12->21 (+5%, chance 27%)").
- Computing (`sim.latency`, the tour on 5 seeds of 10 minutes): before a key plan the 99th percentile was 1.98 ms with 3 ways and 1.99 with one, at most 10.5 ms and 3.6 (a trip's first plan, the two searches: 6-8 ms on this map; a replan while its move is still ahead, 2-3 ms).

**Why her routes were fixed.** What step 1's coverage report left to settle, looking for a modelling error first:
- *Not a move's cost.* The hops cost about what the simulator flies them in: from 23 at x 900, 4.89 s with their risk (0.5 s, a guess) against 5.05 flown, and the climb past 23's end 3.81 against 3.93, 1.1 s apart both ways (with no monsters and no noise, the hops chosen by hand). Through 12 the planner is, if anything, on 12's side: 19 -> 3 by 18 and 16 is 13.0 s by the planner and 10.5 flown, up through 12 16.0 and 14.2.
- *Not a move left out by its margins:* every move flow can chain passes `Flow.sure_takeoff` from where it starts (Route coverage).
- *Not the rate rule:* 12 is a poor ground by the spawn data (12.3 EXP a visit; the basin 30, 17 19.5) and by what visits gave live (13.7 EXP a visit over 9; the basin 59.5 over 31, 4 61.0 over 21). Live it was among the hunting grounds (its EXP a second 19% of the basin's, where the cut is 15%) and lost on its value.
- *The weights:* the navigator's comment had each trip's weights let routes within about 35% of the quickest take turns. But a route's draws average out (the mean of n has a spread of 0.35 / sqrt(12 n)), so ways 12% or more apart never swapped, and on today's graph the weights would have sent the 342 live trips of 2026-10-19 to 21 (their first plans) through 12 on 1.1, where 71 had a way through it within reach, and by the hops on 0.3 of 29. Those, and the hunt loop's grounds (which follow their value), kept her ways fixed; the choice among whole ways is for the first.

**What the harness says** (preset `game`, 40 seeds of 20 minutes, each setting paired seed by seed against 1 way; EXP a minute as the paired change and its standard error; seeds 1-10 the bar's):

Of these 480 runs with 2 or 3 ways, the fixes to replans (Kept to, above) changed one: the loop with the noise at 0.1, seed 5, 118.2 -> 129.2 EXP a minute, where one replan would have gone back; its row was +0.4% ± 0.9%, drops 81.1%.)

Seed by seed on the bar's seeds, 1 way → `--ways 3` (`harness compare`: the tour better on 6 of 10, the worst -26%, drops 96.4% -> 93.5%: does not pass; the hunt loop better on 2 of 10, the worst -40%, four the same: does not pass):
