The single seeds swing far more than the means: the tour's seed 7 lost 26% (77.45 -> 57.3) where its seed 5 gained 19%, and seed 8 picked up 72% of its drops for 98%; the loop's seed 7 lost 40% (106.8 -> 63.8: the run diverging, not a chosen way kept to, as the loop's 40 runs have 10 replans keeping one between them) and its seed 6 picked up 66% for 97%. Its seeds 1, 2, 4 and 10 had no choice of way and came out the same.

- **EXP a minute is the same** on every setting, within 2% either way (standard errors 0.9-1.7%). **Fewer of her drops are picked up** on every one, by 0.6-3.3 points (each 0.3-2 standard errors; with `--ways 3` 1.5 ± 1.2 in the tour and 2.2 ± 1.9 in the loop, and their worth a minute down too). Not the trips for drops: before those went the quickest way, 3 ways cost the tour 1.0 point and the loop 2.5. HP lost is the same or a little lower (0.03-0.14 bars).
- **Her moves widen in the tour:** 5 more distinct moves over the 40 runs and 2.2 more a run, 0.5 more spawn platforms a run, 5 points less of her time on the basin, half again as many moves onto 12, and the hops 4 times for 1. Of the tour's 183 trips that chose (the trips to its stops: those for drops and to rest go the quickest way), 104 had one way within 35%, 79 two or three, and 28 took another than the quickest; the hops were offered on 14, chosen on 3 and made all 3. The hunt loop hardly leaves the basin (31 of 40 runs hunt it alone, where the simulator's monsters come back in 8 s: Respawns and drop life), so 11 of its 40 seeds had no choice of way at all and came out the same as with one way: 79 trips chose, 25 took another way, and of 3 that chose the hops 2 ended in position (a rule called for her) before 23.
- **None passes the bar** on seeds 1-10: in the tour EXP is better on 5-7 of 10 seeds, the worst 16-26% down, and fewer of her drops picked up with each setting; in the hunt loop 2-4 of 10, as 4 of the 10 come out the same (no choice of way), and so can't pass with any route setting. So `ROUTES` stays 1: diversity is a setting to turn on (`--ways 3`), with the defaults the harness had for it (temperature 0.1, the floor 0.2 after 2 minutes, the noise 0.35). (Before the fix to chains followed on, and with the trips for drops choosing too, 3 ways with no floor gave the tour -1.6% ± 1.3% and the loop +1.5% ± 1.4%: that much the noise moves it.)

**The coverage report with `--ways 3`** (`coverage RUNS --live --ways 3`, the 40-seed runs above against 1 way's, and the live plans as they would have gone):
- Tour: moves taken 127 -> 132 of 204, never taken 77 -> 72; platform 12: 45 -> 67 visits, 1.6% -> 2.1% of her time, 48 -> 71 moves onto it (29 -> 27 stops at it); through it on the way elsewhere at best 0.04 s quicker than the route chosen (13 x 70 -> 4), within 35% on 15 of 1,063 plans, and the choice would send her through it on 2.5 (the weights 0.9). The hops: planned 5 -> 16, taken 1 -> 4, the climb 83 -> 67.
- Hunt loop: moves taken 84 -> 96, never taken 120 -> 108, spawn platforms reached 15 -> 16 of 16 (7 too); 12 hardly (7 -> 5 visits, 2 moves onto it both); the hops planned 0 -> 12, taken 0 -> 2.
- Live (the 685 plans of 2026-10-19 to 21 on today's graph, each counted as a choice, as the weights' were in Route coverage): through 12 on 13.4 for 1.2, by the hops 25.7 for 1.2. Counting each trip's first plan only (its choice; replans keep it): through 12 on 5.2 of 342 trips for 1.1, by the hops 6.3 for 0.3, before the floor (by which the hops, stale until she has made them, are taken 20% of the time they're offered).

**Ties between grounds** (`hunt.GROUND_TIE`, 0 by default): of the grounds whose score (EXP a second, travel included, varied by 0.75-1.25) is within that share of the best, the one she left longest ago, or never got to, is next. At 0.25 the harness's hunt loops (40 seeds) were the same to the key as with 0, and at 0.1 with 3 ways as 3 ways alone: 31 of the 40 runs take up one ground, the basin, for the whole run. (Grounds she never got to all rank first and equal, so the best of them goes, and in the harness she seldom gets past the basin.) Live, by the value map, 17 and 1 were within 3% of each other from the basin and from 3 (4 ahead of both), and her grounds already varied (4 24 times, 36 20, 17 16, 1 6). Nothing measures it: off, and `tour --loop --ground-tie 0.1` turns it on for a run.

To check in game (none of this has been played):
- `tour --loop --minutes 20 --ways 3`, then `coverage --live`: the route records' `chose` (the first plan of each trip that chooses) show the choice working and the ways it takes, and whether those trips take about what the planner gives them (dearer by their margin: through 12 from 19, 3 s); and no route replanned after a miss or a knock should go back onto a platform she has left (the route records' `from` against the moves before). One run can't measure the rates: in the simulator a 20-minute run has 4-5 trips that choose in the tour and 2 in the loop (15% and 32% of them took another way than the quickest); live, at the 342 trips of 105 minutes of 2026-10-19 to 21, a 20-minute run would go through 12 on about 1 trip and by the hops on about 1. Several runs, against as many without `--ways`, would show whether she picks up as many of her drops (in the simulator 1.5-2.2 points fewer).
- The hops: whether she makes them is `nav 17 --avoid climb` from 23 (Found offline, Ropes in mid-air). How often `--ways 3` takes them: `nav 17 --ways 3` from 23 at x 930 or less, each a fresh navigator, so no floor: about 10% of the time (by the planner's chances over 2,000 draws of the weights: 11% from x 870, 10% from 903, 9% from 930, 8% from 950, 3% from 981, where with no weights there's only the one way), so about 20 tries to see them twice (none in 20: 12%).
- `tour --loop --minutes 20 --ground-tie 0.1` against a run without it: whether she alternates between 17 and 1 (within 3% of each other from the basin and from 3) rather than taking the same one each time.
- If the game agrees, `route.ROUTES` 3 could be the default.

## Perception

| Piece | Does |
|---|---|
| `mobs.py` | Finds snails, slimes, pigs and Orange Mushrooms, and any other kind with a colour gate learned for its map (below). It matches each monster's body-colour silhouette first, then checks the best spots with masked colour templates. The matches run on 6 threads, OpenCV letting go of Python's lock while it matches; which spots get checked doesn't depend on what the checks find, so they're all picked first, and the answers are the same as on one thread, in the same order. 21.6 ms for the fight's band around her and 76 ms for the whole screen (medians on the 17 bench frames), against 92 and 350 on one thread. What step 1 does after each match (windows with too little colour out, the peaks picked) is done in C (`gamelab/native.c`, called through ctypes, which lets go of Python's lock for it): in numpy it held the lock, so the threads took turns at it, 18 of the band's 34 ms (33.5 and 120 ms before it, timed back to back). The C does numpy's arithmetic in numpy's order, so the answers are the same to the bit (on all 31 saved 1920×1080 screenshots, and tests compare them on made-up ones); it's built with MSVC 2022's Build Tools the first time it's needed (`python -m gamelab.native` builds it now; see Motion planning), and without them the search runs in numpy as before (`bench-look` says which). More than 6 threads still don't help: the colour tests before the matches are numpy's. The first pass ignores windows with almost none of the monster's colour (summed from one integral image per kind): their normalised score is noise that can reach a perfect 1.0. The frame is also cut to an even size so the half-size mask is an exact shrink. An odd size (the fight's band was 151 rows) smeared faint colour into blank windows. In the basin, those fake matches took all 12 checks per monster type, so the fight never saw the snails and pigs at her level while a whole-screen look did. That's likely also why one pass over the whole screen once missed three slimes in plain view; whole-screen looks still go band by band. A monster found says which template matched it, and so what that template knows (`sprites.py`): where its feet are, which way it faces (the game's sprites face left; flipped, right) and its pose (standing, walking, flinching). It's placed where its feet are, not at its picture's middle and bottom: a sprite's origin is up to 8.5 units off its picture's middle, a slime's hop frames end 10-17 units above its feet, and the cut-outs were cut off anywhere from 9.5 units above the feet to 16 below. On 15 saved frames where she stands (`bodies`), monsters' feet came out -1.4 to +2.5 units from the surface they stand on (18 of them; sprites +1.4 on average), against -8.5 to +16.4 by the picture's bottom, and +7.7 for a pig on platform 26 480 units above her, whose hop frame matched best though its picture's bottom sat on the surface (the walking frames scored 0.83-0.88 against 0.91); on two recordings with the camera still, a snail's x strayed from a straight line by 1.5 and 1.6 units (rms; at most 4.8 and 5.2) against 2.4 and 3.5 (at most 13.4 and 9.8), when it jumped between a sprite and an off-centre cut-out. The way it faced was the way it moved in 192 of the 199 stretches of 0.6 s where it walked (the misses at turns). For the continuous feed a monster seen before is re-found 16 px around where it should be (`refind`): with the template that matched it last, 0.69 ms, else with its kind's templates in that window, 3.4 ms; on the 17 saved frames 84 of 84 were re-found where they were, and 84 of 84 moved 8 px. At the game's own 1366x768 the search is coarse to fine: step 1 at this scale, step 2 at full size around each place with several frames a place (see Screen size). |
| `sprites.py` | Templates for the monsters on the current map, made from the game's own sprites (every stand, move and hit frame) at their on-screen size, with what the game data says of each frame (`index.json`: its origin, the point that stands on the monster's feet, its body's box, its head, how long it shows). They're added to the screenshot cut-outs in `assets/mobs/`, which still catch monsters half hidden in plants. Each cut-out is matched once against its kind's sprites, both ways (`cutouts.json`): all 11 matched a frame at 0.86-0.98 (the other way round 0.29-0.52), which gives where their monster's feet are and which way it faces; under 0.85 a cut-out would stand on its middle and bottom instead. The four pig cut-outs are all the hop frame cut off 9.5 units above the feet, and three slime cut-outs reach 14-16 units below them (grass). Built on first use and kept with the other extracted data (git-ignored). At the game's own 1366x768 the templates are the game's pictures as they are, alpha and all (`sprites_x1`), and the cut-outs aren't used (see Screen size). |
| `loot.py`, `drops.py` | Finds drops on the ground: what the map's monsters drop (on the first map Squishy Liquid, Slime Bubble, Leather, Pig's Head, Pig's Ribbon, Orange Mushroom Cap, Snail Shell; another map's from `drops.py`, below), the potions monsters this level drop (Red, Orange, White and Blue Potion), and bronze and gold mesos (3 spinning frames each; a coin found says which it is, `Item.coin`: their amount picks it). Templates are the game's own item pictures at their on-screen size, scaled by their opaque pixels only. A spot counts when the masked template matches (0.6) and its colours differ from the template's by at most 24 on average, checked where the picture covers whole pixels. The sea and island backdrop matched the Blue Potion's shape at 0.60-0.63, and UI buttons the White Potion's; they differed by 27-57, real drops by 12-19. Only a strip just above each platform is searched (drops lie on the ground), on 8 threads (3 since 2026-10-26: CPU), every strip's templates at once: 6.0 ms for her platform, 54 for every platform in view (medians on the 30 saved screenshots that place her, with and without mesos only), against 8.5 and 89 strip by strip on 4 threads, with the same drops in all 120 searches. On 110 saved frames it found all 18 drops and nothing else. Drops lay on the ground about 2 s after her EXP went up, and monsters are drawn in front of them. At the game's own 1366x768 the templates are the game's pictures as they are, and the shapes are matched on brightness alone, the colours checked as here (see Screen size). |
| `nametag.py` | Her name tag, matched at full resolution (at half size the score swings between ~0.6 and ~0.9). Of the good matches in the play area, the one nearest where she was wins: her name also shows in the minimap, the bottom bar and the party list. A look searches within 240 px across and 120 down of where she was found last first: 3.4 ms, against 37 for the whole play area (`bench-look`, 17 saved frames, the same answers on all of them). The whole play area is searched when she isn't there (a warp, the tag hidden), and every 30th look anyway, so a wrong match can't stick. Since 2026-10-26 (CPU) a look searches within 80 px across and 40 down first (1.6 ms; then the 240 and 120). |
| `exp_number.py` | Her EXP number read, glyph by glyph, by drawing the game's own glyphs as the game draws them and keeping a reading only when every cell is clearly its glyph and the percent fits her level's requirement (see Her EXP number): all 32 saved frames and 19 saved crops of the bar read right, 8.6 ms a reading, only when the number changes. |
| `hud.py` | HP and MP from the bar colours; her EXP number, which only changes when she gains EXP (read only then); a monster's HP bar (its frame matched above the monster, its green rows read: Monster HP bars, below; 0.5 ms at 1366x768, 0.8 at half size of 1920x1080); the purple MISS or damage numbers when a monster touches her. |
| `minimap.py`, `terrain.py` | Her yellow minimap dot, and other players' (see Other players); the ground under her; ropes and ladders on screen. Ropes are looked for only where the map's ropes can show: Senses hands perception the columns, 52 px either side of where her dot puts each (`ROPE_PAD`: all a rope can be off and still place her). 4.2 ms a look, against 31.4 for the whole screen with the rope colour test in numpy as before (in OpenCV now: the same pixels, checked on every colour; the whole screen 19) and 8.4 before each column was copied out of the screen for its colour test (OpenCV split a column viewed in place 8 times slower) and the shapes were made only in the box around the rope colour's pixels (58% of a column) and connected only around what was left of them (9%): the same ropes on all 31 saved 1920×1080 screenshots, over the whole screen and in random columns, and on 3,000 made-up strips. On 17 saved frames the ropes seen were 0-20 px from where her dot put them, and the same as on the whole screen. |
| `lie_detector.py` | The Lie Detector's six forms (see above). A check of the screen takes 64.5 ms (the player's, 4 times a second, about 3 until 2026-10-24: The Lie Detector's recordings; 73.6 before its capture was turned into a picture of its own and the title's yellow tested in OpenCV, with the same scores). Most of it is matching the title's letters (about 35 ms) and the two half-size forms (about 19). The notice and the quiz's two forms (2026-10-25) add about 9 ms each, whatever a template's size: on a 1366x768 frame with the PC busy, 99-103 ms a check against 75-78 without them, about 0.4 of a core at 4 a second. Since 2026-10-26 the watchdog's looks match each form only around where it has always shown, 7 looks in 8, on a frame of the game's own size with its picture where it belongs (`near`, 11 ms; CPU). |

**Monster HP bars** (`hud.hp_bar`, from 2026-10-25). The bar a monster shows once it's hurt, as the game draws it at
1366x768 (game px; the saved frames and the rolling recorder's clips of 2026-10-24 and 25): a white frame 50 x 8 between
two black ones, and inside the fill, 46 x 4: three rows green (0, 255, 0) for the HP left over a red one (170, 0, 0),
then the latest damage, red fading to dark red, and the HP lost black; its frame's top-left 25 left of the monster's
centre and 29 above its picture's top on 567 of the 575 bars paired with a mushroom the detector found within 6 px (25
above on 6 Horny Mushrooms, 33 once, 26 left once; 3 more bars of the clips sat 10-13 px right of the nearest Horny
Mushroom found, perhaps over one it missed, and read only for the search's 14 px either side; at 1920x1080 the pigs'
25-30 game px above, the slimes', whose pictures change height as they bounce, 0-30). A party member's bar over a player
is the same filled red, and a monster's as it dies all red and black.
- **Why every bar read empty:** the reader took the longest run of pure green, black and dark red in a row above the
  monster for the fill. At 1366x768 the black frame outside the white one is 52 px of pure black, longer than the
  fill's 48: of 567 labelled bars 488 read 0 and 64 none (the run read was the black frame on 375 of the 503 read, the
  black rows inside on 53, the red row under the green on 49), and the scenery's 1-px black outlines read as empty bars
  over 267 of 2207 monsters with none. At half size of 1920x1080 the black rows blend into the white or not by where the
  bar falls in a look's px: 111 of 162 bars of the first map's recordings read 0 (and live on 2026-10-21 a third of the
  bars read 0). The simulator drew a bar of its own, grey around green and black, which read right.
- **Now** the bar is found by its frame: the frame's least colour (white high; the black frames and the fill, green, red
  or black, all low) matched against the bar's (`TM_CCOEFF_NORMED`, 0.75: at 1366x768 0.75-1.0 on the 805 bars found
  in the frames, 3 of them under 0.78, and 0.73 at most around a monster with none, a margin of only 0.02; at half size
  of 1920x1080 0.86 and up on the bars, 0.62 at most around none), drawn at the look's scale for each place a game px
  can fall in a px (quarters across, halves down: `_bar_frames`), around where it's expected from the monster (14 game
  px either side, 12 above, 31 below). Of the places matching about as well as the best (0.1), the nearest is read (the
  frame slid along its own long rows matches less well: a slime's slid 7 half-size px matched 0.81 against 0.99,
  nearer, and read 0.97 for 0.74). The HP
  left is green from the left to the last green column (green over red and blue by 60, blue under half of it: a vine's
  mint green over a bar isn't, a glow's yellow tint is), whatever covers the rest of its rows (a damage number over the
  frame's top, sparkles, the red of the latest damage), its blended edge at half size counted by how green it is. If
  what's after the green isn't red or black (little green and blue in all four rows) something covers the end, and
  it's read halfway along that to the first red or black after it (on the clips 0.45, 0.73 and 0.47, where the next
  frame without the cover read 0.46, 0.72 and 0.50). No green: None.
- **Measured** on the labelled bars (`tests/fixtures/hp_bars`, a bar over each monster the detector found, its fill
  counted by pixel): at 1366x768 564 of 565 with the end in view within half a px (0.001 off on average; before 0.540,
  487 read 0, 63 none), 1 of 1 with the end covered within the range it's in, the 408 monsters without one of their own
  none (before 47 read empty); at half size of 1920x1080 161 of 162 within a px (0.003; before 0.518, 111 read 0, 15
  none), 16 of 16 with the end covered, none of the 131 monsters without one (before 3). Two labels were put right on
  review: a full bar whose end a vine covers (its mint green counted as green) is 38 of 46 px green, the HP lost's black
  after it, as in the frames either side; a pig's bar under a damage number's purple halo, labelled with no green, is
  0.675 green. 0.5 ms a monster at 1366x768, 0.8 ms at half size (0.2 before).
- **In the simulator** the bars are drawn as the game draws them (`hud.draw_hp_bar`, at the half-size look's blend), and
  `--world bars-empty` (`Realism.bars_empty`) reads every bar up as empty, as live did before.

**Monsters with no hand-made colour test: learned gates** (from 2026-10-24; `perception/gates.py`, `mobs.py`,
`sprites.learn_background`, `learn-colours`). Step 1 looks for a kind only where its body's colour is, and the first
map's four kinds have that test written by hand (`mobs.PALETTES`). The next maps' monsters weren't looked for at all,
and by hand their tests would be hard to write: West Rocky Mountain II's stumps are browns like its rock, and Tree
Dungeon's mushrooms stand in a green forest, a Green Mushroom's cap pale green and a Horny Mushroom's grey-blue over the
same beige stem. Now a kind without one gets a gate learned from data:
- **Its colours** are its own pictures' pixels as step 1 sees them (the game's sprites shrunk to step 1's scale, as the
  detector makes them, at either window size), as drawn and blended toward the fog's white (`gates.FOG_ALPHAS`, 0.7 and
  0.4: West Rocky Mountain II's drifting fog; at 1920x1080 half size on 2026-10-23, red snails under it went from 48 to
  53 of 57 found with the blends). Tree Dungeon has no fog, and they cost nothing there: without them its gates took
  half as much of its background (0.76% for the two kinds together, against 1.63%) and a Horny Mushroom of the 17 in
  plain view was missed.
- **The background's** are the play area of saved frames of the map (above the HUD) at step 1's scale, with the
  monsters on them cut out (`sprites.learn_background`): they're found with gates learned against the frames as they
  are, monsters and all, then again with gates learned without the ones found, each one's picture cut out 6 game px
  around (`CUT_PAD`, `CUT_ROUNDS`).
- A colour, binned 5 bits a channel (32,768 colours), is the kind's where its share of the kind's pixels (as drawn or
  any blend) is 5 times its share of the background's or more (`gates.RATIO`). On West Rocky Mountain II's labelled
  frames (below) ratios of 3, 5, 8 and 12 found 55, 56, 56 and 57 of the 58 in plain view at 1366x768, but 164, 167, 164
  and 137 of the 203 on its 1920x1080 frames shrunk to that size; each stump's gate took about 5.5%, 4.2%, 3.2% and 2.2%
  of the background.
- `python -m gamelab learn-colours --map ID FRAMES` (saved frames of the map at any window size, or folders of them)
  keeps a map's background counts in `assets/backgrounds/<map id>.npz` (in git: 23 and 28 KB; the frames stay out of
  it), and the map's detector learns its gates from them as it's made (`sprites.detector_for`); with none kept for a
  map, its kinds without a hand-made test aren't looked for, as before. So the next map onboards the same way: its
  sprites are built from the game data on first use, a few frames of it are saved, and `learn-colours` is run once.
  Tree Dungeon's came from its 20 frames (53 monsters cut out; its gates take 0.98% and 1.22% of its background),
  West Rocky Mountain II's from its 133 frames at 1920x1080 and 31 at 1366x768 (667 cut out; each stump's gate takes
  3.8-4.4%, the Red Snail's 0.4%).

Everything below goes for the learned kinds only: the first map's four, their tests, templates and searches are as they
were, and the 86 golden traces are byte-identical.
- **One lookup a pixel:** each colour bin's byte has a bit for each learned kind (`gates.Gates`), so step 1 bins each
  pixel once and looks it up once for all of them (1.5-1.8 ms a band at 1366x768, on one thread).
- **The whole screen's bands overlap by the tallest picture** and step 2's slack (an Axe Stump's flinch is 93 game px
  tall, a band's overlap 43 at 1366x768), so one standing across a band's edge is whole in the next band. The bands
  are every kind's, so on a map with learned kinds the first map's kinds go by them too (on the first map, with none,
  they're as they were): on Tree Dungeon they overlap by the mushrooms' 56 rows and 9, and of 927 slimes drawn standing
  on its footholds (any frame of theirs, both ways, on its 19 frames from 28) the whole screen's search found 923, all
  117 on platform 30, whose surface lies on a band's edge (59 of 82 there with the bands as they were, in the map's
  study); overlapping by the slime's hop frame and the slack (93 rows), 925.
- **The fine gate tries a place's first two templates** (`mobs.GATE_TRIES`), not only its first. A stump's frames'
  silhouettes match a blob of its colour about alike, its flinch frame's (the largest) most often best, and gated with
  it a place failed where its own frame, the place's second or third, would have passed: on West Rocky Mountain II's
  1366x768 frames the places of Stumps and Axe Stumps in plain view scored 0.29-0.46 with the flinch frame and 0.67-0.77
  with their own (then 0.94-1.00 at full size). Gated with the first alone, 53 of the 58 in plain view were found (143
  of the 203 on the 1920x1080 frames shrunk), in 91 ms a whole screen; with the first two, 56 (167), in 106.
- **Kinds of one shape are searched as one** (`MobDetector.units`, `SAME_SHAPE` 0.9 of the larger picture's pixels in
  common, set on their feet): Horny and Green Mushroom are one sprite with two caps (95%), Stump and Dark Stump one in
  two colours (100%). They're searched by their gates together, with one silhouette a frame for both kinds (half step
  1's matches for the mushrooms), one set of places, and at each place step 2 checks each kind's picture side by side,
  the better kept. Searched apart, each kind made a place at the same monster and gated and checked it there, and a
  look where only one kind's gate fired there could have named a Green Mushroom a Horny one: the other kind's picture
  scores 0.82-0.92 on a mushroom (median 0.895, on the labels), where `THRESHOLD` is 0.6. Together, Tree Dungeon's whole
  screen took 65 ms against 81 apart (2026-10-24, alternating), finding the same; West Rocky Mountain II's 106 against
  110, finding 56 of the 58 in plain view against 57 (and 167 of the 203 on the frames shrunk, against 170).
- **An Axe Stump by its axe:** a Stump's picture matches the stump that is all of an Axe Stump but its axe about as well
  as the Axe Stump's own picture does (0.94 and 0.94 on most), so by the score alone 10 of the 40 Axe Stumps in plain
  view were taken for Stumps. A monster found as a kind that shares its body with one searched apart (`twins`: 80% of
  the larger picture's pixels in common; an Axe Stump and a Stump 85%, a Red Snail inside a Stump 32-38%) is checked as
  those at its feet (`mobs._pick_kind`): a picture with parts of its own (10% of its pixels outside the others', an Axe
  Stump's axe 15%) is the monster's only where those parts alone match 0.6 (`OWN_MATCH`), and then over the pictures it
  holds; otherwise the best score. On the 1920x1080 frames (shrunk, and at half size) an Axe Stump's axe matched
  0.83-0.97 on 39 of the 40 in plain view (one at 0.25), its place on a Stump or a Dark Stump -0.6 to 0.70 (one at 0.70,
  the next 0.52).
- **A track is re-found as its own kind only** (`MobDetector.refind`, `_its_kind`; at the game's own size). A monster
  followed is re-found with the picture that matched it last, and a kind's picture fits a monster of a kind that shares
  its body nearly as well. At each labelled mushroom and stump of the 1366x768 frames whose frame the labels give, the
  other kinds' pictures of it, re-found there, scored over `THRESHOLD` on all but 4: a Horny Mushroom's 0.79-0.90 on the
  Greens (median 0.895), a Green's 0.72-0.92 on the Hornies (0.912), a Dark Stump's 0.84-0.98 on the Stumps and Axe
  Stumps (0.979), a Stump's 0.86-1.00 on the Axe Stumps (1.00: its picture is all of theirs but the axe) and an Axe
  Stump's 0.66-0.89 on the Stumps (0.807). So the other kind's track re-found 52 of the 55 mushrooms and 115 of the 116
  re-finds asked of the stumps: a Horny's track would have gone on on a Green walking past or standing where it died.
  Now a monster re-found as such a kind is checked side by side with the other kinds' pictures of its frame, set on its
  feet, as a search tells them (`_pick_kind`), and isn't re-found where another kind's is the monster's: 0 of the 55
  and 0 of the 116, its own kind's track re-finding 53 of the 55 and 58 of the 58 as before (the two not re-found, a
  Horny standing on the chat box's border, its feet on row 700). The other kinds' pictures are matched where they lie
  on the monster's, not searched around, in numpy (`mobs._score`: OpenCV's masked score to 1e-9, 0.1 ms a picture,
  where OpenCV takes 0.73 for one place and 1.1-1.5 for 4 px around): the check takes 0.40 ms a mushroom and 0.85 a
  stump (medians on one thread, with other work sharing the CPU), where matched as a search does they took 1.36 and
  4.49. Where one kind's picture lies on another's is worked out once (`MobDetector.aligned`: the shift, 2 px at most,
  at which their masks overlap most, `mobs.ALIGN`): an Axe Stump's move/1 and move/3 lie a px across from a Stump's set
  on their origins, and checked there, 27 of the 40 Axe Stumps failed their axe's test. A track turned away goes on to
  its kind's whole set in the window, as one not found does (18-23 ms here), until it's taken for hidden (0.3 s,
  `tracking.MISSING_S`). From one labelled frame of a run to the next, each monster of these kinds the whole screen's
  search found re-found where it was: on Tree Dungeon 15 on their kind with the check and without; on West Rocky
  Mountain II 14 on their kind either way, and without the check an Axe Stump's track on a Stump and a Stump's on an
  Axe Stump too. `bench-look`'s feed step (her place and the monsters re-found), with the check and without it in
  turn, twice, with other work loading the CPU (its medians a third over the table's below): Tree Dungeon 33.9 and 34.2
  ms against 32.1 and 32.7, West Rocky Mountain II 36.9 and 37.8 against 33.4 and 30.3. At 1920x1080 half size
  re-finds go as they did: there the axe's test failed on 3 of the 81 Axe Stumps drawn and re-found as their next frame
  (`bench-paste`). The axe's test is worked out by `_score` in the search too: the same on every labelled frame, in the
  table below.

**The HUD's top** (`screen.Geometry.hud_top`). Monsters and drops are looked for above the HUD, and `HUD_TOP` (0.88 of
the height) put it at row 675 at 1366x768, where the chat box starts at row 700 (its dark border, from x 283 to 1080,
on every 1366x768 frame of both maps; at 1920x1080 at screen row 984-985, game row 700.2). At 1366x768 the search now
goes down to row 700 (`HUD_TOP_GAME`), and so do her band, the re-finds' bounds, the drops' strips and the view a scene
records: on Tree Dungeon those 25 rows hold platforms 31 and 32 seen from 28 (the Horny Mushroom on 31 stands half under
the chat bar there, its feet at rows 701-718, and still isn't looked for). At 1920x1080 it's as it was (the simulator
draws its world at that size, and the scenes it records go by it; the game isn't run at that size any more). Searching
the 25 rows more cost nothing measurable: the first map's whole screen took 89-98 ms either way (`bench-look --shrink
1366x768`, two runs each).

**On the labelled frames** (whole-screen searches; a monster counts as found where one is within 10 game px of its
feet; in plain view: not hidden, flinching, spawning, dying, faded, in fog or behind vines):

| | Tree Dungeon, 20 frames at 1366x768 | West Rocky Mountain II, 31 frames at 1366x768 | ... 42 frames at 1920x1080, half size | ... the same 42 shrunk to 1366x768 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| labelled (in the rows searched); in plain view | 78 (57); 44 | 116; 58 | 287; 203 | 287; 203 |
| the background learned on | the other run of frames (14 or 6) | the 91 1920x1080 frames not labelled | those 91 and the 31 at 1366x768 | the same |
| found in plain view | 44 of 44 (Horny 17, Green 27) | 56 of 58 (Axe Stump 40 of 40, Stump 16 of 18) | 172 of 203 (Stump 55 of 67, Dark Stump 28 of 39, Axe Stump 35 of 40, Red Snail 54 of 57) | 167 of 203 (52, 27, 36, 52) |
| ... of all of them | 53 of 57 | 71 of 116 | 175 of 287 | 169 of 287 |
| of the wrong kind | none | none in plain view (1 of the others: a body facing right under an axe drawn facing left, labelled unsure) | 2 | 1 |
| found that aren't there | none (with 52 players, 38 pets, all orange snails named Snail, and their name tags on the frames) | none (another player fighting the stumps, his hits' sparks, her pet, ropes, vines) | none of these kinds (6 of the first map's Orange Mushrooms on rock, as before at this size) | none |
| feet against the labels, across / down: mean; at most (game px) | 0.3 / 0.8; 2 / 3 (the labels are good to 2) | 0.0 / 0.0; 1 / 0 | 0.5 / 0.5; 1.8 / 1.1 | 0.6 / 0.2; 1.7 / 0.5 |
| facing as labelled | all 53 | all 71 | all 175 | all 169 |

On Tree Dungeon, in her band (140 units above her feet to 75 below): all 5 in plain view, 10 of 13, nothing else; with
the background learned on all 20 frames (as kept), the same, and 52 of the 57 in the rows searched. Those missed: a
Horny and a Green Mushroom dying (their caps alone, faded), a Green spawning (fading in), and a Horny standing on the
chat box's border on two frames (its own frame scored 0.58 above it). Horny against Green: at each of the 55 mushrooms
above the chat box whose frame the labels' sprite fit gave, either kind's picture found there was given the right kind
(110 of 110; the spawning, flinching and covered ones too), the closest by 0.067 and 0.074 (two Greens fading in) and
0.077 (a Horny flinching under siwooda's tag). On West Rocky Mountain II's 1366x768 frames the two missed are Stumps in
plain view, one of them walking. The 1920x1080 frames shrunk to 1366x768 aren't the game's own pixels (the stretch
blurred them) and fog covers many of them: there the game's pictures score lower and miss more than on frames of the
game at its own size. At half size, with each kind searched apart, 177 of the 203 had been found (3 of the wrong kind);
searched as one, 172.

West Rocky Mountain II's 1366x768 frames are labelled in `tests/fixtures/west_rocky_native_monsters.json` (new: each
kind by eye on 2-3x crops, the feet and facing from its sprites' best fit), its 1920x1080 ones in
`west_rocky_monsters.json`, and Tree Dungeon's in `tree_dungeon_monsters.json` (from the map's study, with every player
and pet); the frames stay in `D:/MapleHelper-scratch`, and `tests/test_gates.py` checks these numbers where they are.

**Drawn onto the maps' own frames** (`bench-paste FRAMES --map ID`: every frame of each kind, both ways, twice, at
random places): on Tree Dungeon's 20 frames at 1366x768, Green Mushrooms 28 of 28, Horny Mushrooms 27 of 28 and slimes
43 of 44 found, each as its kind and as the frame and facing drawn, scoring 1.00, the feet exact; stretched to
1920x1080, 28, 27 and 42 (scoring 0.80-0.99, the feet within 1.1 units but for a slime's hop, 7.4). On West Rocky
Mountain II's 31: Axe Stumps 20 of 20, Dark Stumps 18 of 20, Red Snails 20 of 20 and Stumps 20 of 20 (stretched, 16, 20,
20 and 20). Each one found, drawn again as the frame it may show next, was found again as the feed re-finds (44 of 44 at
each step on Tree Dungeon, all 24-32 on West Rocky Mountain II, 6 of the 32 steps between standing and walking going on
to the kind's whole set).

**What it costs** (`bench-look` on each map's own frames, 3 looks a frame, medians in ms of two runs on 2026-10-24 with
other work sharing the CPU, beside the first map's 17 bench frames shrunk to 1366x768 in the same runs):

| | Tree Dungeon (20 frames) | West Rocky Mountain II (31) | the first map's (17, shrunk) |
|---|---|---|---|
| monsters: her band | 18.7-19.7 | 18.0-18.2 | 26.3-29.3 |
| ... the whole screen | 63.8-67.6 | 105.1-106.0 | 89.1-98.2 |
| `Senses.look` with her band | 22.9-24.6 | 24.6-24.7 | 32.6-35.6 |
| ... the whole screen and every drop in view | 109.8-117.9 | 194.4-194.9 | 176.0-193.6 |
| the feed's step: her place, monsters re-found | 23.8-26.8 | 24.3-26.2 | 22.1-23.5 |
| ... with the whole screen | 85.8-96.7 | 129.0-130.3 | 109.7-119.6 |

(The day's CPU made the first map's search 15-35% slower than its rows above, 22.8 and 71.7.) So a fight's look costs
less on both maps than on the first, and so does the whole screen on Tree Dungeon; on West Rocky Mountain II the whole
screen costs about 10% more (four kinds, each stump's gate taking 4% of its rock, the bands taller for the Axe Stump),
and 146 ms with six drawn on each frame beside the monsters there (`bench-paste`; the first map's six, 85.6). On one
thread its whole screen takes 261 ms: step 1's 30 silhouette matches a band 90, the gates 80, step 2's checks 52, the
Axe Stump checks 6. Tree Dungeon's slime, a kind with a hand-made test, takes about 55 of that map's 155 ms on one
thread (22 of its 36 silhouettes a band): its colour test passes 1,000-1,500 px of trunk and platform rims a frame, none
of whose places ever passes the gate, for 24 of the map's 1,003 spawn EXP.

**To check in game** (the game at its own 1366x768; these send no keys and need no player):
- `python -m gamelab hunt look --map 10002073`, she on 28 by the portal: the Horny Mushrooms on 23 and the Green ones on
  22, 24, 29 and 30 listed on their platforms to a unit or two, each of its kind (a Horny's cap grey-blue, a Green's
  pale green on `lab/shots/hunt_look.png`), and no snail (other players' pets are orange snails named Snail) or slime
  there.
- `python -m gamelab watch --minutes 3 --map 10002073`: monsters followed and their deaths (an hour of `watch` there on
  2026-10-24, before the gates, followed none), the Greens other players kill near her among them.
- `python -m gamelab bench-look --live --n 100 --map 10002073` with the game in front: a fight's look about 25 ms and a
  whole screen about 65, the grab on top.
- The same three on West Rocky Mountain II (`--map 10004021`): its Stumps, Dark Stumps, Axe Stumps (by their axes) and
  Red Snails.
- Frames saved there in game (`snap --scale 1`, copied off `lab/shots/snap.png`), more Horny Mushrooms among them (the
  labelled ones are mostly the same one or two on 23), can go to `learn-colours` with the 20 of 2026-10-24.

Not done: monsters half under the chat box aren't looked for (the HUD covers x 283-1080 only, and the map's sides
below row 700 aren't searched either); a kind is looked for all over the screen, where its spawns can't show too (Tree
Dungeon's slime, above); a re-found monster's kind is checked at the game's own size only; and a map's background is
only as good as its frames (Tree Dungeon's 20 are one from platform 19 and two short runs from 28: the top of the
tower, 6-15, isn't in them).

**Drops of each map** (from 2026-10-24; `perception/drops.py`, `loot.detector_for`, `behavior/looting.py`'s `Worth`,
`sim/world.py`'s `game_drops`). The loot detector had looked for the first map's drops alone (`loot.ITEMS`), and the
hunt loop priced those alone. Now each map's detector looks for what its own monsters drop, the potions and the coins:
- **What a monster drops** is its meowdb MapleStory Classic page's (`meowdb.com/msclassic/monsters/<id>`, read through
  the page's own lists of drops and mesos reports on 2026-10-24). Of its three lists the community's is the one kept:
  items players say they've seen drop in game, voted up and down. An Etc or Use item there with more votes up than down
  is looked for, but scrolls (three pictures, a shop pays 1), quest items (the fossils, the Omok pieces, the Very Dry
  Branch: they drop only for a player on the quest) and arrows (a thief throws stars). Voted down as often as up or
  more, and left out: a Red Snail's Iron Ore (5.25 up, 7.25 down) and Amethyst Ore (2, 2), an Axe Stump's Wooden Top
  (3, 14.5), a Dark Stump's "Lemon (Alt)" (no item of that name in the game data). OSMLib's reference rates, on the same
  page, are kept for the simulator only ("These rates may not represent MapleStory Classic World rates": the first
  map's slimes dropped Squishy Liquid at 65% of kills where they give 40%). The mesos are the players' reports: a Horny
  Mushroom 40-53, a Green 25-30, a Stump 1-49 (one report), a Dark Stump 19-23, an Axe Stump 30-41; none for a slime
  (the first map's 10-20, as seen there) or a Red Snail.
- **A map's drops** (`drops.items_for`) are its monsters' in that order, then the potions not among them. Tree Dungeon
  looks for 16 items: Horny Mushroom Cap, Orange Potion, Bronze Ore, Blue Potion, Magic Potion, Topaz Ore, Sapphire Ore,
  Fragment of Magic, Green Mushroom Cap, Silver Ore, Red Potion, Emerald Ore, Orihalcon Ore, Slime Bubble, Squishy
  Liquid, White Potion. West Rocky Mountain II 17: Iron Ore, Leaf, Garnet Ore, Tree Branch, Red Potion, Lemon, Bronze
  Ore, Emerald Ore, Adamantium Ore, Firewood, Orihalcon Ore, Blue Potion, Opal Ore, Red Snail Shell, Silver Ore, Orange
  Potion, White Potion. The first map's 11 are pinned as they were (meowdb's slimes drop Orihalcon and Sapphire Ore too;
  none was seen there). A monster the table doesn't know adds nothing (`drops.unknown` names it, and `watch` and the commands
  with a hunter, `hunt` among them, say so as they start): the next map's monsters go into `drops.MONSTERS` from their pages.
- **The pictures** are the game data's (each item's `iconRaw`, by name), built for every map into one folder a window
  size, and a map's detector holds its own and the coins (`LootDetector(items=)`). Not all of them: with the three
  maps' pictures the first map's detector would have found 243 drops on the lab's 1920x1080 frames and the native
  scenes with its drops drawn, where its own find 216; with its own, the same 216 as before, to the field. An item whose
  kind can't be a file name is refused (`drops.safe`: "Omok Piece: Slime" would have been written as a hidden stream of
  a file "omok-piece").
- **The Fragment of Magic** is the one drop the game draws partly see-through (a crystal in a glow: of its 917 px, 404
  drawn as they are, 513 partly). At 1366x768 a picture is kept as the game's now, alpha and all, and matched and
  checked on the px drawn as they are only, as the monster sprites are. Drawn onto the new maps' frames 200 times, it
  had scored 0.95-0.98 with its glow taken as drawn, its colours 10.4 off (15.5 at most); now 1.00 and 0. The other
  pictures are opaque or clear, and the same either way.
- **Pictures that are one picture recoloured** (the ores, the gems) match each other's shapes on brightness: an Iron
  Ore's scores 0.998 where a Silver Ore lies, its colours 12.1 off there, under the check's 24. Where two kinds' pictures
  of one size are found a px apart at most with scores within 0.02 (`loot.SAME_SHAPE`), the one whose colours are
  nearer is kept. Drawn on West Rocky Mountain II's frames with a fifth of each drop behind a piece of one of its
  monsters' sprites, 9 of the 43 Silver Ores had been taken for Iron Ore (a third behind it, 14), and none now; with 4-7
  of its columns painted over on a saved frame, 168 of 1,296, and none now. The Red and Orange Potions (23.8 apart in
  colour) score 0.89 on each other.
