**What a look costs** (`bench-look` on the 17 saved frames, medians in ms; each pair of columns from runs back to back on 2026-10-20, the first 5 looks at each frame, the second 3 rounds of 3 alternating; offline, so the grab itself comes on top: 16.7 ms with mss, which waits for the compositor's next frame; with DXGI the wait for the screen's next frame, 8 ms on average at 60 frames a second, and copying it off the graphics card takes about 1 ms more than converting a saved frame):

| | at first | after the first speedups | the same, timed again | now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Eyes.look` (her tag, HP and MP, the ground, her dot, ropes, EXP) | 87.7 | 14.6 | 13.9 | 9.2 |
| `Senses.look` without monsters (the navigator's look placing her) | 88.7 | 16.0 | 17.0 | 12.9 |
| ... with the monsters in her band (a fight's look) | 180.7 | 48.0 | 48.4 | 31.8 |
| ... and the drops on her platform (the hunt loop's) | 199.6 | 62.6 | 62.4 | 43.0 |
| ... the whole screen and every drop in view | 562.5 | 229.4 | 237.1 | 162.9 |

Where it went: the capture was viewed as RGB in place, and every OpenCV call on it copied it first (8.9 ms for the half-size frame alone, now 0.9); her name tag was matched over the whole play area (46.0, now 3.4 near where she was); ropes were looked for over the whole screen with numpy's colour tests (31.4, now 8.6 where the map's can show); the monster search ran on one thread (the band 92.0, the whole screen 349.7; now 33.5 and 122.0 on 6); a monster's HP bar was read pixel by pixel (0.50, now 0.20). Then (the last two columns): each rope column was split viewed in place (8.6, now 4.3); the monster search's numpy work after each match held Python's lock (the band 34.1, now 22.5 with it in C; the whole screen 127.9, now 78.1); drops were searched strip by strip on 4 threads (see Where the time goes). Every answer is the same as before on all 17 frames. With the grab, the look placing her should take about 25 ms in game with DXGI (33 with mss) instead of 110; not measured there yet (`bench-look --live`).

**Capturing the game** (`gamelab/capture.py`, a shared module; `[capture] method` in config.toml, `"dxgi"` by default). mss copies the screen with GDI, which waits for the compositor's next frame: 16.7 ms a grab whatever the box's size, 17-46 ms after a pause, so a glance (two grabs) took 33 ms. DXGI desktop duplication (through ctypes, no new packages) is handed each frame as the monitor shows it, with when it was shown (QueryPerformanceCounter's ticks, the clock `time.monotonic` reads); the frame is copied into a texture of its own on the graphics card, and boxes of it are copied off the card as they're asked for. A frame comes only when the screen changes, so the last one is the screen as it is until another comes.
- A look waits for a frame shown after it began, as with mss (a look sees what came of the keys sent before it): up to 20 ms (`FRESH_WAIT_S`), a frame of the game's 16.7 ms and the ~2 ms a frame comes after the time it was shown; when none comes by then, nothing on screen has changed and the frame held is the screen as it is. It was 15 ms at first, shorter than a frame: against a duplication presenting every 16.7 ms with its frames coming 0-2 ms late, 6-18% of looks got the frame shown 1-3 ms before they began (now none, `tests/test_capture.py`). With nothing on screen changing, a 20 ms wait lasts 31 ms (the timer's ticks, below); a frame that comes ends it at once. A glance takes its minimap box and HP row from the newest frame, without waiting.
- Measured on this PC's desktop with the game not running (`bench-capture`: a 1920x1080 box of the 3840x2160 monitor, one corner of it changing 30-60 times a second; 100 rounds, medians): a look's capture, RGB, 12.4 ms against 24.5 with mss, of which the frame came 5.6 ms after the look began; a glance 1.0 ms against 33.7. With nothing on screen changing, a look's capture took 25 ms against 22 (with the 15 ms wait of then; with 20 ms, about 34 by the same ticks, not measured), as the wait for a frame that doesn't come ends on Windows' 15.6 ms timer ticks (a 1-15 ms timeout took 16 ms, 17-20 ms took 31). Copying the whole box off the card and converting it took 2.0 ms (3.8 copied first and then converted). The pixels were mss's exactly wherever the screen didn't change between the two grabs. Over 10,000 glances and 1,000 looks the process's memory went from 148.5 to 151.1 MB and was flat over the last 7,500 glances.
- The first frame after duplicating starts is black, with no present time: frames without one (also those where only the mouse pointer moved) don't count, and the first real one came 12 ms later even with nothing changing. Duplicating is lost on a UAC prompt, a mode change or a full-screen switch and is then started again (180-240 ms); anything else that fails falls back to mss, and DXGI is tried again 10 s later. A monitor can be duplicated only once in a process (a second DuplicateOutput was refused), so the process shares one grabber. Boxes are in the monitor's physical pixels: like mss, the grabber makes the process DPI-aware first. A game window across two monitors is grabbed with mss.
- Glances still start 63 ms apart at the soonest (`GLANCE_EVERY_S` in `perception/eyes.py`), the pace mss's two grabs and the navigator's 30 ms between glances gave: what the navigator makes of glances in a row (her dot standing still, so cutting a plan off its path and `recover`) was set at that pace, and the simulator charges it. It's judged in time now (Control: a route), so it would mean the same at any pace; without a feed glances keep this one, since the simulator charges a glance mss's 35 ms and can't say what a faster one would do, and with a feed every frame didn't pay (Continuous perception: Glances at the feed's rate).
- The elevated key player keeps mss (its Lie Detector watchdog included): it loads as little as it can. Not tried in game yet: `bench-capture` and `bench-look --live --capture dxgi` (or `mss`) measure it there.

**Capture checks: looks that aren't of the game** (from 2026-10-24; `perception/framecheck.py`, `localization/placecheck.py`, `Eyes.recover`, `Senses._take` and `regain`). At 16:46:50 a hunt on Tree Dungeon (`tour --loop --skill`) read her place off the map from its first look (x 950 on a map whose camera stops at 410, platform none) and for two minutes sent a plan off a rope every 5 s, 25 of them: nothing asked whether a look made sense. Every one of its 1,148 looks had read her dot without the map's minimap picture, with the first map's origin (Localization; the offline repro of two saved frames gives the telemetry's places to 0.1 unit), and why the picture was missing in that process isn't known: `mapdata.minimap_picture` swallowed whatever kept it from loading, and nothing logged it. At 16:48:44-48 the user moved the game window, and four looks were of a frame offset from the client area (+26,+26: the window's own title bar in the top rows; -5,+6); at 16:55:33 a look captured another program's window over the game. Now a look is checked before anything is read from it, one that fails is never handed on, and what's wrong is said in words:

| Check | Where | Constant | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| The game window: there, not minimized, the client size the eyes are for, every corner on a monitor (`window.on_screen`), in front, nothing over it (`window.shows` at a grid of 7 x 5 points, 225 x 188 px apart) | `Eyes._capture`, `glance` | `COVER_GRID` (6, 4) | 0.25 ms, the grid's 35 points 0.23 of it (the five points of before missed a window over x 300-660, y 60-360); the client 1396x1952 (as once after Chrome Remote Desktop) is said and waited out, never resized here (the commands that act set it back with Alt+Enter twice: below), and a window hanging off the desktop is said so, not taken for one covered |
| The window didn't move while it was captured: its client area read again after the shot | `_capture` | | 0.002 ms; a window being dragged moves between the two reads |
| The frame isn't old: shown less than `STALE_S` before the look | `_capture` | 2 s | the game draws many frames a second when it's in front; not seen in game |
| The minimap window's title strip ("MINI MAP", x 0-80, rows 2-20) is its picture (`assets/minimap_title.png`) | `look_at`, `glance` | mean difference 10 at most | 0.0-1.1 on the 79 good saved 1366x768 frames of both maps; 22-33 shifted 1 px, 40-82 by 2-26 px; 43 and 73 on the frames of 16:48, 191 on 16:55:33's |
| The HUD's border: row 700 dark and row 701 white, every 4th px of x 290-1075; its ends (row 701 white from 285 to 1080, dark just outside) | `look_at` | half of them at least | 1.000 on the good frames but one, 0.883 with the game's menu over part of it; 0.000 shifted up or down by a px or more; the ends tell a shift across |
| The map's minimap picture found in the minimap window (once at least) | `look_at` | `PICTURE_MATCH` 0.45 | not found: another map, or the minimap covered; her dot's origin isn't known then |
| Her place on the map: within its VR box, 20 units across and 50 down past it at most | `Senses._place_her` | `OFF_X`, `OFF_Y` | all 1,148 looks of 16:46:50 92-956 units out; that day's other tours there (10 runs, 7,634 looks) 100 or more inside |
| The game's picture where its client is (since 2026-10-26): a frame failing both anchors whose HUD border is whole further up (its sampled px and both ends, at a shift: `whole_at`), or nearer at the window's own frame, is the game drawing off its client, kind `drawn` (`framecheck.drawn_offset`, `drawn_problem`; the service's frames know the frame, `window.inset`) | `ScreenFrames.next`, `look_at` | `DRAWN_REACH` 160 up, `DRAWN_ACROSS` 64, `DRAWN_SLACK` 2 | the frame of 23:40 (below): (-18, -85), the window's frame at 300% to the px. Only a frame failing both anchors is looked at: of the 29,416 saved 1366x768 frames (20,782 distinct), 359 (245 distinct), 216 of them the fault (the 215 of the player's recording at 00:29:02-00:29:55 on 2026-10-26, as the capture shows it, black under the HUD: `tests/fixtures/capture/tree_002902_drawn.png`; and the frame of 23:40) and drawn, all at (-18, -85); none of the other 143 (30 distinct: black map changes, the drag of 16:48, 16:55:33, countdown frames shrunk from 1920x1080), with no frame known or at the insets of 100-300% (the review's scan, `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/winfix/review-detect/fp_lab.csv`, `fp_scratch.csv`); the cash shop, the login and character screens, a game window over both anchors aren't saved yet. 12.5 ms at most, on frames that fail both anchors only |

Both anchors are drawn by the game at the same pixels on any map; at 1920x1080 (and in the simulator) they aren't measured and aren't checked. Their cost on a good frame is 0.04-0.07 ms; a frame that fails also has the HUD's border searched 48 px each way to say how far it's off (about 5 ms, only then): the frames of 16:48 come out +26,+26 and -5,+6, as the minimap window and the quest box matched by hand had them. Across, the border's end nearer where it should be counts: something over one end (a game window over x 1080) only shortens it, and with the minimap closed too such a frame was "offset by (+0, +0)", which reopened the capture for nothing; it's the minimap now. A look costs about 0.3 ms more in all; a glance one more box (80 x 18 px), 0.03 ms to compare it, and the window's checks.

**When one fails.** `Eyes.look` raises `BadLook` (a `Problem`: its kind and why). `Senses` takes the look again 0.2 s later (`RETRY_GAP_S`), after `Eyes.recover`: the minimap's picture and her name tag are searched for afresh; when the capture itself may be at fault (the frame offset, or old) the duplication is opened afresh at the first such failure since a frame last passed, mss takes over for 10 s at the second (`Grabber.reopen`, `fall_back`), and the two again in turn every 5 s while they last (`CAPTURE_AGAIN_S`; going by the failures of any kind in a row, a drag that failed first as "moved" and left the capture lagging was never reopened); a window moving is waited out. While the window is unfit in a way only the user sets right (`framecheck.WAIT_KINDS`: another size, partly off the screen, gone, or minimized while waiting already), a look asks the window alone (`Eyes.window_wait`): it doesn't capture or bring the game to the front, where a hunt standing still on a resized window used to pull the game over whatever the user was doing twice a second; a game closed or crashed is waited for the same way, where it stopped the hunt. A look that fails isn't placed, the camera learns nothing from it, the other players are kept, and the listeners (her EXP and HP, the mini-map's telemetry) never see it. After 3 s of failing (`LOST_S`; the drag of 16:48 gave four bad looks in 4.1 s), `Senses.look` raises `LookLost`: the hunt loop stands still, sends nothing, takes a look every 0.5 s (`Senses.regain`) and hunts on when one passes, or ends at its time as before. Its own clocks move on by the pause (`Hunt._paused`: time without progress, the objective's and its trip's, the ground's and her spot's, and on a rope her time up there and the wait before another, Positioning), so it isn't booked as hunting time or as no progress (a pause of 125 s ended the simulator's hunt as stuck on the first look after it, and a shorter one dropped the objective at once); the game's own (respawns, drops) aren't moved. A navigator's trip stands still the same way (`Navigator.go`: the `nav` command, a trip to rest, the tour's stops), up to its deadline, or the hunt's end for the hunt loop's trip to rest, which then stops the hunt as a pass does (`stand_until`; before, a hunt could stand past its end by up to the trip's 180 s; its last trip, to stand by after its end, stands up to its own deadline), the deadline moving on by the pause, as the time its chains have stopped short of a monster does (`TripState.paused`; the escorted trips' `MEET_S`), a look taken before it not used; the climb from Tree Dungeon's floor to its top with every look failing from 4 s to 16 s stood still from 9.0 s, sent nothing, and arrived after (`tests/test_framecheck.py`); a trip the hunt loop drives a pass at a time pauses with the hunt. A pet check that meets a look failing its checks presses nothing on it and tries again 90 s later (`PetCare.check`; it used to stop the hunt); any other command stops with the reason, as a navigator's trip cancels the plans it sent on any error. A glance that fails (the window as above, or the title strip) has no dot and keeps the last HP read, so a running plan isn't cut on its account (its look after it is checked). In the simulator, every look failing from 20 s to 35 s into the hunt loop on Tree Dungeon (with positioning and the escorted trips, as merged): the first look failed at 20.7 s, no plan went out from then until they passed (a throw decided on the last good look went at 20.6 s, the next plan at 35.3 s), standing still from 24.0 s, and hunting on after (`tests/test_framecheck.py`). The log says it so, and the telemetry has each as an `event` (kinds `look`, `lost`, `window`):
- `a look failed its checks: the capture is offset by (+26, +26) px from the game's client area (the window moving while it was captured?)`, `... then: the game window moved while it was captured: (2446, 146) -> (2472, 172)`
- `the game window moved: (2446, 146) -> (2472, 172)` (between two looks that passed)
- `standing still: the game's client is 1396x1952, not 1366x768: waiting for it to be set back (it isn't resized here) (nothing is sent until the game looks as expected again)`
- `standing still: her minimap dot puts her at (950, -910), off the map (x -360..410, y -2070..460): the minimap's picture not found where it is, or another map`
- `the game looks as expected again (15.0 s after a look failed)`

**A client of the wrong size, or the game drawing off it, set right with Alt+Enter twice** (built on 2026-10-24 on `work/window-recover`, never merged; ported onto the perception service on 2026-10-26: `control/window_size.py`, `Senses.window_fix`, `window --recover`). At about 19:55 on 2026-10-24 the client was another size, the cause not known, and the user asked for the way they set it back by hand: "use alt + enter to enter full screen mode, then use alt + enter again to return to window-ed mode". On 2026-10-25 at 23:21 the game was full screen at 3840x2160 for about a minute; at 23:40 the client was the right size, 1366x768 at (2454, 107) (DPI 300%, the window 2436,22..3838,893), and the game drew its picture 18 px left and 85 up, at the window's corner (`tests/fixtures/capture/tree_234040_drawn.png`): the minimap's title strip cut off, the HUD's border on row 615, and "the capture doesn't show the game where its window is" on every look, a hunt standing still for ever. Alt+Enter twice set that right too. Both are faults `framecheck.FIX_KINDS` names (`size`, `drawn`).
- *Who sees it:* the perception service, which sends no keys: its frames say the client's size (`size`), the game drawing off its client (`drawn`, the table above), or a capture that failed outright (`capture`: mss refused mid-switch ended the frame loop, and the service with it; now the frame says so, the duplication is opened afresh or mss takes over as for an offset capture, and the loop goes on).
- *Who sends Alt+Enter:* a command that hunts (`hunt kill` and `clear`, `nav`, `tour`, `probe run`): its Senses hand every look with nothing to act on for such a fault to a `SizeFix`, which tries once the same fault, the window where it was, has stood 5 s (`STEADY_S`, a guess: never in the middle of the user's own drag), once more 30 s later (`AGAIN_S`) if the looks after didn't pass, then waits for the user (said once); 4 tries an hour at most, by any process (`HOURLY`, `lab/window_tries.json`). Any command's looks wait for the first try before LookLost (`SizeFix.pending`; a plan holding it off is waited for 10 s more at most, `HOLD_S`), where LookLost (3 s) came first and they stopped untried; the hunt loop (`tour --loop`, `--grind`) does so at her first look, then makes its tries while it stands still (`Senses.regain`; standing still set before her first look, `tour --loop` on a client drawn off ended untried). Before a try it looks for the game in play on the service's picture of the client (the HUD's three buttons, `framecheck.hud_seen`, 0.7 or more, as the pet does: Behavior: Her pet): below that (its login or character screen, where Enter types into a field or picks) no try, and the picture is looked at again 30 s on. A try is counted as its first Alt+Enter goes to the player (a try cut short, its process killed in the hold, is one too); one that brought the game forward but sent none (no picture to look for the Lie Detector on, the game not in front) isn't, here or in the hour's, the next 30 s on, 4 of them at most (`UNPRESSED`). A command that finds the client at a size the eyes have no calibration for as it starts (3840x2160, full screen) tries at once and waits while it isn't right, 180 s at most (`_fit_start`, `START_WAIT_S`, a guess: the two tries end within a minute and a half), then ends (`error: the game's client is still 3840x2160 after 180 s ...`), where it stopped at once with "not calibrated at 3840x2160 yet" (waiting for ever, it kept the pet's babysitter stopped, the pet unfed); its telemetry is recorded from before that. At 1920x1080 (the stretched window) the eyes get no fix: Alt+Enter sets the game's own size. `hunt look`, `map` and `watch` send no keys; the pet's babysitter makes one bounded try of its own (Behavior: Her pet).
- *One try* (`refit`): Alt+Enter through the player (Alt down, Enter tapped 50 ms later, Alt up 50 ms after it: `ALT_ENTER`, the explore steps' `alt+ 50 enter 50 alt-`), each a plan of its own, then a wait for the window to change (within 8 s, `CHANGE_S`) and stay as it is 1.5 s (`SETTLE_S`); from full screen none goes out sooner than 8 s after the player answered the one that put it there (counted from before that plan was made ready, a first that waited 2.5 s for the game to come forward had the way back 5.5 s after it) (`FULL_HOLD_S`, measured: at 23:41 the user's fix through `act alt+ 50 enter 50 alt- 3500 alt+ 50 enter 50 alt-` put it full screen with the first, the second, 3.5 s on, wasn't taken, and one more about 8 s after the first set it back at (2454, 107), drawn right; as built on the branch the second went once full screen had stood 1.5 s, 2.7 s after the first), and one from full screen that changes nothing is pressed again, 3 at most (`BACK_PRESSES`); then the client's size read. A game full screen already gets only the way back; the second goes out only once the first has put the game full screen (one that left it windowed, or changed nothing, would put it full screen and leave it so), and none after a first that set it right. On a fake window (`tests/test_window_size.py`): a game that takes no key for 4 s after a switch is set back with two Alt+Enters, one for 9 s with three, and one that never takes another is left full screen, said.
- *What holds it off:* the Lie Detector alarm up (said: a hunt's next plan or look raises it and she retreats, the pet goes on feeding); the Lie Detector on screen, looked for before each Alt+Enter on the service's picture of the client (below), and seen there the alarm is raised as the player's watchdog raises it (its file, the picture as its screenshot, the page): every command refuses from there, a hunt retreats, whoever is at the PC is told (only the watchdog raised it, and it didn't see the Lie Detector at another size); no picture, no Alt+Enter: none to begin with, and after one put the game full screen the look is asked again every 0.5 s for 8 s (`LOOK_FOR_S`), then the try stops with the game left full screen for the next try or the user (the way back went out unlooked: three Alt+Enters over 24 s with a game that didn't take them, and Enter can answer the quiz); a plan waiting for the player or being played (`client.busy`; before the way back from full screen it's waited for, 10 s at most, `BUSY_WAIT_S`: a food of the pet's in the hold ended the try with the game full screen); another process's try (a named mutex, `window_size.LOCK`, let go of by Windows when a process ends however it ends: the babysitter kills the pet's process when a hunt starts, where the branch's lock file stood 120 s; the pet sends nothing while another holds it: `window --recover` runs beside it); the game minimized, at the try's start or in it (switched away from full screen, the game minimizes: it isn't brought back over what the user is doing); or the game not in front once brought forward (and the player checks that again before every key: Alt+Enter in another window does something else); a plan played without its Enter is said. Alt is her jump key too: she jumps where she stands at each.
- *Through the switch:* the service never sends a key and makes no new run for it (the window at another size is a wait); its frames say `size` while full screen, `capture` for a copy refused (DXGI's lost access is duplicated again, as before), and are read as before once the client is back. With no run to look with (the service started with the game full screen: 3840x2160 has no calibration; run 0 from 23:01 to 23:22 on 2026-10-25) a full ask was dropped with the asks, the Lie Detector look got no picture ("no frame from the perception service within 1.0 s", three of three), and no Alt+Enter could go out from full screen, the tries counted all the same; now it's kept and answered then too (`Service._not_visible`: a real service and client on a test pipe, the picture three of three, at once). After a try that set it right a command's looks and glances are of frames shown after the window settled at the soonest (`Senses.settled`), with LOST_S afresh; a try that set the size right with the game still drawing off it is followed by one more 30 s on, then it's the user's (never a loop: only a look that passes starts it afresh).
- *The Lie Detector at another size:* the spotter's templates are of the game stretched to 1920x1080, and the player's watchdog grabbed the client as it is: no saved screen was seen at another size (0.53-0.78 of the thresholds at 3840x2160 to 1280x720, `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/winfix/review-safety/watchdog_sizes.out`), so nothing raised the alarm while Alt+Enter put the game full screen on purpose. A player started since 2026-10-26 brings a client of any other size than 1366x768 and 1920x1080 to 1920x1080 first (`lie_detector.to_template`), as the service's picture below is: then each form is seen as it is there, and on 545 frames of the game in play shown so at 3840x2160 and 2560x1440 none is seen (the best of any form 0.733-0.734 of its threshold, as on the same frames as they are: `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/winfix/fix2/ld_fp_sizes.py`, `ld_fp_sizes.out`). A `full` ask that takes any size (`ServiceClient.full_frame(any_size=True)`) is answered, while the frames aren't fit to read for such a fault, with the client's picture as it is (`perception.frames.glimpse`: 1366x768 as it is, any other size brought to 1920x1080; 6.2 MB down the pipe, 12 ms to convert and shrink a 3840x2160 one). On the saved Lie Detector screens as the game's own view stretched to a client of 3840x2160, 2560x1440, 1600x900, 1396x1952, 1350x700 and 1280x720, so brought to 1920x1080, every form is seen at every size (1.08-1.43 of the threshold) but the old countdown, seen at 3840x2160, 1600x900 and 1396x1952 (1.01-1.02) and not at 2560x1440, 1350x700 or 1280x720 (0.76-0.86); brought to 1366x768 instead, the countdown was seen at none (0.70-0.71; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/winfix/build/ld_sizes.py`, `ld_sizes2.py`).
- *Said* (and in the telemetry, `event` kind `window`): `the client is 1350x700, not 1366x768: Alt+Enter twice` or `the client is 3840x2160, full screen: Alt+Enter to set it back`; `the client is 1366x768 again (Alt+Enter 2 times; the window settled 1.2, 1.2 s after)`; `... (Alt+Enter 4 times; ...): the window didn't change within 8 s of Alt+Enter`, or `...: the first Alt+Enter didn't put it full screen (a second would)`; `no Alt+Enter: the Lie Detector alarm is up` (or `a plan is waiting for the player or being played`, `the game is minimized`, `another process is setting it right`); `the Lie Detector is on screen (quiz): the Lie Detector alarm raised (the player's watchdog may not see it at this size)`; `no Alt+Enter: the game isn't seen in play (its HUD's buttons match 0.30, 0.7 needed: its login or character screen?)`, `... the screen couldn't be looked at for the Lie Detector (...)` (after a first: `... in 8 s: no Alt+Enter without that look`), `... the game was minimized meanwhile: it isn't brought back over what's in front`; `Alt+Enter didn't set it right in 2 tries: waiting for the user to set the client back to 1366x768` (or `no Alt+Enter could be sent in 4 tries: ...`); at a command's start `the game's client is 3840x2160, a size the eyes aren't calibrated for: waiting 180 s at most for it to be set back`.
- `python -m gamelab window --recover` makes one try by hand (not counted in the hour's), saying the client's size, place and DPI awareness before and its size and place after; at 1366x768, windowed and drawn right by the service's newest state, it does nothing unless `--force`. Only `FULL_HOLD_S` is measured; the rest are guesses, which a try's "the window settled A, B s after" sets.
- *Not done:* nothing of it has run in game but the user's own Alt+Enters of 23:41; the watchdog's other sizes take a player started again (the user's UAC click), and whether its GDI grab sees a full-screen game at all isn't known; the in-play check has met no login, character-select or cash-shop screen (none is saved: only the desktop, black, and the play area moved over the HUD, 0.25-0.52); the drawn check's window frame is matched only at 300% (further up than 48 px it needs none); a hunt that gave up waits for the user to its end, and the pet isn't fed meanwhile (its babysitter is stopped while a hunt runs).

**Diagnosing a run.** A command that acts says, as it starts, the game's client size and place in desktop px and the process's DPI awareness (2: per monitor), and the minimap picture it loaded (`the minimap picture of map 10002073: 67x183 game px`). If the map has a picture and it won't load (tried three times), the command stops saying why, where it used to read her dot with the first map's origin; a picture that failed to load is tried again, not kept as missing. Every `scene` record has `minimap`: where the picture was found (its corner, full-size px) and how well it matched (0.645 on `tree_135315.png`). Her name tag's full-size template is cut again from calibration's after 50 looks in a row without her tag (`nametag.RECUT_AFTER`; 14 at most in that day's tours), in case it no longer matches her. A box off the monitor (the window moved off it, the desktop resized) is a capture failure now, which falls back to mss, where it stopped the command.

**Offline tools.** `bench-look`, `bodies` and the feed run offline (`saved_world`), their eyes given the map's minimap picture (above), say at once of a saved frame whose look fails its checks why it does, and the command ends (`bench.refused`; it used to take the frame again for `LOST_S`), a refused frame handing on no HP. `bench-look --shrink` leaves the frames' own checks out: the shrink blurs their 1-px anchors.

**Not done.** The continuous feed (`watch`, `--feed`) reads its frames through `Eyes.look_at`, so a frame that fails the anchors or the picture, or puts her off the map, isn't placed (a look it serves has no place, and nothing acts on it), keeps the last good frame's HP and MP, and its EXP and HP aren't read for events (the 16:48:45 frame's 0% made a hit); the feed's child stops if the map's picture won't load, and the command's own eyes (which have it) take the looks then. But its frames aren't checked for the window moving mid-capture or for age, and a feed never makes `LookLost`. Several windows with the game's title aren't told apart (the first is taken). Whether there's a platform or rope under her isn't asked (a fall off a platform can have nothing under it for hundreds of units). The checks have run on saved frames and fakes only; on the first map and West Rocky Mountain II the anchors are the same pixels, but nothing was run there. (Since 2026-10-25 the perception service checks every frame as the command's eyes did, the window moving under a capture and a capture held 2 s included, each state carrying its frame's problem, and a look given none to act on makes `LookLost` after 3 s: The perception service.)

**To check in game** (no attacks: no key is sent; a look brings the game to the front, as any look does): `python -m gamelab hunt look --every 0.5 --seconds 90 --map 10002073` prints the client's size and place, the DPI awareness and the minimap picture loaded, then her place twice a second. While it runs: drag the game window by its title bar for a few seconds and let go (expect `a look failed its checks: the game window moved while it was captured ...` or `... the capture is offset by ...`, then `the game looks as expected again`, then `the game window moved: (x, y) -> (x', y')` and her place as before, never a place off the map); hold it dragging longer than 3 s (`standing still: ...`, then as expected again once let go); close the minimap window in the game and open it again (`the minimap window's title isn't at the top left`, standing still, back). If the desktop is resized (Chrome Remote Desktop from another device) and the client isn't 1366x768 any more, it says `the game's client is WxH, not 1366x768: waiting for it to be set back` and leaves the window as it is (`hunt look` sends no keys), and while it waits it doesn't bring the game to the front: another program clicked meanwhile stays in front. Each line goes to the telemetry too (`event`). **Alt+Enter** (keys this time: Alt, her jump key, and Enter, through the player; the game switches to full screen and back: only with someone at the PC watching the screen, the player running, no Lie Detector due, the perception service up): `python -m gamelab window --recover --force` with the client at 1366x768 should say `the client is 1366x768: Alt+Enter twice`, the game going full screen and back, then `the client is 1366x768 again (Alt+Enter 2 times; the window settled A, B s after)` and the client's place after; A and B, against `CHANGE_S` (8) and `SETTLE_S` (1.5), and whether the second was taken 8 s after the first (`FULL_HOLD_S`), say whether the waits want changing. With the game put full screen by hand (Alt+Enter), `window --recover` alone (`full screen: Alt+Enter to set it back`); a `nav` between two safe platforms started on it tries at once and goes on (`_fit_start`); a `hunt look --every 0.5` while it's full screen stands still and sends nothing; with `pet --every 1` running and the game full screen, the pet says `pet: waiting: the game is full screen (3840x2160)` once and sends nothing until it's back (`pet: the game looks right again`), and during a `window --recover` of the user's it says `pet: waiting: another command is setting the game's client right with Alt+Enter`. A player started again since 2026-10-26: with the game full screen, its log should say nothing new (no Lie Detector seen on the game's own screen at 3840x2160); at the next Lie Detector while it's full screen, `LIE DETECTOR seen` as at 1366x768. Each line goes to the telemetry too (`event`).

## Localization

Her position comes from the minimap dot, which is always visible. The minimap is the map at 1/16 scale shown 1.40625× larger, so one minimap pixel is 11.4 map units; the dot is snapped to the platform she's standing on. Where the map's picture sits in the minimap window isn't in the map data: the picture is centred across in a window as wide as the map's name needs, so it moves from map to map, and on a map taller than the window shows the window scrolls it with her. The eyes match the map's own picture there (`minimap.MinimapPicture`, the miniMap canvas from the game data, `mapdata.minimap_picture`, as bands of it) and read every dot as if the picture were where it was on the first map, where the dot's origin was fitted (`PICTURE_BASE`, game px (47, 72)), by whole game px. The game draws it at whole game px: an edge fit of the picture's sides (the motion track's, 2026-10-23, since replaced by the match) put its top-left corner at game px 46.95-46.96 across on all 30 saved frames of the first map, 41.96-41.98 on all 133 of West Rocky Mountain II at 1920×1080 and 42.03-42.04 on its 31 at 1366×768, 71.92-71.98 down on all. So on the first map nothing moves (its saved frames place her as before, to the unit), and on West Rocky Mountain II the picture is 5 game px to the left: with the first map's origin her x read 80 map units left of where she stood (one minimap pixel of the game's is 16 units; on the floating step 24 -1026, clamped to its left end, for the -938 two ropes in view put her at, -946 through the picture; at 1366×768 on stair 28, -880 on no platform, for the -792 the ropes put her at, and -800 on 28 through the picture). On Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II the picture (67 × 183 game px) scrolls in a window showing 112 rows: read with the first map's origin her dot put her at x 902 on a map 360..410 wide. With another map's picture, nothing on the minimap, or Kerning City Construction Site's wider window, nothing is found, and until it has been a look isn't handed on (Perception: Capture checks; the dots were read as they are, which on Tree Dungeon put her off the map for a whole hunt). With her name tag on screen, her x is refined from a rope in view (ropes are drawn exactly at their map x) or the visible end of her platform; on a map narrower than the view (Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II, 770 wide) the camera can't move across and is centred, so her x comes from her name tag first (`pose.camera_x`). Her place on screen comes from her name tag. When the tag is hidden, for example behind the party HP box at the map's edge, it comes from the dot and the camera instead: the camera keeps her at a fixed spot on screen except near the map's edges, where it stops, and it learns that spot from the looks where the tag is visible.

The map model (`mapdata.py`) comes from the game's own data, read with `gamedata` and kept off git: platforms (footholds joined end to end), walls, ropes and ladders, portals, monster spawns, EXP per kill, each monster's body (from the boxes of its frames: how tall it is at least while it stands or walks, how tall while it flinches, how wide, and from its die1 frames how long it's seen dying; `monsters.bodies`) and the minimap's scale, in map units (the screen shows them 1.40625× larger at 1920×1080). Each foothold keeps its group, as the map image files it (`foothold/<layer>/<group>/<id>`, `Segment.group`): a floating block is one group, its floor and the walls hanging from its ends; steps joined by a wall share one (platforms 2 and 4, and the basin's 34, 35 and 36). The game's collisions in the air go by it (Motion planning), and so does what stops a star (Attack). And each keeps whether the game lets her drop through it (`forbidFallDown`, `Platform.no_drop` and `drop_ok`): 14,197 footholds in 51 of the 306 map images forbid it, none on this map. A platform's footholds almost always agree (of the 2,100 platforms on those maps, 1,340 forbid it all along, 757 nowhere, 3 in part). No drop is planned through one, nor within 8 units of one (`route.DROP_ROOM`: where she stands to drop is good to about that; a route's drop moves along to the nearest spot clear of it), and in the simulator ↓ + jump on one does nothing, as on the bottom floor. Nor does a plan drop her standing a tap's step (`flow.STEP_ACCEPT`, 8 units) outside the stretch where a drop goes through, which other moves allow: there she steps in first (from 104 on a foothold that forbade it, the plan used to drop her where she stood, and nothing happened), and so does the mover, which otherwise drops within 20 units of the move's spot. The same goes for the 30 units kept from a rope's top (down there climbs onto it), which the plan let come down to 22.

**Twins** (`MapModel.twins`, `place_of`, `places`): a platform lying on another's footholds, within its ends and 2 units of its surface all along, is that platform for everything the stack does. On West Rocky Mountain II 11 lies on 14 and 21 on 22, 0.00-0.15 units above them, in the same foothold group: each is a chain of footholds of its own (with a free left end) whose last foothold joins the long one's next (11's 119 into 14's 88, 21's 76 into 22's 73). By the client's rules (a fall lands on the highest floor under her; walking past a foothold's end takes its next or prev, or the floor under her when there's none) she lands on the twin, walks off its right end onto the long one without a step, drops 0.15 units onto the long one off its left end, and walking left from the long one's right part is on the twin (88's prev is 119). So her dot is never snapped to a twin, no flight lands on one (the long one is 0.15 lower), no move goes from or to one, spawns on it are the long one's (14 has 10, 22 has 14), and a trip to it is a trip to the long one. No drop is planned through the long one under a twin (`route.droppable`): standing there she may be on the twin, and a drop takes her through the twin alone. None on the first map. Platform 20 (-473..-442, y 2385) lies 0-6.4 units under 22's slope from -481 to -442, more than 2: it stays a platform of its own that nothing lands on (a fall lands on 22 first, and walking left on 22 keeps to 22's own foothold), with her ways off it onto 22 and down into the pit.

A monster seen on screen goes where its feet are (the matched template's origin; see Perception). With those known and her standing on the ground, it goes on whatever platform's surface is within 12 units of them (`FEET_SNAP` in `monsters.py`): monsters do leave the platforms they spawn on (in `after_rest.png` a pig walks platform 26, which spawns nothing, and was placed nowhere; live runs put pigs on the snails' 20 and 22 nine times, as the nearest pig platform). Otherwise (a template that doesn't know its origin, or her in the air, when her height above where her dot puts her shifts every monster) it goes on the platform its kind spawns on nearest its feet: feet read from the bottom of the picture that matched were up to 20 units off, which once put pigs "on" the safe platform above them, and she backed away from them and off it. Either way it's kept within that platform's ends: read from the screen one can seem up to 25 units past an end, and past the step at the end of platform 2 it would seem in front of the wall it's behind. From look to look, a monster is the same one when it's the same kind within 85 units. The move planner also tracks which way each walks and how fast (`Tracker`), to tell where it can be next: from one look to the next it takes the nearest within 90 units. It could take, for one seen walking (a move frame), the nearest to where it can have got to the way it faces, so two walking past each other aren't swapped and taken for two standing still (`WALK_MATCH`), but that goes by the pose of the template that matched, and in game that was wrong for 18% of the sightings of monsters walking and 22% of those standing, for up to 1.4 s on end (`bodies` on 3 recordings with the camera still): two pigs standing 100 units apart in move frames, facing each other, were each taken for the other walking at 125 units/s. In the simulator, where poses are right, it made no difference worth its risk: the tour and the hunt loop came out the same on all 20 seeds, and of six navigation scenarios over 16 seeds only two moved (the long way 13 of 16 arrived either way, in 64.2 s against 67.6 without it; the staircase 37.6 s against 35.5). So it's off. Which way one seen walking for the first time is heading could come from its facing too (`FACING_HEADING`), but in the simulator keeping clear of monsters went worse with it (monsters turn, and the planner allows them only 25 units back), so the heading still takes two looks. A monster she threw at is matched to the nearest: hit, it flinches and is knocked back rather than walking on (matched to the way it faced, the hunt loop in the simulator lost 6.4% of its EXP a minute).

How fast monsters walk comes from the game data too: the game's walkSpeed (125 units/s, Physics.img) times 1 + each monster's speed stat / 100, so pigs and Orange Mushrooms 125 units/s, slimes 100, snails 43.75 (`MapModel.mob_speeds`, `monsters.speeds`). On recordings with the camera still, snails walked 43.0 and 43.3 units/s (`bodies`, medians of where they walked), and in `knockback` pigs 121.2 and slimes 98.9. A base of 70 had been guessed, 1.79 times too slow: 43% of the speeds the tracker measured in live runs sat at its clamp. Where the move planner thinks a monster can get (`MonsterTrack.reach`, the hops' timing), the tracker's clamp and the simulator's monsters all go by these. In the simulator's game-like world (preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes) the faster monsters made the tour harder: 89.4 -> 82.7 EXP a minute (better on 5 seeds), HP lost 2.75 -> 3.02 bars, 95.8% -> 92.8% of her drops picked up, rests 0.65 -> 0.95 a run; the hunt loop 122.5 -> 123.9 EXP a minute (better on 10), HP lost 2.48 -> 2.96, drops 81.1% -> 75.3%, rests 1.9 -> 2.5. The simulator had been easy on her. Nine scenarios moved to other seeds; each says why. Seven no longer showed what they're there for (no monster in her way to go around, a pig walking into reach before she moved on). Two failed: on their old seeds `nav_fight_up` (10) and `nav_fight_across` (3) ran out of their 180 s, knocked off or hit mid-jump on the way and fighting 28 and 34 monsters there (over 16 seeds 10 arrive against 12 before, and 12 against 11). That is the faster monsters against decisions tuned for slow ones (hop timing, `TOO_CLOSE`, the star/claw boundary), not retuned yet; `nav_fight_up_runs_out` and `nav_fight_across_runs_out` pin the time-outs, so a retune that mends them shows: the second on seed 3, the first on 11 since the merge with the physics and attack tracks, with which it arrives on 10 (of seeds 1-48, 37 of `nav_fight_up`'s trips arrive; of 1-16, 8 of `nav_fight_across`'s).

## The perception service (from 2026-10-25)

The user, told the Lie Detector's retreat did worse with `--no-feed`: "We should just remove the --no-feed mode, perception should just always run and the other modules just work based on latest perception result"; and, of how: "run one shared perception process at all times". So the game is looked at by one process, all the time, and every command decides on the latest of what it saw.

**What runs** (`localization/service.py`; `python -m gamelab perceive [--map M]`, glue in `__main__`). One at a time on the PC (a named mutex, as the rolling recorder's), at normal priority with OpenCV on one thread and Windows' timers at 1 ms (its waits ended on 15.6 ms ticks otherwise: a 33 ms wait took 47). It never sends a key and never brings the game forward; with no game window (or one minimized, or at a size no templates are for) it waits, a state every 0.25 s saying why, and starts looking once one is there. It runs the feed's frame loop as the per-command child did (`feed.run`: the world step on every frame, its searches on a thread of their own, `worldstate`) on `ScreenFrames` through the process's one DXGI duplication (`capture.shared()`), with Eyes' own checks of before: a frame the window moved under or the capture held 2 s is unfit, and the eyes recover in the loop (`Runner.recover`: the minimap's picture and her name tag searched afresh, the duplication opened afresh, mss), every 0.2 s at most. Each state carries its frame's problem (`WorldState.bad`: the anchors, the minimap's picture, her tag, her place off the map, or the window's for a frame not on screen), and no look is given a state with one (it was served as an unplaced look, and the mover tried 40 times, nudging the mouse, then stopped the command). *Its watchdog* (`Service.stuck`): if the frame loop hasn't gone round for 10 s (`STUCK_S`; 60 while it makes a run, the map's templates loaded) or a search has been out that long, the process ends (exit code 3), its pipe and mutex with it, and a client's `ensure` or the babysitter starts another; the loop goes round every 0.2 s at the least. Before, a loop hung with its process and pipe up was noticed by nobody: every look found nothing to act on and a hunt stood still to its end, the pet unfed.
- *Its pace follows who's attached:* 30 frames a second and the hunt's schedule (her band every 0.2 s, the screen every second) while a command that hunts is, from the frame after it attaches; else 10 a second, her band every 0.5 s, the screen every 2 s, the drops at `watch`'s old rates (`IDLE`). (5 a second with fewer searches was tried on 2026-10-26 for the CPU, and set back: the user, "Don't reduce perception fps please".) Her throws are known to the tracker while a command that tells them is attached.
- *A followed monster's HP bar* is read while it shows one or her throws went its way in the last 2.5 s, and in the frame it's seen again in after 0.15 s or more unseen (`BAR_UNSEEN_S`: its bar as last read is from before what hid it, and carried over, the probes read a star that hit as a miss), else every 5th frame, its track's bar as last read meanwhile (`worldstate.BAR_EVERY`): read in every frame, the bars were 74% of what the simulator's feed cost (loop_tree_lie_detector: 37.5 s of its 41 in `SyncFeed.tick`), and live pays for them as much.
- *Its telemetry* is `watch`'s was (`Publisher`): a scene 10 times a second (every monster on screen, the drops last searched and where), the tracks twice a second, the deaths, EXP gains and new hits, her EXP records, in `lab/telemetry/<time>-perceive.jsonl`; a line on its console every 10 s (frames a second, who's attached, the monsters followed, the deaths, her EXP a minute).
- *The map:* it starts on `--map`; a command names its map at hello. The commands that hunt hold it (`Hello.fast`): one on another map has the service look at its map next (a new run, `epoch`, its rings new, told to every client), and a second that hunts on another map is refused, saying why. Any other (a viewer, or `pet`, which sends keys but reads only whole frames) moves it only while none holds it, is never refused, and a viewer says so when it looks at another map than its own; once no command that hunts is left, the service follows the newest command that acts (`Service._follow`). (The pet's babysitter, `pet --every 1` on its map outside hunts, held it as a command that acts, and a hunt on any other map was refused at its start: the babysitter stops the pet only once it sees a hunt.) A command that acts is handed only states of the run on its map (`ServiceClient.on_map`: at its start `ready` waits for it, the welcome's `next`; after a reconnect to a service on another map, the babysitter's say, its looks wait for that run) and of the run it was last welcomed to (a state of the run before can follow the new run's welcome, which goes first). The window at another size is a wait (the user sets it back, or a command that acts with Alt+Enter twice: Capture checks), never a new run.

**The commands are its clients** (`ServiceClient`, a `localization.feed.Feed`: what Senses reads), any number at once, attaching and detaching as they start and end, none its children:
- a named pipe (`\\.\pipe\gamelab-perceive`, multiprocessing.connection, the authkey in `lab/perceive.json` with its pid): down it each state, pickled once (3-5 KB), by a writer thread of each client's own (the newest 64 kept; older ones dropped, counted, and a `gap` said; what's said to one client, its welcome, a new run, a full frame's answer, never dropped); up it `hello` (name, pid, whether it sends keys, tells her throws, hunts; its map), `ask` (what to search on the next frame: every client's merged, mesos only if each asked only for mesos), `throw`, `full` (the next frame at full size), `status`, `bye`;
- the frames in shared memory: a ring of 8 look frames (25 MB at 1366×768) and 2 full-size ones for `full` asks;
- the client reconnects every 0.5 s, and after 10 s without a word from the service (`SILENT_S`: a state comes every 0.2 s at the least, every 0.25 s without the game), and while it can't reach a service it starts one (`service.ensure`: detached through WMI, as the babysitter starts its programs, from this checkout, its console in `lab/perceive.log`), if none holds the mutex, at most every 10 s. At a command's start it waits up to 20 s for its welcome (and, if the service is to look at its map, for that run), saying so.
- *`perceive --frames DIR`* (saved frames, offline) has a pipe, mutex and info file of its own (`\\.\pipe\gamelab-perceive-saved`, `lab/perceive-saved.json`): the live service starts and runs beside it, and a command reaches it only with `GAMELAB_PERCEIVE=saved` (and starts none). With the live ones, a run of it kept the live service from starting (the mutex held, and the babysitter's `gamelab perceive` matching it), and a command from the same checkout acted on saved frames.
- *Who attaches as what* (`__main__._perception`, `_hunter`): `hunt`, `nav`, `tour` (`--loop`, `--grind`) and `probe run` send keys and tell her throws; `pet` sends keys but doesn't hunt (the service keeps its idle pace for it); `hunt look`, `map` and `watch` only look. `act`, `do`, `snap`, `combo` and `lock` are as they were: the player's recordings.
- *A command's eyes take no shots* (`perception.eyes.NoScreen`: one raises): what they read off a whole frame (the pet's panels, the pick-up log's OCR, `hunt look`'s EXP number) is the service's frame at full size (`Eyes.full_frames`: `ServiceClient.full_frame`, the game brought forward first for a command that sends keys); her heading, `trip_monsters`' taller band and `hunt look`'s drops are read off the ring's frame of the look.
- *`watch`* is a viewer now: its console line every 10 s, `--heading` off the ring's frames, `--lie-detector` off a full frame twice a second; it starts the service if none runs and waits for it. Its own loop is gone (the service publishes what it did).
- *The mini-map* (`ui.sources.LiveSource`) takes what's on screen (scenes, tracks, her EXP, the deaths and hits seen) from the newest perceive file while it grows, and the rest (routes, chains, the hunt's rules, the log) from the newest command's, whose own scenes it leaves out meanwhile (they'd flip the page between two views), her facing carried over from them; with no command's file, the service's is the run. The hunter still writes its scenes to its own file: respawn, her memory and coverage read them.

**What a look is** (`Senses`: one path, a Feed always; `fresh_after`): the newest state of a frame newer than the last look's (never one frame twice), shown 68 ms after her last key at the soonest (`KEY_EFFECT_S`: the game acts on a key 66-68 ms after it; `Actions.last_key`, kept by `Actions.play` and the navigator's chains as each plan's result is read, less a chain's run on past its last key; the Lie Detector retreat's keys too, its actions telling the hunter's of each, `Actions.moves_only`: before, they went unseen there, and on loop_tree_lie_detector's seed 1, sighted at 40 s, 4 of the retreat's 13 looks were of frames shown less than 68 ms after its last key, two of them before it) and 0.1 s before the look at the most (`MAX_AGE_S`), with its searches as fresh as before (her band within 0.3 s, the screen within 0.5 s, the drops on that very frame: `feed.fresh`). What a look asks of the next frame is judged by the frame after the newest (`feed.to_ask`: judged by the look's `after`, often before the newest frame, a search the next frame would have stale went unasked, and a wide look waited 0.6 s for nothing and failed its checks). Which looks wait for the keys' effect is `KEYS_WAIT`: every look ("all", as built), the mover's placing her after a move alone ("moves"), or none; where a look can be of a frame shown before a throw's key (the last two), her heading goes by the frame where it's newer than her last arrow (`Actions.heading`: a throw without a tap doesn't turn her) and a cast's MP is off at its throw (`Attacks.on_throw`): without, with "moves", every star after a turn without a key went the wrong way, and she cast Lucky Seven with 5 MP. Glances are the newest frames 63 ms apart at the soonest, waiting 0.25 s at most; `recover` keeps the frame she stood still in. Events go with the look of their frame, each once, as before. In the simulator `SyncFeed` is the same (every scenario and harness run has it now: the `feed_*` twins folded into their originals), and on saved frames and in tests `InlineFeed`, the step run on the eyes' own frame when a look asks (`bench-look`, `bodies`, the unit tests).

**When there's nothing to act on** (perception down, the game not on screen or not as expected): a look asks again every 0.2 s, says why once, and raises LookLost after 3 s (`LOST_S`); `Hunt.run`, `Navigator.go` and `hunt look --every` then stand still and send nothing, looking every 0.5 s, and go on with their clocks moved on by the pause, as they did for the capture checks. A glance with no state has no dot and the last HP, and after 0.3 s of none raises LookLost: a chain running is cut, every key let go. A command that sends keys has the game brought to the front when it isn't, once a second at most, never while the window waits for the user (minimized, another size, gone, off the screen), at every look without a state, the ones after LOST_S too (each raised LookLost before it got that far, and a hunt standing still on a game not in front never brought it forward again). The service gone (or stuck: its watchdog ends it): the client sees its pipe close, reconnects, starts one (and the babysitter would within 5 s); a new run's track ids are new, and her memory matches by place; `watch` counts its frames, numbered from 1 again (it waited for the new numbers to pass the old ones, 0 frames a second meanwhile). A client gone is dropped with its asks. The player restarting touches nothing of this. `pet --every` waits for the game window instead of ending (with no game the babysitter restarted it every 5 s), and for a client of the wrong size or drawn off (Behavior: Her pet). A client of the wrong size, or the game drawing off it, is set right by a command that acts with Alt+Enter twice; a `full` ask for any size then gets the client's picture as it is (Capture checks).

**Removed:** `--feed`/`--no-feed` and `--sweep`; the per-command child (`FeedClient`, `serve`, `_feed`, `_FEEDS`, `_close_feeds`) and `feed.TRACKED` (always on); Senses' own look, the sweep (`sweep_hold`, `sweep_counts`, `SWEEP_SLICE`, `SWEEP_BODY_SLACK`, `Eyes.monsters_rows`) and the resting (`FEED_SLOW_S`, `FEED_SLOW_LOOKS`, `FEED_REST_S`); `Hunter.hold_sweep`, `pressed`, `SWEEP_LOCK_S`, `SWEEP_HP`; `hunt.SWEEP` and `Hunt`/`Grind`'s `sweep`; the harness's `loop-nosweep` and `--feed`, `fakes.DETECT_SLICE_S`; `NO_FEED_SWEEP` and test_feed's rest and sweep tests; `watch`'s own loop; every capture a command made (`Eyes.look`/`_capture`/`glance` on the screen, `capture.shared()` but in `ScreenFrames`). Kept: `SyncFeed`, `saved_world`, `bench-look` (its looks the step inline), calibrate's own capture.

**Measured** (D:/MapleHelper-scratch/perceive; before: master 1c9d1e3, her jump attacks in, exported to `base/`, with the feed as live has run since 2026-10-24 and on demand; after: this branch. The simulator: the hunt loop on Tree Dungeon, preset `crowd`, `--world zero-walls`, `--costs measured`, the attack choice, 8 seeds of 10 minutes).

What a look waits for and holds (`latcov3.py`, after `integ3/feed/latcov.py`: a look's wait on the fake clock; a plan's end to the next look's scene; at every look she took standing, what the look and her memory held of the monsters the world had in view):

| held by her memory at p 0.5 (off her band) | 0.75 (0.71) | 0.91 (0.93) | 0.91 (0.92) | 0.90 (0.92) |

EXP a minute swings far seed to seed (master's feed was stuck 234 s on seed 7, 92 a minute), and the stuck seconds are mostly rests a touch stood her up from (Found offline: a rest that a touch stands her up from waits out its 120 s). Against master's feed she makes more (+20.9%, 1.3 standard errors; she kills more), touched about as often a kill for it (1.10 touches a kill against 1.15; on her trips 0.26 a kill against 0.24), her decisions 72 ms later. Between the three ways of waiting for the keys, over 16 seeds (9-16 added: `pb-loop-*-fix-b`; before `BAR_UNSEEN_S`): every look so had her touched on her trips 16.4 times a run (1.45 bars) against 20.1 (1.66) for the mover's alone and 22.9 (1.85) for none, falling 33 times against 44 and 55, 75.4 touches in all against 85.6 and 90.0, 0.73 bars of HP lost a minute against 0.83 and 0.88, plans cut 30.2 times against 40.4 and 39.1, potions 18.9 against 21.4 and 23.6, EXP a minute level (225.3, 228.0, 235.4: the other two +5.5% and +9.0% seed by seed, standard errors 7.1% and 7.9%), drops 79% against 85% and 84%; on seeds 9-16 alone the same order (13.0, 16.0 and 25.9 touches on her trips). So every look waits: the design's first choice. (Measured first with the next frame's ask judged by `after`, and her heading and MP read off frames from before a throw as after it, the mover's alone had looked the safest, 13.2 touches on her trips against 21.8, and was built so until these were found.)

The Lie Detector's retreat (`ld_branch.py`, `build/ld_halves.py`: test_retreat's sweep, loop_tree_lie_detector, seeds 1-8, the sighting at 40, 75, 110 and 150 s; on a safe place 10 s after it): every look after her keys 27 of 31 (15 of 16 on seeds 1-4, 12 of 15 on 5-8), her moves alone after the sighting on every run; the mover's alone 28 of 31 and none 29 of 30, each with a run where something but her moves went after it; master's feed 28 of 29; before her jump attacks (the design's bar) 24 of 29 with the feed. Shooting 23 from 24 (loop_tree_lie_detector_23) 32 of 32.

**The service on saved frames** (`svc_measure.py`: the 20 saved 1366×768 frames of Tree Dungeon, each 30 frames in a row, `serve_saved` at a pipe of its own; a client "hunts" as the hunt loop asks: her band with her platform's drops every 0.15 s, the whole screen every 2 s, her place between; what its looks had against each frame's own whole-screen search; other work on the PC):

| | no hunting client (a viewer) | one hunting client | three at once |
|---|---|---|---|
| frames a second | 10.0 (its idle pace) | 26.5 | 25.9 |
| its CPU, cores | 0.55 | 1.66 | 1.73 |
| a look at her band: median, p90 | | 53, 83 ms | 53, 88 (each) |
| ... the whole screen; her place | | 162, 231; 7, 34 | 172-176, 220; 9-10, 39 |
| of each frame's monsters, in its looks | | 0.79 | 0.77 (each) |
| ... with 22 cores spun at 100% beside it | 10.0; 0.75 cores | 22.7; 2.03; her band 64, 108 ms; 0.70 | 19.7; 2.42; 72-78, 145-152; 0.81 |

Before, each command that looked had a child of its own (`watch`, `pet` and a hunt: a feed each, 1.33-1.50 cores at 30 frames a second, README Where the time goes; on these frames with the machine busy 11-12 frames a second on 1.2-1.4 cores) and a duplication of its own (three of Windows' four, Captured with mss, not DXGI); now one process at 0.55 cores idle and 1.7 hunting, one duplication, however many commands attach. A command's start to its first state: 1.08 s (median of 20; max 1.46), 0.73 of it importing, the attach 47 ms (max 363), where a command's own child took 1.8-2.0 s.

Robustness (`svc_measure.py robust`): 20 command starts and stops, every one served, the viewer attached throughout; two hunting commands and the viewer at once, all served (24.3 frames a second); a client killed is dropped 0.05 s after, the service going on; the service killed under a hunt three times: noticed in 5-6 ms, started again at once (`ensure`), the hunt standing still 2.27-2.30 s in all and going on with the new run's first state. Moving and closing the game window, and the player restarting, are the capture checks' and the tests' (test_service, test_framecheck): offline only.

**The simulator's cost.** With the feed in every scenario, a scenario's world step runs 30 times a simulated second where looks came a few times: `loop_tree_lie_detector` (seed 1) took 4.6 s against 2.8 on demand (34.7 against 5.9 before the bars' rule and `FakeEyes`' drawn and read bars kept by their pixels, `hud._drawn`, `FakeEyes.bar`), `loop_basin` 20 s against 3.1. The scenarios over 15 s are marked slow (`SLOW_SCENARIOS`, the shorter of each kind stay in): the suite takes 115-130 s on 8 workers with other work on the PC.

**Re-pinned** (each says why in its docstring), against master: 15 scenarios where the hunt goes otherwise with every look perception's (`nav_monsters_staircase` 2, `nav_fight_across` 4, `nav_fight_across_runs_out` 27, `move_climb_down_nudge` 8 with a nudge or a few from one side, `hunt_kill_far` and `_tag_hidden` 15, `hunt_kill_misses` 8, `fight_step_wall` 8, `tour_hunt_other_spot` 16 with the other pig at 1320, `tour_run` 3, `loop_rival` 3 with the folded twin's death someone else's, `loop_tree_23` 1, `loop_tree_ore_waits` 2, `loop_tree_lie_detector` 2, `loop_tree_lie_detector_28` 12, the one seed of 16 that goes back onto 28); West Rocky Mountain II's three left on master's seeds, expected to fail as before (`test_sim.NOT_YET`; `nav_rocky_fight_bridge` falls out of the map on seed 1 at master too); the six `feed_*` twins folded; the slow tests: test_respawn on `loop_rival` for 2 minutes, test_probe's air scorer (her jumps from x 30.0 and 30.7), air in the v83 air's world and the hidden target on seed 2; the knock-turn heading tests on seed 24 and over seeds 1-8 (3 wrong-way stars for 12 knocks, 6 for 13 blind); `test_the_jump_onto_14...` (now every seed of 1-8 falls held back to 0.6 s); the rope test's notes; the probe's unplaced looks counted by time (2 s), not by 10 looks: while a jump throw flies she's looked at 30 times a second, on no platform, and the probe stopped after its first jump throw. The frames without her tag or dot are counted on perception's frames (`trace["frames"]`).

**Goldens:** the 21 Tree Dungeon goldens re-recorded (every `loop_tree_*`, `nav_tree_*` and `tour_tree_*`): every look is perception's now (`eyes.looks` 0, the feed's counts and `frames` in each trace), so each run's plans, log and world differ from the first look on (tour_tree_run, say: 945 plans against 951, 23 EXP gains against 10, 984 simulated s against 946); the six `feed_*` goldens, whose scenarios are folded, moved out to `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/perceive/build/stale_goldens`. Other maps' goldens differ too and are left as they were.

**Found offline** on the way, fixed: *a trip stopped for good for a monster only her jump reaches* (`Hunt._interrupt` counted in reach what `Hunt._hits` does, a star from the ground or her jump's attack, where the pass shoots only its targets, `fight._wanted`: in loop_tree_walls a green mushroom on the spot's platform above her held her 107 s, 2,373 decisions and no move); *a look's ask judged by its `after`* (above); *a bar carried over a monster's hiding* (`BAR_UNSEEN_S`, above).

**After the review** (each found by reading the code or running it, and fixed; tests for each in test_retreat, test_feed, test_service, test_navigation):
- *The retreat's looks didn't wait for its keys:* `Actions.moves_only` made actions whose last key the hunter's never heard of, and `Senses.keyed` reads the hunter's; on loop_tree_lie_detector's seed 1 (sighted at 40 s) 4 of the retreat's 13 looks were of frames shown less than 68 ms after its last key, two before it; now none of its 10 (`fix/ld_keys.py`), the retreat 4.4 s against 4.8 to the same rope. The sweep is as before: 27 of 31 safe (15 of 16, 12 of 15), her moves alone on every run, 32 of 32 shooting 23 from 24 (`fix/ld_halves.py`); `loop_tree_lie_detector`'s golden re-recorded (its retreat 4.95 s against 4.78, the rest the same).
- *A hung service was restarted by nobody:* the watchdog and the clients' `SILENT_S` (above).
- *`perceive --frames` took the live service's mutex, pipe and info file:* its own now (above).
- *The pet's babysitter held the service's map,* and a hunt on any other map was refused at its start: only the commands that hunt hold it now (above).
- *After LOST_S a hunt never brought the game forward again:* LookLost was raised first (above).
- *After a reconnect a command that acts could be handed a state of another map's run,* or of the run before the one it was welcomed to: `on_map` and the epoch (above).
- *`watch` stopped counting after a new run,* its frames numbered from 1 again (above); the service's own states while it can't make a run go on from the last number.
- *A client closed between its poll and its read* raised in its thread (multiprocessing's ReadFile on a closed handle: seen once in `svc_measure.py robust`); it ends quietly when closing.

The harness (8 seeds of 10 minutes as above, `pb-loop-all-review`) is the same to the byte as the branch before these: none of them touches a hunt without the alarm or a failure of perception. `svc_measure.py robust` again (the machine quieter than before): a command's start to its first state 0.60 s (median of 20; the attach 25 ms), two hunting commands and the viewer served at 29 frames a second, a client killed dropped in 0.05 s, the service killed under a hunt three times: noticed in 5-6 ms, the hunt standing still 1.37-1.39 s and going on.

**To put it live** (supervised, the user at the PC; this branch merged first: the babysitter runs from the main checkout):
- *The babysitter* (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/live/babysit.ps1`, its owner's to change; until then its `watch` starts the service through `ensure`, its `--hz` ignored, and its hunting pattern doesn't match `perceive`, so the service runs on through hunts). In its loop, after the recorder's start:

  ```powershell
      if (@(Procs 'gamelab perceive(?!.*--frames)').Count -eq 0) {
        Start-Detached "cmd.exe /c set PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1&& set OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1&& $py -u -m gamelab perceive --map $map >> D:\MapleHelper-scratch\live\perceive.log 2>&1"
        Say "started the perception service"
      }
      foreach ($p in @(Procs 'gamelab watch --hz')) { Stop-Process -Id $p.ProcessId -Force -Confirm:$false; Say "stopped the old watch $($p.ProcessId)" }
      $hunting = @(Procs 'gamelab (tour|hunt|nav|probe|combo|act|do) ').Count -gt 0
      $pet = @(Procs 'gamelab pet')
      if ($hunting) {
        foreach ($p in $pet) { Stop-Process -Id $p.ProcessId -Force -Confirm:$false; Say "a hunt is running: stopped $($p.ProcessId)" }
      } elseif ($pet.Count -eq 0) {
        Start-Detached "cmd.exe /c set PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1&& set OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1&& $py -u -m gamelab pet --every 1 --map $map >> D:\MapleHelper-scratch\live\pet.log 2>&1"
        Say "started the pet babysitter"
      }
  ```
  in place of its `$hunting = ...` to the end of the `else { ... }` (lines 23-37): `watch` goes (the one it started, `watch --hz 10`, is stopped; a `watch` started by hand, without `--hz`, is left), the service runs through hunts, a `perceive --frames` (offline) isn't taken for it, and the pet babysitter is as it was (stopped while a hunt runs, which feeds the pet; a hunt on another map is no longer refused while it runs).
- *A check without attacks* (the game at 1366×768, no monster near her): `python -m gamelab perceive --map 10002073` (or the babysitter's), then `python -m gamelab watch --minutes 2` (frames a second near 10, `attached: watch`), `python -m gamelab map --map 10002073` and `python -m gamelab hunt look --every 0.5 --seconds 60 --map 10002073` (her place twice a second; the service's line says `attached: hunt-look`); while it runs drag the window, cover it, minimize it: each says why and stands, then goes on. Then a short `nav` between two safe platforms (keys: moves only): the service at 30 a second while it's attached (its console), back to 10 once it ends; `perceive`'s CPU share in Task Manager, and the game's frame pacing with it running (`check` on a recording). Stop the service under the `nav` (end its process): the trip stands still (`standing still: ...`), a new one starts within ~10 s (`ensure`, or the babysitter's 5 s), and the trip goes on.
- *Then* `tour --loop --minutes 10` as the design's first live run, when attack tests are allowed again.

**Not done.**
- *Live:* nothing of this has run in game; the service's CPU beside the game, the game's frame pacing, a duplication held for hours, WMI's start from a command, all to see.
- *The service doesn't start itself again:* a crash, or its watchdog's end, stops perception until a client's `ensure` (10 s) or the babysitter starts it again (~1-2 s more for its first state). A hang that holds Python's lock stops the watchdog's thread too: the clients hear nothing, drop their connection after 10 s and stand still (their reconnect waits on that process's answer), and nothing ends it (the babysitter only checks it's there). Not seen; offline the hangs tested let go of the lock (a capture that never returns).
- *The window's size:* the service fixes it at its first sight; the game restarted at another size waits for the user (or a service restart).
- *Viewers on another map:* `hunt look --map M` while a command that hunts holds the service on another map sees that map's places (it says so).
- *A rest that a touch stands her up from* still waits out its 120 s (Found offline): most of the stuck seconds above.
- *The probes on continuous perception* (test_probe, expected to fail with why): claw-down in the swings' world with the game's errors decides on 1 of seeds 1-8 (master's feed 2, on demand 8), and step-down in its crowded test world sees a lone pig or slime in front of her in 16% of its looks against 37% and throws 0-4 times in 300 s against 6-19, its fights stopping it first (master's feed the same). Their evidence (where she and the target were at the pick, from which look) wants looking at again before they're run live.

## Continuous perception

*Since 2026-10-25 every command perceives this way, through one process all the time (The perception service, above): the per-command child, `--feed`/`--no-feed`, the sweep and the feed's resting below are gone. What follows is how it came to be.*

`python -m gamelab watch` looks at the game every frame with no command running, and `hunt`, `nav` and `tour` do the same for themselves (since 2026-10-24 by default; `--no-feed` looks without it), taking their looks and glances from it. Both were tried in game once, on 2026-10-21: a 60 s `watch`, and a 134 s `tour --feed` that took all its 1072 looks and glances from the feed, none without it. That tour's summary printed '?' for each of the child's counts: the child sends them only once asked to stop, and they were read before it was (`_close_feeds`); now they're read after, and `FeedClient.close` waits for them before it closes the pipe. Off by default after the simulator's comparison of 2026-10-21 (below), it's the hunting commands' default since 2026-10-24 (The feed by default, below); not tried in game since.

**The feed by default** (2026-10-24; the user: "I imagine reading from the feed is going to be faster than the ondemand"). `hunt`, `nav` and `tour` (the hunt loop and grinding with it) take their looks and glances from the feed unless told `--no-feed`; `hunt look` starts none (what her own looks make of the game; the child takes seconds to start). With it:
- *A look reads the feed's world state* (`feed.ASK_ONLY_STALE`): her place and the monsters followed are those of the first frame shown after the look was asked for (re-found in every frame), monsters new in her band are of a search within 0.3 s, the whole screen within 0.5 s; only the drops it asks for are searched on that frame. It waits for a frame, not for a search of its own. It asks once, and again at a new frame only for a search gone stale since that it hadn't asked for (asking at every frame, the drops were asked for twice).
- *Her memory takes what the feed follows* (`feed.TRACKED`, `Feed.look`): a look at her band has the monsters the feed follows off it, re-found in that frame (`Scene.swept`, as the sweep's slices had them), and says it covered the whole screen while the feed's last search of it is within 0.5 s. The sweep runs only in looks no feed serves: while a feed rests (below), when it gives none, and once it's gone.
- *A trip's chain stops for a monster coming into its way* (`navigator.REACT`): each glance while a chain runs keeps the feed's state (`Senses.glanced`), and a monster it follows that the chain's legs ahead of her now meet (`flow.danger` over the rest of the plan, 0.25 s on at the soonest), of the kinds the chain keeps clear of, that the look the chain was planned from hadn't (one it had was planned against, or past on purpose after a wait: `Navigator._known`), cuts the plan as a hit does, while she runs along a platform with room (50 units) to stop on it; once per monster in 3 s. The next pass looks with the monsters and meets it (a shot, a way around, a wait); the stop isn't counted as a miss. The glances keep their pace and the chains that follow on their timing (the check runs on each glance's state, between the look around and the look placing her). Reacting anywhere on the ground, the cuts slid her off platforms: 31 falls in one run where there had been 4.
- *The look placing her after a stop* is of the frame a glance saw her standing still in, or a later one (`Navigator.recover`, `Senses.look`'s `after`): taken anew it waited for the feed's next frame.
- *A feed starved of the CPU is rested* (`senses.FEED_SLOW_S`, `FEED_REST_S`): the child runs at below-normal priority, so the game keeps its frame pacing, and when other work takes the CPU its frames come slowly. Once 3 looks in a row at her band or place have waited 0.15 s or more for it (a guess), or one was given up after 0.6 s (`FEED_WAIT_S`), her looks and glances are taken here for 2 s, then the feed is tried again (and rested again at once if that look is slow too); said in her log and as telemetry (`event` kind `feed`).

What a look waits and sees, in the simulator (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/integ3/feed/latcov.py`; the hunt loop on Tree Dungeon, `crowd`, zero-walls, the regular attack, `--costs measured`, 4 seeds of 5 minutes; a look's wait on the fake clock; a decision's latency from a plan's end to the next look's scene; what a look holds at every look she took standing, against the world's truth, as Her memory of the monsters measures it):

| | on demand (no feed, the sweep) | the feed, reading | the feed, asking (as before) |
|---|---|---|---|
| a look at her band with her platform's drops (the hunt loop's): median, p90 | 70, 70 ms | 43, 79 | 73, 94 |
| ... at the whole screen with every drop in view | 200, 200 | 230, 255 | 233, 270 |
| ... at her place alone | 20, 20 | 40, 55 | 44, 57 |
| a plan's end to the next decision: median, p90 | 80, 154 ms | 60, 154 | 88, 180 |
| of the monsters on screen, in the look itself | 0.32 | 0.76 | 0.77 |
| ... of those in her band | 0.56 | 0.63 | 0.68 |
| ... of those off it | 0.16 | 0.85 | 0.83 |
| held by her memory at p 0.5 (of those off her band) | 0.75 (0.70) | 0.90 (0.94) | 0.91 (0.93) |
| of what it holds, right | 0.87 | 0.85 | 0.86 |

The whole screen's look waits longer from the feed: its drops on every platform in view are searched on the look's frame (about 28 strips at 8 ms on the searches' thread), and the whole screen's monsters too when its last search is over 0.5 s old. On saved frames (`savedcov.py`: the 20 saved 1366x768 frames of Tree Dungeon, each shown for a second, the real child on them, the stack's side looking as the hunt loop does for 60 s: her band with her platform's drops every 0.15 s, the whole screen every 2 s, her place between; what a look saw against each frame's own whole-screen search), with the machine 74-94% busy with other work, the child looked at 11-12 frames a second on 1.2-1.4 cores (on the first map's 1920x1080 frames with the CPU idle, 2026-10-21: 29.6, 1.78), and a look at her band waited 149 ms for it (median; p90 655; 30 of 226 looks gave up after 0.6 s) against 61 (104) taken here. Rested when slow, the feed rested 17 times in the minute and served 112 of 329 looks: a look at her band waited 62 ms (p90 214), her place 23 (134) against 10 (24), the whole screen 280 (350) against 217 (296). The looks it served had 0.84 of each frame's monsters, those taken here 0.39 (her band and the sweep's slice); every one in her band, both ways. How fast the child is at the game's own size on an idle machine, or live, isn't measured yet (`bench-look` on these frames, other work on the CPU: its step 35.0 ms, median, p90 53.6; with her band searched in it 51.5).

In the harness (Tree Dungeon, `crowd`, zero-walls, the regular attack, `--costs measured`, 8 seeds of 10 minutes; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/integ3/feed/exp.py`, runs `f2-loop-nofeed-8x10`, `t-loop-feed-8x10`, `t-grind-nofeed-8x10` and `t-grind-feed-8x10`; the feed's runs take about 4 times as long):

| | hunt loop, no feed | hunt loop, the feed | grinding, no feed | grinding, the feed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute | 73.1 | 75.0 (+2.8% seed by seed, standard error 12.4%; better on 4 of 8) | 66.9 | 80.5 (+25.7%, 16.2%; better on 5 of 8) |
| kills a minute | 2.23 | 2.25 | 2.01 | 2.44 |
| HP lost a minute (full bars) | 1.01 | 0.94 | 1.12 | 1.04 |
