| deaths | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| touches that hurt her on her trips, a run; their HP | 31.6; 2.83 | 19.9; 1.74 | 27.3; 2.49 | 18.6; 1.60 |
| landings missed (falls) | 60 (56) | 26 (21) | 73 (55) | 22 (15) |
| potions a run | 29.5 | 27.0 | 32.8 | 29.1 |
| looks from the feed (taken without it) | | 8,285 (0) | | 8,681 (0) |

Level within the spread for the hunt loop, as asking and reading were (asking 68.4, reading 67.3 before the trips reacted; on 2026-10-21's first map asking had been 7-9% better than reading, the searches then in the frame loop); grinding's +26% is 1.6 standard errors, its kill-time prior still Lucky Seven's. What changed is her trips: with the feed reading and her memory, against the same with trips that don't react (`f4-loop-feed-noreact-8x10` against `f6-loop-feed-react-8x10`), touches on her trips 28.9 -> 23.0 a run, their HP 2.57 -> 1.85 bars, all HP lost 10.69 -> 8.69, potions 30.0 -> 23.9, EXP a minute -1.4% (standard error 10.1%). With `--costs coded` (looks taken here at 130 ms, before the speedups) the hunt loop made 74.5 EXP a minute without a feed (`integ3-loop-crowd-8x10`), as with today's costs: in the simulator the time a look takes isn't what bounds her EXP (the next throw waits for the attack's lock whatever the look took).

**A process of its own.** Python here has its one lock (the GIL) on, and the stack's planning holds it: a perception thread in the same process got 16 of its 60 looks in 2 s while the planner worked (the research's `gil.py`). So the feed (`localization/feed.py`) is a child process, started with spawn, at below-normal priority, so the game, mostly on one core, keeps its frame pacing. Its half-size frames go into a ring of 4 in shared memory (the oldest overwritten; a frame being copied out as it's overwritten is given up), and each frame's state, plain data of a few KB, down a pipe; what the stack asks of the next frame (her band searched, the whole screen, the drops) and her throws go up another. A thread on the stack's side reads the pipe as it comes, so the child never waits on the stack. If the child can't start, stops (it says why) or is killed, the stack is told and looks for itself as before. The searches (her band, the whole screen, the drops) run beside the child's frame loop, on a thread of its own (`worldstate.SearchThread`), not inside it. Measured on the 17 saved frames (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/gilbench.py`, the frame loop's step back to back, the searches 5 a second in her band and once a second over the whole screen beside it): the step took 21.4 ms on average (p90 35.7) against 19.4 (24.8) with no search beside it, and the searches 30.9 ms for her band and 124 for the whole screen (medians) against 26.0 and 93 on their own, Python's lock being held between OpenCV's matches and the C. In a process of their own, with their own templates and threads and the frame from the ring, the step took 18.9 ms (26.7) and the searches 30.6 and 103: better, for a second copy of everything the search holds. The thread is enough for the 30 frames a second, so it's a thread.

**Each frame** (`localization/worldstate.py`; 30 a second, or at once when something is asked):
- her place, as `Senses.look` takes it: her name tag, HP and MP, the ground, her minimap dot and ropes. Ropes every frame: placed by a rope in one frame and by her dot alone in the next, her x and every monster's would step by up to 11.4 units, which the tracker would take for walking;
- her EXP number and HP compared with the frame before: `exp_gain` and `hp_drop` events, each at the frame it showed; a changed number is read then (Her EXP number), the reading in the `exp_gain` (`exp_read` when it's read unchanged: to start from, or to confirm a jump), and the kills its gain was go to the tracker by kind;
- every monster followed is re-found 16 px around where it should be (`MobDetector.refind`): with the template that matched it last, 0.69 ms (median), and if that misses, with every template of its kind in the window, 3.4 ms (at 1366x768, where the last one scores under 0.9, first the frames its animation most likely went to: Screen size, re-finding). On the 17 saved frames the last template re-found 84 of 84 monsters where they were and 84 of 84 moved 8 px;
- her band is searched 5 times a second for monsters not followed yet, the whole screen once a second, the drops only when asked (`watch`: every 0.5 s on her platform and every 2 s in view), on the searches' thread: one search at a time, handed over as soon as she's placed in the frame it's due in (it needs only her place), so it runs beside that frame's re-finds. What's asked while one is out is owed, and handed over on the newest frame as soon as it's back. Its answer goes to a mailbox of one, which the frame loop empties while it waits for its next frame and in each frame once she's placed, before the re-finds, so a monster found anew is re-found in that very frame. It's merged into the tracker as of its own frame (Tracking, below), and that frame's state is given again (`late`), with the monsters and drops found in it, as the frame loop would have given it with the searches inline;
- the tracker (`localization/tracking.py`) keeps each monster's id, velocity, facing, HP bar and state, and says what happened.

**What a frame costs** (`bench-look` on the 17 saved frames, 5 looks at each, medians, the CPU idle when it began): her place with the monsters followed re-found, 17.6 ms (p90 25.8), against 16.0 for the look placing her alone; with her band searched, 48.3; with the whole screen, 137. So at 30 frames a second the step computes for about 0.8 s a second (30 × 17.6 + 5 × 31 + 120 ms), most of it on one core (the searches run on 6 threads), and a frame with a search takes longer than a frame's time. The feed's own loop on the saved frames, asked for a look every 0.1 s as a fight asks (every 5th with the drops on her platform, every 30th with the whole screen and every drop), looked at 21.7 frames a second: 625 in 40 s plain (17.3 ms, median), 155 with her band (59), 42 with the whole screen (161). Through the real child (`python -m gamelab.sim.soak feed`, the stack's side asking as above, 6 minutes, the CPU idle), a look waited 38 ms (median; p90 129) with her band, 50 (98) with the drops on her platform, 144 (263) with the whole screen and every drop, and the child looked at 24 frames a second; a blocking look takes about 55, 70 and 229 ms with its frame's wait and copy, all of it the stack's own time. With the profiling pass's speedups and a look asking for all it wants again (`ASK_ONLY_STALE` off), 4 minutes of the same: 40 ms (p90 81), 59 (129) and 227 (258), the child at 23.8 frames a second (the whole screen is now searched on the look's own frame, not taken from one up to 0.5 s before). The saved frames are there at once: in game a look from the feed waits for the screen's next frame too, about 8 ms more. With the speedups of Where the time goes, a frame's step takes 14.8 ms (with her band 33.9, with the whole screen 91.8; 17.5, 50.0 and 147.9 before them, back to back), and a second of frames on its schedule takes 1.02 s of CPU over all its threads (1.33 before; `watch`'s, with the drops, 1.21 and 1.50).

**With the searches off the frame loop** (2026-10-21; the real child on the saved frames, the stack's side asking as above, 2 minutes each, back to back with the code before, other work sharing the CPU; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/feedbench.py`): the child looked at 29.6 frames a second against 22.4, its CPU 1.78 cores against 2.37 (it searched her band 9 times a second against 14: a look asks once now, where it asked again at each frame that came before its search was done), and a look waited 46 ms (median; p90 105) with her band against 50 (97), 62 (95) with the drops on her platform against 68 (133), and 206 (260) with the whole screen and every drop against 219 (263). On `watch`'s schedule, nothing asked (and the drops every 0.5 s on her platform and every 2 s in view), with the CPU busier: 27.9 frames a second against 24.1, and 1.37 cores against 1.15 (more frames, each with its re-finds, and the searches slower beside them). A look's own search starts once her place is known in its frame, beside that frame's re-finds, and is merged as soon as it's back; one asked while another is out waits for that (the whole screen's, 100 ms, once a second), which the p90 of her band's shows. A run before her searches were handed over that early, at the end of the frame's step: 30.2 frames a second, 1.69 cores, 50 (97), 65 (103) and 205 (251), the code before 23.2, 2.17, 45 (91), 66 (130) and 210 (264). `soak feed` (3 minutes, the CPU otherwise idle): the frame loop's step 15.1 ms (median; p90 31.9), her band's search (with the drops, when asked) 25.6 (35.1), the whole screen's 99.7 (178). Stepped at 30 a second in one process with her band asked for every 0.1 s (`beside.py` there), a step with a search out beside it took 15.6, 31.6 and 19.1 ms (median, p90, mean) and one with none 13.4, 24.4 and 16.8, the searches 26.5 ms for her band (p90 31.6) and 100.4 for the whole screen (120.5). Waiting for its next frame, the frame loop polls its pipe, and Windows ends such a wait on its timer's ticks, 15.6 ms apart (a 16 ms poll took 27, a 20 ms one 32; `time.sleep`'s high-resolution timer, 16.1 and 20.1): with a 1 ms timer asked for (timeBeginPeriod, for this process alone since Windows 10 2004) the child looked at 32.9 frames a second against 30.2, the asked-for frames on top of the 30, for 0.14 cores more (the code before at 22.9 against 23.2), and on `watch`'s schedule at 28.9 against 27.9 (the code before 25.7 against 24.1). Not used: the 30 come without it; it's the knob to try should the game show fewer. The frame loop's step itself, before and after, in one process on the saved frames with a fight's asks (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/stepbench.py`, a minute each, twice, alternating with the code before, other work sharing the CPU): 14.1-14.9 ms (median; p90 29.9-30.8, mean 17.8-18.9) at 30.5-31.0 frames a second, against 15.5-15.7 (p90 47.4-49.4, mean 27.0-28.2) at 25.2-25.9 with the searches inline, where a frame with her band searched took 37.1-38.4 (p90 50.3-53.0), one with the whole screen 97.8-113 (p90 168-179) and one with neither 13.2-13.3 (p90 18.9-19.6). So no frame waits for a search now, but a frame with none takes longer at its tail than it did (p90 30 against 19): the search beside it holds Python's lock between OpenCV's matches, and shares the CPU.

**Tracking.** Sightings re-found in their windows keep their track; what a search finds is matched to the tracks that weren't (same kind, same platform, within 10 + 1.3 × its speed × the time since + 25 units, nearest first). A new one is `entered` at the screen's edge, `spawned` near a spawn point of its kind, else `appeared`. A search's answer comes back a frame or a few after its own frame, the tracks followed meanwhile (`MonsterTracker.merge`): what it found that wasn't a track re-found in that frame is matched to the tracks where they were then; a track seen since learns nothing from it, one found dead since isn't brought back nor made anew (`late sightings of the dead`), one gone off screen since stays off, and a monster no track explains is new as of the search's frame (its `entered`, `spawned` or `appeared` at that frame, once), re-found from the next frame on, and put in the path of her throws since that frame that went its way, as followed from then it would have been (so is one back from off screen): else her kill of it came out as someone else's death and an unexplained gain (3 tracks in about 7,500 merges of `feed_loop_basin`, `feed_loop_rival` and `feed_tour_hunt_basin`). The tracks re-found in that frame are those the tracker took the re-finds for, as inline (a re-find it turned down, a monster walking through one that's fading, doesn't keep that monster from the search's sightings); and a track hidden too long isn't judged gone while a search for monsters is out of a frame after it was last seen and by the frame it came due (`LATE_SEARCH_S`: inline, that search would have been merged first). A search for the drops alone isn't waited for: it can't see the track (`watch` searches for the drops alone about 1.5 times a second, each out 15-125 ms, and had held the judgement meanwhile). A monster in a move frame walks its kind's speed the way it faces, in a stand frame it stands, at once (the filter alone took 0.5 s to follow one setting off, and lost it), but only while its sightings agree (`POSE_GATE_S`): a move frame's speed once it has left a 10-unit box in the last 0.25 s (or was first seen that recently: 0.08 s of walking for a pig), a stand frame's stop once it hasn't. The pose is the matched template's frame, wrong in game for about a fifth of the sightings and for up to 1.4 s on end (see Localization), and a pig standing in move frames had been followed at 125 units/s, its track ahead of it. Otherwise an alpha-beta filter on x. One not seen in a frame is `offscreen` if where it should be is off the screen (event `left`; known again for 10 s, `back`), `hidden` on screen after 0.3 s unseen (hit, a monster hides under its damage numbers for 1-3 s), and `dead` after 3.5 s hidden, killed by someone else (`died`, cause `other`, `by` the other player by it), the 3.5 s counted from her last attack its way, since every hit renews the numbers; but only with another player on the minimap by it (400 units across, 100 up or down: `players.near_rival`), else nobody else can have killed it, and it's let go without a death (`OTHERS_BY_PLAYER`, counted as "nobody by it"; see Other players). Her EXP gain waits 1.8 s for the monster it was to go (a killed monster is still seen fading where it died for about a second): one she threw at, of the kind its size named (Her EXP number), out of sight by then, the one that stood still from the gain until it went first, then one that went after the gain, then the least HP, then the nearest her (`died`, cause `hers`; `dying` while it fades). A throw is aimed at the monsters up to 474 units ahead of her by their middles, 70 above or below her feet (`THROW_REACH`: a star's 434, `attack.reach.STAR_REACH`, and 40 for what she and they move before the pick; of the 724 picks of the tracker table's seven runs below, the farthest was 449 ahead at the throw). It had been 540, from when a star was taken to reach 460 to a body's edge, which put a kill on a monster no star reached. Played by hand (`watch`), her throws aren't known, and a gain is put on a monster within that reach. A track seen in fewer than 5 frames goes without a death (a match on scenery), and so does one that went out of sight right beside another of its kind ("lost in a crowd": in a crowd of one kind tracks lose their monsters to each other).

Checked against the simulator's own kill log, the deaths it judged (a death of hers right when a kill of hers of that kind came within 0.3 s; someone else's right when another player's kill of that kind faded then):

| | her kills | judged hers (right) | others' kills | judged someone else's (right) | let go: nobody by it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `feed_tour_hunt_basin` (nobody else hunts) | 67 | 63 (63) | 0 | 0 (0) | 0 |
| `feed_loop_basin` | 49 | 48 (48) | 0 | 0 (0) | 1 |
| `feed_loop_rival` (a rival kills one every 20 s; seed 17) | 44 | 41 (41) | 9 | 2 (2) | 0 |
| hunt loop, preset `game`, 4 minutes, seed 3 | 36 | 33 (33) | 9 | 0 (0) | 2 |
| ... seed 5 | 41 | 40 (40) | 9 | 0 (0) | 2 |
| tour, preset `game`, 4 minutes, seed 2 | 32 | 32 (32) | 10 | 4 (4) | 3 |
| ... seed 4 | 35 | 33 (33) | 7 | 1 (1) | 3 |

(Someone else's judged right when another player's kill of that kind came at most 0.3 s before it was last seen, or up to its fading's length, 0.66-1.26 s by kind, and 0.3 s more before. Measured on the feed, route and other players' tracks together (2026-10-22; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/integ-ideas/review-lens/judged7.py`), on the scenarios' seeds of now: of her 304 kills 290 judged hers, all right, and of the other player's 44, 7 judged someone else's, all right, each naming the player by it (how it got there: below, Other players and Her EXP number). Before, with the cross-validated attack model on all three tracks of 2026-10-20 and a throw's reach at 474, 263 of the 278 judged hers were right (94.6%; at master's head before it, b4bd362, 248 of 262, 94.7%), and 4 of the 16 judged someone else's (6 of 21; there at 540: the loop's seed 5 52 kills, 46 judged hers, 45 right; the tour's seed 2 5 judged someone else's, seed 4 24 right); on the perception track alone, before it, her kills were judged right 81-94% of the time in the game-like world, 92% over the seven runs.) In the game-like world (preset `game`) stars miss 27.5% of the time, a hit monster hides 1-3 s, a look misses a monster one time in ten and her dot is read to whole pixels; the rival kills anywhere on the map, mostly off screen, where no death can be seen. Her kills are judged hers 92-100% of the time there, every one right (95% over the seven runs; 83-98% before the other players and her EXP number's kinds, not all right), someone else's 7 times of the other player's 44, all right but too few to go by yet: nothing decides from them.

With the other player hunting one place at a time and on the minimap (see Other players), over the same seven runs (`feed_loop_rival` on its seed of now, 17): her kills 274, judged hers 263, right 254; the other player's kills 42, judged someone else's 14 with 4 right before a death needed another player by it, and 4, all right and each naming the player, after (10 let go, nobody by them). Of the monsters her gain may be, one with another player by it now comes after those without (`PLAYERS_LAST`): no difference in these runs. With each gain's kills of the kinds its size names (Her EXP number), against the same code without the kinds: her kills 277 -> 299, judged hers 266 -> 283, right 257 -> 283 (all of them); the other player's 42 -> 43, judged someone else's 3 -> 3, right 3 -> 2, named 3 -> 3 (the runs differ: her fights go by the kinds too). On the three tracks together (the feed's searches off its frame loop too: the table above): her kills 304, judged hers 290, all right; the other player's 44, judged someone else's 7, all right and each naming the player; 11 let go, nobody by them.

With the searches off the frame loop (2026-10-21), measured the same way on 10 seeds of 4 minutes each of the hunt loop and of the tour (preset `game`, the feed; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/judged.py`), against the code before on the same seeds: her kills 725 (before 705), 688 judged hers, 633 right (684, 630); others' kills 184 (182), 65 judged someone else's, 22 right (55, 26). Over 12 of those runs the tracker made 867 tracks (854), lost 19 in a crowd (22) and left 21 gains unexplained (16). Her kills are judged right about as often as before (92.0% of those judged hers, against 92.1%), a few fewer of them judged at all (87.3% of her kills, against 89.4%), and 43 deaths were judged someone else's that weren't, against 29: in the runs looked at closely, monsters followed while hidden at a place they had left (so a throw at them wasn't aimed at their track) and her gain put on another of the kind nearby. Nothing decides from someone else's deaths yet. Before two fixes to the merge (above: the re-finds the tracker took, and a hidden track waiting for a search out) 48 tracks had been lost in a crowd and 49 deaths judged someone else's wrongly. With a late search's new tracks put in the paths of her throws since (above), the same 20 runs: one changed (the loop's seed 5), 42 deaths judged someone else's wrongly against 43, her kills judged hers 688 with 632 right (633): of the 43, one was that.

The simulator's poses are right; in game they aren't (above). With a fifth of them read wrong, in spells of a second (`Noise.pose_wrong` 0.2), over the same seven runs the deaths wrongly judged someone else's and the tracks lost in a crowd were 14 and 30 with the gate, 25 and 38 with the pose alone (with the poses right, 9 and 12, and 10 and 11), and 32 and 34 with the filter alone; her kills were judged right about as often every way (90-94%). (Measured on the perception track, before the merge, with the looks' costs and asks as they were then; when the gate went in, 12 and 19, 34 and 36, and 41 and 46.) Requiring a pose over a few frames in a row wouldn't have done it: on the recordings the wrong ones came in stretches (walking said "move" 82% of the time from one frame, 84% from two in a row, 81% from four).

The first version was wrong far more often: in `feed_tour_hunt_basin`'s world, where nobody else hunts, it judged 49 deaths hers and 32 someone else's (she killed 51). What it took to get here, in that order: walking at once from the move frames (32 false deaths of someone else's left 22), two overlapping monsters being two (16), a track seen in the last frame having first claim on a detection and a fading one not following another walking through (11), letting crowd-lost tracks go (3), and waiting out the fade for each gain, oldest first, with a throw's reach to a body's edge plus a walk-in during the wind-up (none). In the game-like world, counting a hidden monster's 3.5 s from her last attack took someone else's deaths from 3 of 16 right to 2 of 3 (seed 3) and from 1 of 12 to 0 of 2 (seed 5).

**A look from the feed** (`Senses(feed=...)`, the hunting commands' default). A look is the newest state of a frame shown after the look was asked for (so a look still sees what came of the keys sent before it), with what it needs fresh: her band searched within 0.3 s of the state's frame (it's searched every 0.2 s, and the monsters followed are re-found in every frame), the whole screen within 0.5 s, the drops searched on that very frame (a pile just picked up mustn't be seen again: the frame searched given again, `late`). If the newest state hasn't that, the look asks the next frame for what wouldn't be fresh by then (`ASK_ONLY_STALE`, since 2026-10-24: The feed by default, above; before, it asked for all it wanted, her band searched on a frame of its own as a blocking look does, which on 2026-10-21 was worth 7-9% in the simulator: see In the simulator) and waits, 0.6 s at most; then, or as soon as the newest frame isn't showing the game, it's taken here as without a feed. Every event since the last look, up to the frame it shows, comes with it (`Scene.events`), each once (a later frame's go with the next look, whose monsters no longer show the one an EXP gain killed), and the hunter counts from them: two EXP gains between two looks are two kills, and the HP lost is every drop's, a potion drunk in between or not. Glances come from the feed's newest frames, 63 ms apart at the soonest (`senses.FEED_GLANCE_EVERY_S`, as `GLANCE_EVERY_S` without a feed), each with its frame's time (`Senses.glanced_at`): the navigator judges them in time, but glances at every frame didn't pay in the simulator (Glances at the feed's rate, below). Her throws go to the feed, for whose kills are whose. The stack's own camera learns where it keeps her from the feed's looks too, so a look it takes itself with her name tag hidden places her as the feed would have.

**`watch`** runs the same step in the foreground, her throws unknown. It records a run (`<time>-watch.jsonl`, in parts of 64 MB, the oldest telemetry deleted at each new part as when a run starts): a `session` (mode `watch`), a `scene` 10 times a second (every monster on screen, and the drops last seen), the `tracks` twice a second (id, kind, state, x, y, velocity, facing, platform, bar, hits, cause), an `event` for each death, EXP gain and hit, and an `exp` record for each gain as the hunter's (her EXP and level as read, the gain and the kinds it was, the total, EXP a minute: the mini-map's EXP line). The mini-map and the phone page follow it as they follow any run (two runs written at once take turns being the newest, so start `watch` on its own). On the saved frames a scene is 393 bytes and the tracks 848 (as written, `watch` itself run on the 17 bench frames for a minute: `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/integ-ideas/final/watch_bytes.py`): about 20 MB an hour, 490 MB a day: a part every 3 hours, and 4 days of it in the 2 GB telemetry keeps, however long it's left going. (Before the scene records said what each look searched, where it looked for drops, the screen's view, whether she stood (Respawns and drop life) and the other players (Other players), a scene was 279 bytes: 16 MB an hour, a part every 4 hours and 5 days in the 2 GB; the 'exp' records, about 110 bytes a gain, add little.) On the console: each death and whose it was, and every 10 s the frames a second and the monsters followed. It never sends a key and never brings the game to the front: a frame the game isn't wholly showing (another window in front or over one of its corners, the game minimized or not 1920×1080) isn't read, and it says so. With `--lie-detector` it checks the screen twice a second and, the first time it sees the Lie Detector, raises the alarm (gamelab then refuses to send keys until `clear-alarm`) and pages; nothing is pressed. The check runs on a thread of its own: each frame is handed to it as soon as it's taken, before its step, where it had waited for the step and taken the frame loop's time. On the saved frames on `watch`'s schedule (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/liebench.py`, 45 s each way, twice, other work sharing the CPU) its verdict came 76-82 ms after its frame (median; p90 102-106, at most 115), where after the step with the searches inline, as before, it came 111-115 ms after (p90 151-152, at most 284); 1.84 checks a second against 1.78, and the frame loop at 29 frames a second against 20.6. The check itself took 76-82 ms either way (64.5 with the CPU idle: Perception). So it sees the detector as often as before, and sooner. It isn't refused during an alarm: it sends nothing.

**In the simulator.** `Scenario(feed=True)` gives her a `SyncFeed`: the same step on eyes of its own, ticked by the fake clock at each of the game's 60 frames a second, a frame taken 30 times a second or at once when asked, its state ready its measured cost after its frame (`feed.COSTS`, as measured after the profiling pass: capture 2 ms, her place 12, a re-find 0.7, the kind's set 3.4, and a frame's step 2 more while a search is out; step 5's were 16 for her place), the next frame not before; none of it is charged to her own time. Its searches run as on the child's thread (`TimedSearches`): searched at once on the frame's own snapshot, but back only their cost after she was placed in that frame (as measured on the thread: her band 26 ms, the screen 100, a strip of drops 8; inline they had been 22, 86 and 7; step 5's 32 and 122), merged by the next frame placing her, or as they come if the frame loop is idle, each such frame's late state ready then. Five scenarios (`feed_*`) are twins of `hunt_kill`, `nav_monsters_basin`, `tour_hunt_basin`, `loop_basin` and `loop_rival`, on their seeds but three, each saying why: `feed_hunt_kill` on 7 (`hunt_kill` 6), `feed_tour_hunt_basin` on 1 (3) and `feed_loop_rival` on 17 (25; Other players). A simulated feed frame takes 5.6 ms of real time, so harness runs with `--feed` take about twice as long. In the harness, against blocking looks as fast as they're measured after the profiling pass (`--costs measured`; 20 seeds of 20 minutes, preset `game`):

| | hunt loop, blocking | hunt loop, feed | tour, blocking | tour, feed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute | 127.6 | 121.6 | 90.4 | 92.4 |
| seed by seed | | better on 4 of 20, worst −15%; −3.1% on average (standard error 2.3%) | | better on 13 of 20, worst −17%; +2.5% (2.0%) |
| share of her drops picked up | 78.2% | 89.6% | 93.9% | 96.1% |
| HP lost (bars of 1.4%) | 2.91 | 3.04 | 3.16 | 3.30 |
| rests a run | 2.5 | 2.55 | 0.5 | 0.6 |
| her looks from the feed | | 56,888 (none without it) | | 32,095 (none without it) |

(On the perception track, before the merge; with the physics and attack tracks the loop with the feed makes 3% more: see All three tracks together, under Found offline.) Neither passes the bar, so `--feed` stayed off then (the default since 2026-10-24: The feed by default, above). The hunt loop with the feed makes 3% less EXP a minute (1.3 standard errors: not clearly worse, not as good) and picks up 11 points more of its drops; the tour makes 2.5% more (1.2 standard errors) and loses a little more HP. The feed looked at 25 frames a second (busy 70% of the time, the searches inside the frame loop), 10-11% of them taken at once for a look's ask. In the loop's runs the tracker judged 3,697 deaths hers (she killed 3,936) and 248 someone else's (the rival killed 953, mostly off screen). The loop's difference isn't from her EXP counted frame by frame: with the hunter counting from the looks' pixels as before, its 20 runs came out exactly the same (two gains never fell between two of its looks), so it comes from what the feed's scenes show and when (two monsters overlapping are both seen, each re-found by its own track, where a search keeps one of them; a monster new in her band shows up a frame or two later). The simulator can't show most of what the feed is for: its perception is exact but for scripted noise, so following monsters, which way they face and who killed them gain her nothing there, and nothing decides from them yet.

How the comparison moved, run by run (EXP a minute, the feed's against blocking looks on the same seeds, leaving out seeds where either run fell out of the map at the basin's ends, a corner of the simulator's walls; see Found offline): with her band searched every 6th frame and a look asking for all it wanted, the loop −1.5% ± 2.5% and the tour +4.3% ± 2.8%; with her band searched by time and a look asking only for what wasn't fresh (`ASK_ONLY_STALE`, for the real feed's frame rate: 21.7 frames a second instead of 18.3 on the saved frames), −4.0% ± 3.1% and +2.9% ± 3.1%, the loop's rests 2.55 -> 3.35 a run (that change's own measure had been the frame rate and the `feed_*` scenarios); with a pose's speed taken only while the sightings agree, about the same (+0.1% ± 1.8% and +0.7% ± 2.6% against the run before); with the looks' costs as measured after the profiling pass, blocking looks gained more than the feed (blocking +2.9% and +6.6%; the feed's against them −8.9% ± 2.4% and −5.4% ± 1.9%); and with a look asking the next frame for all it wants again, as a blocking look searches it all on its own frame, the feed gained 7.1% ± 1.8% in the loop and 9.0% ± 2.7% in the tour, to the table above. Searching her band every 0.1 s instead of asking gained 3.5% in each; asking for her band always but the whole screen only when stale was no better than asking for both. With the searches off the frame loop (2026-10-21; preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, `--feed --costs measured`, against the code before on the same seeds, `fs-base-*` and `fs-final-*`): the hunt loop 126.5 -> 126.5 EXP a minute (better on 5 of 10 seeds, +0.3% on average, standard error 3.5%; seed by seed +0.3, −2.4, −18.2, −1.0, +25.7, +3.4, +4.3, +0.5, −7.3 and −2.6%), drops picked up 81.6% -> 90.0%, HP lost 2.82 -> 2.57 bars, rests 2.4 -> 2.2 a run; the tour 96.4 -> 94.4 (better on 4 of 10, −2.0%, standard error 2.7%; −1.1, −7.7, −7.7, −11.6, +0.6, +15.9, +0.0, +2.6, −13.2 and +1.9%), drops 93.4% -> 93.7%, HP lost 3.03 -> 2.72, rests 1.0 -> 0.8. Neither passes the bar, and neither moved by more than its standard error. The feed looked at 29.9 frames a second in each (24.9 and 25.1 before), its frame loop busy 64% and 63% of the time (70%), its searches' thread 24% and 23%, about 6 searches a second; in the loop the tracker judged 2,006 deaths hers (she killed 2,119; before, 1,962 of 2,091) and 149 someone else's (the rival killed 476; before, 136 of 467). Of that, the two fixes to the merge (Tracking: the re-finds the tracker took, and hidden tracks waiting; `fs-new-*` against `fs-final-*`) made the loop 2.0% less (better on 4 of 10, standard error 2.2%, worst −14.2%; drops picked up 92.0% -> 90.0%) and the tour 0.4% more (2.2%): the loop's +2.4% and the tour's −2.2% in the searches' own commit (f8824ea) are of the code before them. With a late search's new tracks put in the paths of her throws since, and searches for the drops alone not waited for (`fix-feed-*`, against the same code without them, `s2t63-feed-*`), one seed of each moved and the other 18 came out the same to the digit: the loop's seed 7 +9.5% and the tour's seed 10 −14.2%, each parting from the run before a few seconds after a throw that a slime found by a late search was put in the path of (at 647 s and 748 s of 1,200; their frames a frame's time apart from then on), so the loop 126.5 -> 127.2 EXP a minute and the tour 94.4 -> 93.1. Against `fs-base-*`: the loop +1.2% (better on 5 of 10, standard error 3.7%, worst −18.2%; seed by seed +0.3, −2.4, −18.2, −1.0, +25.7, +3.4, +14.1, +0.5, −7.3 and −2.6%), drops 81.6% -> 90.0%, HP lost 2.82 -> 2.59; the tour −3.5% (better on 3 of 10, standard error 2.8%, worst −13.2%; −1.1, −7.7, −7.7, −11.6, +0.6, +15.9, +0.0, +2.6, −13.2 and −12.6%), drops 93.4% -> 93.5%, HP lost 3.03 -> 2.69. Neither passes the bar. (Her looks in the simulator ask for the drops with her band, so the drops alone are seldom searched there, 2 of 4,168 searches in `feed_loop_basin` and `feed_tour_hunt_basin`, and that fix changed nothing: both seeds moved with the throws'.) In game, asking for everything cost the feed frames while the searches ran in its frame loop (with looks as often as a fight's, about 3 band searches a second more), which the simulator charged at their measured cost; on their own thread it costs the searches' time instead, a look's own search waiting behind one out.

**Glances at the feed's rate** (2026-10-21). What the navigator makes of her glances is judged in time now (Control: a route): her dot has stopped once it has stood 0.109 s, a plan is cut 0.109 s after the first glance off its path (0.046 s once one has her where the leg doesn't go), and `recover` is done once her dot has stood 0.046 s, where those were 3, 3, 2 and 2 glances 63 ms apart. At that pace nothing changed: all 86 golden traces, the harness's runs (preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, `--costs measured`, the hunt loop and the tour, with the feed and without) and 324 runs of the `nav_*` and `feed_*` scenarios, with the feed and without (each on its own seed and noise, and the `nav_*` ones on 10 seeds of the game's noise), came out the same to the digit. Glances from the feed at every frame it looks at (`senses.FEED_GLANCE_EVERY_S` 0: 30 a second, where they were 15) don't pay yet, against the same with them 63 ms apart:
- the 290 `nav_*` trips on 10 seeds of the game's noise, with the feed: 2.9% quicker in all (168 quicker, 27 slower; 0.83 s less a trip, standard error 0.58), 283 arriving against 279 (`nav_fight_up` on its own seed now arrives), but 16 landings missed against 11, 11 of them falls against 6, and 70 plans cut short by a hit against 55;
- the harness, 10 seeds: the hunt loop 126.5 -> 127.3 EXP a minute (better on 7 of 10, +0.8%, standard error 2.0%; seed by seed +7.2, +4.6, +3.8, −0.4, −13.3, +1.4, +1.3, +1.8, +7.4 and −6.0%), drops picked up 90.1% -> 87.9%, HP lost 2.57 -> 2.52 bars, rests 2.2 -> 1.6 a run; the tour 94.4 -> 87.4 (better on 3 of 10, −6.8%, standard error 4.3%; −13.6, −15.4, +8.8, +6.4, −1.7, −22.6, −28.1, −4.6, −10.8 and +13.5%), drops 93.7% -> 97.2%, HP lost 2.72 -> 2.46, rests 0.8 both. Her plans cut short by a hit, replans, misses and falls: the loop 25, 33, 8 and 4 -> 38, 44, 5 and 3 (and 2 cut off the path, against none), the tour 61, 74, 12 and 10 -> 70, 85, 14 and 10 (one cut off the path in each). On 20 seeds the loop made 2.4% more (standard error 1.6%, better on 11, the same on 3) and the tour 5.0% less (2.6%, better on 6); cut short by a hit, replans, misses and falls, the loop 61, 73, 11, 5 -> 48, 58, 7, 4 and the tour 111, 130, 17, 14 -> 140, 165, 23, 19.

Neither passes the bar, so the feed's glances keep 63 ms. Most of the misses are `recover`'s: at 149 units/s her dot can sit in one of this map's 11.4-unit minimap pixels for 76 ms, so 46 ms of it standing doesn't tell her stopped from her running, and glanced at every frame that's asked twice as often. In one trip (`nav_basin_up_right_side`, seed 10) she was taken for stopped at full speed, three frames in one pixel, and the climb planned from where the look put her missed its rope. Glanced at every frame only while a plan runs, the trips were 3.8% quicker, with 14 misses (9 falls) and 54 cut short by a hit; only in `recover`, 4.3% quicker with 17 (12) and 65. Asking `recover` for 80 ms of her dot standing (3 frames: longer than a pixel at full speed) left 12 misses (7 falls) with the trips 0.7% slower, and 109 ms 13 (8) and 3.3% slower; without a feed 80 ms (3 glances there) made the trips 2.8% slower, with 18 misses against 20 (12 falls against 14). (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/feed/s2/`: `navfeed.py`, `navcmp.py`, the harness's `s2base*`, `s2t63*` and `s2t0*` runs.)

**Not done yet.**
- *The harness without the feed by default:* `python -m gamelab.sim.harness run ... --feed` runs with it, about 4 times as long (8 seeds of 10 minutes: 4 minutes against 1, 6 jobs), so comparisons made without it don't play as live does now; the scenarios and golden traces stay without it too (the `feed_*` twins have it).
- *How the feed does at the game's own 1366x768, live:* on saved frames, with the machine busy, its child looked at 11-12 frames a second (above), and its looks were often slower than looks taken here; on an idle machine or in game it isn't measured. If it rests often in game (`the feed is slow` in her log), her looks are taken as before, sweeping a slice each as without a feed (until review on 2026-10-25 a look taken here while the feed was up but resting swept nothing, and her memory had only her band from it: in the saved-frame run above, 217 of 329 looks).
- *Trips react only where the navigator glances while a chain runs* (`Navigator._watch`): not during the mover's single moves, nor while she recovers after a chain; and the reaction is a stop, not a replan on the move.
- *Continuity across commands.* Each command with a feed starts a child of its own, so the track ids, facings and deaths a `watch` (or the command before) has followed don't reach the next command, and running `watch` beside a command makes two captures, the mini-map flipping between their two files. A `watch` that commands attach to (the research's `watch --daemon`) isn't built: what was asked for as perception that keeps running between instructions is, so far, a feed for the length of one command.
- *Glances at the feed's rate* (above: tried, and they don't pay yet). They'd want a `recover` that tells her stopped from her running inside one minimap pixel without waiting longer than 46 ms of her dot standing: at 149 units/s it can sit 76 ms in one of this map's 11.4-unit pixels.
- *The searches in a process of their own,* should the thread's share of Python's lock cost the frame loop frames in game (`watch`'s frames a second): the frame loop's step took 2.3 ms more on average with a search out on the thread beside it, and none more beside a process (above).
- *Hitboxes.* Each template's body box from the game data (its frame's lt/rb, flipped for its facing: `MobTemplate.box`) is read but not carried to `Mob` or `Monster`, and nothing uses it yet: attacks and keeping clear still go by each kind's half width and heights (`MONSTER_HALF`, `MONSTER_HEIGHT`, `MONSTER_TOP`).

**What it holds.** The ring has 4 frames, the stack's side the last 8 states (late ones among them) and at most 10,000 events not yet drained (the oldest go, counted), the searches' thread one job and one answer, the tracker only monsters seen in the last 10 s (dead ones 2 s after they're judged), her gains and throws for their windows. A late state's frame may be gone from the ring by the time its whole screen comes back (4 frames are 133 ms; on the saved frames 1 of 13 looks with the whole screen, none of 369 with her band or the drops): the stack reads a look's frame only for its size. A late state goes out with the slot its frame is in: one whose search was back before its frame's own state went out had been copied from it before it had its slot, and went with none (13-16 of 411-455 late states, a black frame for 13-15 of 300-336 looks with her band). Over 15 minutes on the saved frames neither process's memory grew beyond its swing (see Memory and disk).

**To check in game** (the first three send no keys): `watch --minutes 3` with her standing near walking monsters, then `bodies --tracks` on its telemetry file (each track's velocity against how its x moved: those standing should say under 15 units/s and those walking their speed; in the simulator's `feed_loop_basin` with a fifth of the poses read wrong that was 90% and 88% of the time with the pose taken only while the sightings agree, 76% and 72% with the pose alone, 90-93% and 88% with the poses right; also the frames a second, deaths when someone else hunts there, the mini-map following); with her standing still near walking monsters, a recording (`act 6000 --record --name still_pigs`, the player running, no keys pressed) and `bodies --recordings still_pigs` (how often the pose is what each does: on the 3 recordings so far walking said move 82% of the time and standing said stand 78%; much better would let `WALK_MATCH` and the pose's speed be trusted more); `bench-look --live --n 100` (the feed's step on the screen as it is); with `watch` running, `combo jump_back_shot --runs 2 --chain 8` and `check` on the recording, against the same without it (the game's frame pacing), and the watcher's share of the CPU (Task Manager's: on the saved frames, 1.37 cores on `watch`'s schedule against 1.15 before, and 1.78 with a fight's asks against 2.37); a supervised `tour --loop --minutes 5 --feed` (its last line says how many looks came from the feed). Since the searches went off the frame loop (2026-10-21): `watch --minutes 3` should say about 30 frames a second every 10 s (on the saved frames, on its schedule with the CPU busy, the feed's child looked at 27.9), where it said 21.2-22.4 (2026-10-21, 00:33); if it says much less with the game's frame pacing kept, the searches' thread's share of Python's lock is the suspect (the step's `work["s"]` is in each state; a process of their own is the next step, above). With `--lie-detector`, it should still raise the alarm on the Lie Detector (its check now on a thread of its own). Since the navigator judges her glances in time (2026-10-21): trips without `--feed` (`nav 17` from 23, `nav 1` from 36, a hunt loop's trips to rest) should cut, replan and miss as before, the log's "plan cut short ... after X s" as long; if the game's frames come late (a glance's frame more than one of the game's frames old, as when its frame pacing dips), three glances 63 ms apart can span less than `STILL_S` (0.109 s) and her stop takes a fourth to count, which `STILL_S` can come down for. Glances at the feed's rate (`senses.FEED_GLANCE_EVERY_S` 0) haven't been tried in game. With the feed the default (2026-10-24), on Tree Dungeon at the game's own 1366x768: a supervised `tour --loop --minutes 10 --map 10002073` (the attack choice, as by default: Lucky Seven first, the regular attack to finish and while her MP is under a cast; `--attack regular` for the regular attack alone), and its last line (looks and glances from the feed, those taken without it, the child's frames); her log's `the feed is slow` lines (the feed rested: how often, and the waits it names), `a monster coming into its way` cuts (did a monster really come, did she then shoot it or go around) and whether her trips are hurt less than on the hunts of 2026-10-24; Task Manager's share for the feed's child (below normal) and the game's frame pacing; then the same 10 minutes with `--no-feed`, against it.

## Other players

Other players show on the minimap, each as a marker: the game's own pictures (`Map.wz` `MapHelper.img/minimap`, read by `gamedata.canvas.minimap_markers`). Hers is `user`, a yellow core 6 game px across in a dark grey ring and a white halo, 12 px in all. Everyone else's are 11 px with a core 5 across: `another` (anyone: red), `party` (her party: orange), `guild` (lavender) and `friend` (a buddy: cyan); `partymaster` and `guildmaster` are 15 px tall, with a yellow crown above the core. Then `npc` (green), `portal` (two cyan rings, 14 px) and a dozen more (shops, traders, runes). Only `user`, `another` and `friend` have an origin in the data, the core's middle. The game draws them at its own 1366×768, each at a whole game pixel, and the screen shows the whole picture 1.40625× larger, filtered: `another` pasted so onto `hover_D.png` came out within half a level of the one the game drew there (0.47 on average over a 30 px square, at game px (87, 181)), and `partymaster` within 4.4 on `calibrate.png` (its opaque part; the minimap's picture behind it differs a little). So a core is 7 px across on screen.

**What's seen** (`perception/minimap.py`, `other_dots`; every look, 0.60 ms: `bench-look`, 17 frames × 5, median). A marker is a core of its kind's colour (`MARKER_COLOURS`, by hue: red, orange, cyan or lavender, bright enough), of a core's size (14-52 px, 4-9 across and down: the 11 cores on the full-size saved frames were 32-40 px and 6-7 across, the 105 found on all the saved frames 24-44 and 5-8), with a dark grey ring around it (in at least 60% of 16 directions, a dark grey px 3-5.5 px from its middle: 15 or 16 of 16 on the saved frames). Her yellow, a leader's crown, an NPC's green and the minimap's dark blue aren't any kind's colour; a portal's cyan is a buddy's, but drawn as rings, never a core of a buddy's size. A buddy's cyan is hue 92-99, 2 short of the data's other cyan markers either side (`eventNerota`'s 90, the `zeronpc` NPCs' 101).
- **On the saved frames.** Of the 31 saved 1920×1080 frames of this map, 4 show another player, and each is found, of the right kind, and nothing else is: a stranger on 2026-10-18 at 17:29 (`hover_D.png`, in the air at x 166 off 34's end; 3 s later on the basin floor at x 615, `hover_G.png`), and a leader of her party on 2026-10-21 at 01:25 and 01:30, standing on the safe platform 32 (`lie_detector_012504.png`, `calibrate.png`: its core placed as hers is, 2 units off 32's surface). In Kerning City Construction Site (`lie_detector_025439.png`) nine strangers stand in rows, their halos touching, with an NPC among them: all nine are found, and a tenth cut off by the minimap's edge, not the NPC. Crops of three of them are test fixtures (`tests/fixtures/minimap_*.png`). Of the 154 saved 960×540 frames, 151 of this map, 58 show other players (checked by eye on enlarged crops of every one), and enlarged back to full size bilinearly each is found, of the right kind, and nothing else is, nor in the other 93 (`HALF_SIZE`): the party leader on 32 in 37 of 2026-10-21 (00:26 to 01:32) and a leader at another spot in 5 of 2026-10-20; strangers in 43, of 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-21 (on 2026-10-21 in all 16 frames of the kick sweep of 00:26, in 11 of the 18 of 01:31, and in the `hunt look` of 01:52), two at once in three frames (`kick2_140_l.png`: one touching the leader's marker on either side). (Only the full-size frames had been looked at before.)
- **The game's markers pasted on those** (`tests/test_minimap.py`): each kind at 17 spots over this map's minimap (sky, cliffs, trees, platforms), on two saved frames, is found where it is drawn, of its kind, within 0.2 px (0.09 on average); none of the data's 25 other markers pasted there is (NPCs of every kind, portals, shops, traders, runes, the compass: the cyan `eventNerota` and `zeronpc` NPCs had each been found as a buddy wherever they were drawn, and `compassActive`'s star at one spot, before a buddy's hues and a core's size were narrowed, from 88-102, 70 px and 11 across); beside a portal (6-10 game px off) a stranger still is. Two strangers 5 game px apart or more (57 map units) are two, 4 apart a third of the time; nearer, the one drawn last hides most of the other's core. Touching her dot, theirs is always found 7 game px off or more (80 map units) when hers is drawn over it; 6 px off, 12% are missed, 5 px 60%.
- **Her dot isn't pulled off.** With hers drawn over theirs 5-12 game px off, of every kind, her dot doesn't move at all. Which the game draws on top, hers or theirs, isn't known: on the saved frames hers and a stranger's came no nearer than 7-8 game px (`esc_walk.png`, `kick2_120_r.png`), where either order draws the same (the two drawn so on a clear background and shrunk as the frames were fit the half-size pixels they cover whole to 11.1 and 8.2-8.6 levels either way); of two others', a party leader's is drawn over a stranger's (`kick2_140_l.png`: 12.0 levels that way, 37.9 the other). Drawn over hers, another's marker 4-5 game px off pulled her yellow's middle 1.3-1.7 px its way on average (15-19 map units, up to 2.2 px), so a yellow cut short beside another marker is now taken from its side that shows (`DOT_UNCOVER`): 0.4 px on average (up to 1.2). Over hers within 4 px, or a leader's marker (15 px tall) from just below her, theirs can hide her dot whole; then she's placed as when a tooltip covers the minimap. Her dot is the same as before on all 31 saved frames; on the half-size ones enlarged back, taking it from its side that shows moved it 0.75 px across and down where a stranger's marker touches hers (those two: there her yellow, blurred by the shrinking, is narrower than a full one).

**Where they are** (`localization/players.py`, in every look through `Senses`, and the feed's frames). Each dot is placed as hers is (`pose.Localizer`, snapped to the platform under it), less the half game pixel its core is drawn right of and below hers (`minimap.OTHER_SHIFT`, 0.70 px: their markers' origins in the data are (5, 5) of 11 px, hers (6, 6) of 12; on the saved frames hers sat 0.66 and 0.50 px left of and above the game pixel it's drawn at, a stranger's 0.16 and 0.17 right of and below it). A dot is a player once it's been seen in 2 looks in a row (`CONFIRM`), and a player is kept until their dot has been missed in 2 looks in a row and for 1 s (`MISSES`, `MISS_S`): with the feed every frame is a look, 2 of them 67 ms, and her marker drawn over theirs as she walks past them (if the game draws hers last) hides it for about half a second, so by looks alone they'd go and come back under a new id, and a death by them in between would be let go as nobody's; a player who leaves is kept 1 s longer. A look that didn't see her own dot (something over the minimap) says nothing of theirs. From look to look a dot is the same player within 4 px and 15 px a second since (40 at most; a player walks 11 px a second), the same kind, nearest first; farther, a new one. A look's `players` (`Scene.players`, with the feed `WorldState.players`) are the players then, each with an id, a kind, a map place and platform; the scene's telemetry lists them (when there are any), the hunter logs each one coming and going ("another player (stranger) on the map: platform 21 at x -399"), and the mini-map draws them. A look the feed serves brings the players its frames follow, ids and all, and the hunter's own (for the looks it takes itself when the feed can't serve one) goes on from them (`Players.adopt`; a player it finds itself has an id from 1,000,001, apart from the feed's): before, its players were as of its own last look, with ids of their own, so each switch between the two logged the same player going and coming. Her party (`party`) don't hunt against her (`players.RIVALS`): their kills near her give her a share of their EXP, and the one seen stood on the safe platform for five minutes.

**What they change.** Each use is behind a constant, its default chosen by the harness (preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes, each against the same code with it off), with the preset's other player, which kills a monster every 25 s (a guess), and with one killing every 8 s, as a real player likely does more. Either way it hunts wherever it happens to, and most runs seldom cross it: each use changed only some seeds' runs, or none. Where the harness can't tell, it says so.
- *Deaths credited to them* (the feed's tracker; see Continuous perception): a monster gone from sight is someone else's kill only with another player by it, within 400 units across and 100 up or down (`players.REACH_X`, `REACH_DY`: about a star's reach; what other players' classes reach isn't known), and the death names them. Over the tracker table's seven runs, the deaths judged someone else's went 14 -> 4 on this track, and all 4 are right (on the three tracks together 7 of the other player's 44, all right: the table).
- *Her EXP gains placed off them* (`ledger.PLAYER_KILLS`, on; `tracking.PLAYERS_LAST` for the feed, on): of the monsters of the kind her gain named that a throw of hers can have taken and that are gone from view, one by another player comes after the others. The harness can't tell: with her gains naming their kinds (Her EXP number) neither changes a run at all (0 of 80: the hunt loop and the tour, 20 seeds each, with the preset's other player and the busy one), the ledger put none of her kills on the other player's (0 of 3,569 placed, 98.8% of them right, with the busy one, on or off), and the tracker judged the same 283 of 299 hers, all right (290 of 304 on the three tracks together, all right). (Before the kinds: of about 4,200 kills in the hunt loop 9 were placed where the other player's kill was without it and 8 with it, and EXP a minute went 129.93 -> 129.76 on the 2 seeds it changed: a little against it.) They're on because the request was for her kills to go by the other players, and they only reorder monsters of the kind named; whether a real player's kills are ever in her throws' paths is for the game to say (below).
- *Their piles left to them* (`hunt.PLAYER_PILES`, off): in the hunt loop, a pile no kill of hers explains with another player by it isn't gone for, however old, unless a drop in it is worth `VALUABLE` (30 mesos). Off by the harness: with the preset's other player 11 seeds' runs changed, 6 for the better, 130.80 -> 131.16 EXP a minute (+0.4%, standard error 0.8%), but her drops picked up fell 83.7% -> 82.7%; with the busy one 11 changed, 4 for the better, 124.43 -> 122.15 (-1.9%, standard error 1.1%; the worst seed 11% down, and one fell out of the map), drops 85.3% -> 85.8%. (It had been on: before her gains named their kinds, 9 seeds changed, 4 better, 129.93 -> 129.88, drops 83.7% -> 83.9%.) The other player there takes its drops 22 s after its kill, before `OTHERS_AFTER`'s 30 s make any pile worth a trip, so only piles taken for no kill of hers are left. The tour can't tell whose drops it sees, so its looter still remembers drops by another player (`looting.PLAYER_PILES`, off): leaving them, 11 seeds' runs changed, 7 for the better (91.4 -> 92.8 EXP a minute, HP lost 3.00 -> 2.88 bars), but her drops picked up fell 94.2% -> 93.9% (seeds 1-10: 94.7% -> 92.6%), some of those by the other player being hers; with the busy one 15 changed, 11 for the better, 85.39 -> 88.47 (+4.1%, standard error 2.3%, the worst 19% down), drops 94.2% -> 93.4%.
- *Their grounds discounted* (`hunt.PLAYER_DISCOUNT`, off: 1.0). With a rival on a ground, on its spot's platform or within 200 units of its ends (`PLAYER_NEAR`, `PLAYER_DY`), the hunt loop's choice of the next ground and the rate rule's best other ground would count that much of its EXP a second. At 0.85 or 0.7 it changed 1 or 2 seeds' runs and cost seed 7 27% (129.9 -> 128.7 on average; 0.5 on seeds 1-10, 127.9 -> 125.6). Leaving the basin, she went to 17 (35% of it in reach) rather than to 4, where the other player had just come, and stayed 5 minutes. On 4, beside it, she had made more: one kill every 25 s takes little of a ground, and it moved on 90 s later. A real player may take far more; but with the other player killing every 8 s, 0.85 changed no run and 0.7 one (+0.4% on average over 20 seeds), too little to turn it on.

All of it together, against the branch's starting commit (harness, preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes; the world changed under it too, above), as it was with `hunt.PLAYER_PILES` on: the hunt loop 128.7 -> 129.2 EXP a minute (better on 5 of 10 seeds, the worst 12% down: seed 7, by the world's change; seed by seed 142.6 -> 148.4, 146.6 -> 146.0, 100.5 -> 103.0, 170.4 -> 165.5, 117.6 -> 122.3, 133.3 -> 148.6, 106.8 -> 94.2, 139.2 -> 128.3, 109.3 -> 109.0, 120.5 -> 126.3), drops picked up 80.5% -> 82.6%, HP lost 2.71 -> 2.62 bars; against the new world with the stack as it was, 127.9 -> 129.2 (better on 4 of 10, worst 7% down), drops 82.2% -> 82.6%, HP lost 2.67 -> 2.62. The tour's runs are the world's own (92.2, against 90.9 at the start; 96.2 -> 103.2, 95.1 -> 101.3, 85.5 -> 82.8, 100.0 -> 103.0, 83.2 -> 102.7, 101.6 -> 92.3, 77.5 -> 78.6, 94.0 -> 91.0, 89.4 -> 73.1, 86.3 -> 93.8), as nothing on by default changes it. Neither passes the bar (8 of 10 seeds better): the uses fire on a few seeds. With her EXP number read, `hunt.PLAYER_PILES` off and the review's fixes (`rv-final-*`), against the starting commit: the hunt loop 128.7 -> 129.6 (+0.9% paired, standard error 1.9%; better on 4 of 10, the worst 6% down: 142.6 -> 134.1, 146.6 -> 146.3, 100.5 -> 97.6, 170.4 -> 161.9, 117.6 -> 128.5, 133.3 -> 144.7, 106.8 -> 101.3, 139.2 -> 143.2, 109.3 -> 108.0, 120.5 -> 130.8), drops 80.5% -> 83.6%, HP lost 2.71 -> 2.61; the tour 90.9 -> 90.8 (-0.3%, standard error 2.2%; better on 6 of 10, the worst 13% down: 96.2 -> 103.9, 95.1 -> 102.2, 85.5 -> 88.8, 100.0 -> 101.5, 83.2 -> 72.5, 101.6 -> 93.4, 77.5 -> 71.8, 94.0 -> 97.0, 89.4 -> 87.8, 86.3 -> 89.5), drops 96.4% -> 94.2%, HP lost 3.08 -> 2.82. Neither passes the bar.

**In the simulator** the other player (`Realism.rival_every`: preset `game`, `loop_rival`, `feed_loop_rival`) hunts one place at a time: it kills a monster within 400 units across and 60 up or down of where it stands (`world.RIVAL_REACH`, `RIVAL_DY`) every 25 s on average (20 in the scenarios), and walks at 125 units/s straight across the map (as its dot goes) to another place, by a monster alive (a platform as likely as it has monsters alive), after 90 s there on average (`Realism.rival_stay`) or as soon as nothing is left in its reach; it kills nothing on the way, and its next kill comes once it's there. Before, it killed a monster anywhere on the map and had no place. Its dot is on the minimap (`FakeEyes`: where hers would be there, 0.70 px right and down, read to whole px with `Noise.dot_px`, hidden with hers by `Noise.dot_hidden`), so the whole chain runs: placed, followed, drawn, logged. What the change did to the world (harness, preset `game`, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, the stack as it was): the rival's kills 47.4 -> 43.2 a run (it walks between places), the hunt loop 128.7 -> 127.9 EXP a minute (better on 5 of 10 seeds, the worst 12% down), drops picked up 80.5% -> 82.2%, HP lost 2.71 -> 2.67 bars; the tour 90.9 -> 92.2 (better on 6 of 10, the worst 18% down), drops 96.4% -> 94.7%, HP lost 3.08 -> 3.14. `feed_loop_rival` moved to seed 17: on 25 the rival hunted 2 and 21 the whole 2 minutes, out of her sight, and the feed saw none of its kills (on 17 it hunts the basin with her: 7 kills there; of seeds 1-30, 16 showed the feed a death someone else's as this was written, 10 once such a death needed another player by it, and 13 on the three tracks together, each of them meeting all it expects). `feed_hunt_kill` moved to seed 7 on the three tracks together (2026-10-22): on 6 the kill that ended the run was a pig's (her claw's, at 24.0 s), which the feed track, where a gain's kind wasn't known, had put on the slime she was after; with the kinds known she fought on, the basin's other monsters walked into her way, and the slime was still alive when the 30 s ran out. Seed 7 is the first after 6 that meets all of it; 8 of seeds 1-16 do. `loop_rival` keeps seed 25: the rival hunts 21, then 2, and its kills are still none of hers in the ledger.

**To check in game:** with another player on the map, `hunt look` (no keys: it lists each other player's dot, its kind and where it puts them) and `snap`, whether their dots look as the game data draws them here and are found; with a party member, a buddy or a guild member in view, their colours (`MARKER_COLOURS` has them from the data, unchecked, and the party leader's crown); whose marker the game draws on top when another player stands where she does (a `snap` with them side by side: if theirs, her dot can be lost under it within 4 game px); and the position of a party, guild or buddy dot against where that player stands (their markers have no origin in the data: the party leader on 32 sat 2 units from its surface placed by its core, as hers). With another player hunting near her: `watch --minutes 3` (no keys), whether the deaths it judges someone else's name them and none comes with nobody by it; how many monsters a minute they kill on their ground (`watch`'s deaths someone else's), which says whether `hunt.PLAYER_DISCOUNT` should come on and how far (the simulator's other player kills one every 25 s); and in a supervised `tour --loop` (`hunt.PLAYER_PILES` off), her trips to piles her kills don't explain with another player by them, and what was left of those piles when she got there (taken by them: then `hunt.PLAYER_PILES` should come on), and whether the kills the log places ever sit by another player's ("... down at x ..." against `watch`'s deaths someone else's, run beside it: that's what `ledger.PLAYER_KILLS`, on, is for; never, and it can as well be off).

## Her memory of the monsters

What she knows of the monsters off her band is what her looks have seen, kept from look to look: her memory of them
(`localization/memory.py`, `Hunter.memory`). Without it, one look's monsters were all she had, and a look's monsters
are only what it searched: in the live hunts of 2026-10-24 on Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II that was the band around
her (140 units up, 75 down) on 90% of her looks. Of the 2161 looks of the 20-minute hunt of 14:01, 207 searched the whole
screen, a median 4.9 s apart and up to 22.8 s (they came only once nothing else was to be done: the hunt loop's rules 7
to 11), and what one saw was gone at the next look. "Right now during the hunt she has tunnelled vision", as the user
put it, watching her.

*(The sweep was removed on 2026-10-25: every look has the monsters perception follows on the whole screen, `Scene.swept`; The perception service.)*

**The sweep** (`localization/senses.py`, `Senses.sweep`; on in the hunt loop, `hunt.SWEEP`, `tour --loop --no-sweep`
for without; `--sweep` on `tour`, `nav` and `hunt` for the others). A look at her band also searches one slice of the
rest of the screen, the next in turn from the top down (`Eyes.monsters_rows`): 193 map units tall (as tall as each band
of a whole-screen search at the game's own size), within what the band leaves out, each overlapping the one before and
the band by the map's tallest body and 8 units, so a body cut by one's edge is whole in the next. At 1366x768, with her
standing, 4 slices go round the screen. What a slice finds goes into `Scene.swept`, not `Scene.monsters`: the fights go
by the band's monsters as before. And `Scene.rows` says where a look searched (its 'scene' record too, with `rows` and
`swept`). A slice is searched only where it costs her nothing (`Hunter.hold_sweep`): while her last attack's lock has
50 ms to go at least (her next attack waits for it anyway), or with no monster in her band; never in close quarters (a
monster on her platform within the claw's swipe) or with her HP within 0.1 of `--low-hp`. Searched on every look but
those, it cost the simulator's hunt loop on Tree Dungeon 23% of its EXP a minute (preset `crowd`, `--world zero-walls`,
20 seeds of 20 minutes, standard error 11%), and once every 0.4 s at most 11% (6.5%): the 23 ms of each slice on the
simulator's clock (with them free it made 2% more, 6.7%, 16 seeds of 10 minutes). With a feed (the hunting commands'
default since 2026-10-24) there's no sweep while it serves her looks: it searches the whole screen every second, and her
band looks have what it follows (Continuous perception: The feed by default; her memory then holds 0.94 of the monsters
off her band, against 0.70 with the sweep). What it costs (`bench-look` on the 20 saved 1366x768 frames of Tree
Dungeon, 3 looks a frame, medians in ms, with other work sharing the CPU):

| | median | p90 |
|---|---|---|
| Monsters: her band | 26.0 | 31.2 |
| ... the whole screen | 88.7 | 110.7 |
| `Senses.look` with the monsters in her band and the drops on her platform (a hunt look) | 33.7 | 41.9 |
| ... and a slice of the sweep | 49.8 | 65.5 |
| ... the whole screen and every drop in view | 143.8 | 160.6 |

So a slice adds 16 ms to her look, where a whole-screen look adds 110, and 4 of them see the whole view. With nothing
else on the CPU (OpenCV on one thread, `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/exp-grounds/design/slices.py`), a slice took 16.7 ms
(p90 25.4), her band 21.9 and the whole screen 72.9, and the 5 slices of a whole-screen search found together its 53
monsters, at the same places. A slice is searched only with 50 ms of her attack's lock to go, so at p90 it's over before
her next throw is due.

**What's remembered** (`Memory`, a `Senses` listener after the ledger's). Each monster seen: its kind, platform and x,
when it was seen last, how many looks have covered its place since without it, whether another player is by it, and
whether it's still there ("seen"), killed by her or forgotten. A sighting is one remembered when it's the same kind on
the same platform within 40 units of where it was and 1.3 times what it can have walked since (the nearest first);
else it's a new one. A kill of hers the ledger places marks the nearest of its kind on its platform within 150 units
killed; one no throw of hers explains, the likeliest of those she threw at (of the kind her EXP gain named), one the
look didn't see first. A body seen fading out where it died within 1.5 s of its kill, where it can be from its last
sighting or 30 units from where the kill was placed, is that one, not a new one (only within 30 units of the kill's
place, a body knocked 77 units on was taken for a new one in `loop_shoots_21_from_22`, and it kept her off the drops
there).

A look covers a remembered monster's place when it searched its whole body (one stretch of its rows from the body's
top, as tall as it walks and 4 units, to its feet; 30 units inside the screen's sides; not under the minimap) over half
of where it can have walked on its platform since it was seen (9 places along it); looks while she's in the air don't
count. So a body cut by the view's edge, the bottom bar or a band's edge is never missed by a look, only not found.
Taller (as tall as it flinches, 62 units for a Horny Mushroom), platform 19 seen from 27 (60 units under the view's top)
was never covered, and on the `watch` run below every respawn there added one more (5.0 remembered at a time, where the
looks saw 0.93); all of the span, platform 30 (a third of it a unit behind the bottom bar) kept its one up to a minute
after it went (0.84 against 0.22). Covered looks without it don't count while a throw of hers went its way in the last
4.7 s and it was seen since (`tracking.HIDE_S` after `HIT_AFTER_S`): it may be under her damage numbers.

How sure one is still there (`Remembered.p`) goes down two ways; it's forgotten under 0.15 (`P_FORGET`) or a minute after
it was seen (`MEMORY_S`):
- **By looks that covered its place without it:** exp(-d / 3 s), d from its last sighting to the latest such look
  (`MISS_S`). On the `watch` run of 2026-10-24 at 14:44 (8,750 whole-screen looks at 10 a second, her on 27, a crowd
  hunting 19-24 and 29-30), of the monsters missing that long with their place covered 0.84 were seen again after
  0.3 s, 0.69 after 1 s, 0.51 after 2, 0.35 after 3, 0.27 after 4 and 0.20 after 5 (exp(-d / 3): 0.90, 0.72, 0.51, 0.37,
  0.26, 0.19): a monster hit hides under its damage numbers for 1-3 s, and other players' hits too. On her hunts' own
  looks of that day the same table, by covered looks and time, was the same shape, lower in the first second (0.6-0.7)
  with her kills in it, which the memory takes from the ledger. So one look missing it says little, and about 5.3 s of
  looks without it forget it.
- **By age:** exp(-a / 45 s), a since it was seen (`STAY_S`): of the monsters seen at t, 0.75-0.86 were there again
  10 s later in that day's telemetry (normalised by the same at 2-4 s): 35-66 s.

**What reads it.** The hunt loop counts one as there at p 0.5 (`memory.SURE`, about 2 s of covered looks without it),
where it went by one look's monsters off her platform: the ground's monsters out of reach that rule 7 (better spot)
weighs, whether anything is left on the ground (the fast exit, and rule 8: a look missing one hidden under its damage
numbers doesn't empty it), and the monsters by a pile elsewhere that keep rule 5b (on the way) off it. Of the piles
the memory said were guarded where the base's look saw no guard (`loop_rest_hp_low`, `loop_spot_off_ground`,
`loop_leaves_empty_ground`), the simulator had a monster by nearly all. Her own platform's monsters, which the fights
react to, are the look's as before. For the choice of ground (the next step): `Memory.alive(t, min_p)` (each
`Remembered`: kind, platform, x, y, when seen, `p(t)`, covered looks without it, whether another player is by it, its
last sighting as a `Monster`), `there(t, platform, kinds, min_p)` (the sightings), `covered_at(platform)` (when a look
last covered all of it) and `events` (each new, killed and forgotten monster, with when).

Its telemetry is the topic 'memory' (`Memory.record`, every 0.5 s at most): each monster with its age, p, covered looks
without it, state and whether another player is by it, and how long ago each platform was last covered whole. On the
mini-map each monster remembered is a ring of its kind's colour where it was seen last, fading over 20 s to a quarter,
broken once looks covering its place have missed it; a red cross where one was killed, a dim one where one was
forgotten; the latest look's own monsters (its band and its slice) are the filled dots, as before (checked on the page,
`minimap --replay` of the hunt of 14:23 run again, `--port 8799`). `python -m gamelab memory FILE --out OUT` runs it
again on a recorded run (each look's monsters and what it searched, her kills from the 'kill' events; her throws aren't
recorded), for `minimap --replay OUT`: on the hunt of 14:01, 334 monsters seen, 130 killed by her, 194 forgotten by
looks covering their place, 2 by age, 3.2 held at a time at p 0.5 and 0.7 less sure.

**Against the simulator's truth** (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/exp-grounds/vision/eval_sim.py`; at every look she took
standing, the monsters alive whose whole body was on screen, and those of them off her band; held: the same kind on the
same platform within 80 units, one to one; a ghost: held at p 0.5 with no monster of its kind on its platform within
205; 4 seeds of 10 minutes each):

| | Tree Dungeon, `crowd`, zero-walls: sweep | no sweep | the first map, `game`: sweep | no sweep |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| monsters on screen a look (off her band) | 6.97 (3.88) | 7.21 (4.04) | 6.91 (3.51) | 6.64 (3.26) |
| ... held by the memory at p 0.5 (any p) | 0.71 (0.75) | 0.60 (0.66) | 0.74 (0.75) | 0.51 (0.54) |
| ... off her band | 0.62 (0.68) | 0.42 (0.51) | 0.66 (0.69) | 0.19 (0.25) |
| ... in the look itself (off her band) | 0.34 (0.13) | 0.28 (0.03) | 0.38 (0.11) | 0.35 (0.01) |
| ... in the last whole-screen look (off her band) | 0.28 (0.30) | 0.34 (0.39) | 0.27 (0.36) | 0.24 (0.31) |
| held at p 0.5 a look, and right | 8.2, 0.89 | 6.9, 0.88 | 7.6, 0.88 | 4.9, 0.88 |
| the last whole-screen look's, and right | 3.6, 0.87 | 4.7, 0.89 | 4.1, 0.72 | 3.6, 0.73 |

**Run again on the live runs of 2026-10-24** (`eval_live.py`; no truth in game, so at each whole-screen look, the memory
as it was before it, against what it saw: recall, of the monsters it saw off her band, those the memory held; ghosts,
of those held at p 0.5 whose place it covered, those it didn't see):

| run | whole-screen looks | off her band | held at p 0.5 | in the last whole-screen look | ghosts: memory | ghosts: last whole-screen look |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| hunt 13:53 | 21 | 56 | 0.48 | 0.45 | 0.50 | 0.60 |
| hunt 13:56 | 41 | 73 | 0.38 | 0.38 | 0.59 | 0.67 |
| hunt 14:01 (20 min) | 202 | 252 | 0.44 | 0.42 | 0.52 | 0.61 |
| hunt 14:23 | 117 | 281 | 0.57 | 0.57 | 0.40 | 0.47 |
| `watch` 14:40 (10 a second) | 200 | 641 | 0.97 | 0.94 | 0.28 | 0.05 |
| `watch` 14:44 (10 a second) | 8,744 | 32,685 | 0.98 | 0.95 | 0.25 | 0.05 |

The hunts of that day had no sweep, so between whole-screen looks the memory knew what the last of them had seen, a
little more of it (what her band and the places covered hadn't contradicted), with fewer ghosts. On the `watch` runs
how often a platform's count changed from one look to the next went down 2-6 times (19: 12.2% of looks to 4.1%; 23:
8.8% to 2.5%; 24: 6.3% to 1.1%), and it had more there (19: 1.51 at a time against 0.93 a look) and a quarter of what
it held at p 0.5 unseen in any one look: those missing under 2 s, half of whom come back.

**What it changed** (harness, the hunt loop as `tour --loop --skill` runs it, against the code before (bc036b9); the
simulator throws Lucky Seven as a regular star, so `--skill` changes nothing there; per seed in
`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/exp-grounds/vision/ab`):

| | EXP a minute | ... while alive | kills a minute alive | HP lost a minute alive (bars) | rests | deaths |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| first map, `game`, 60 seeds of 20 minutes | 130.2 -> 131.9 (+1.3%, standard error 1.8%) | 132.2 -> 131.9 | 10.76 -> 10.76 | 0.14 -> 0.15 | 1.93 -> 2.02 | 0 -> 0 |
| ... `--costs measured` | 134.3 -> 133.3 (-0.7%, 1.0%) | 134.6 -> 133.3 | 10.92 -> 10.91 | 0.14 -> 0.14 | 2.07 -> 1.90 | 0 -> 0 |
| Tree Dungeon, `crowd`, zero-walls, 20 seeds | 37.4 -> 37.4 (-0.1%, 9.1%) | 57.3 -> 52.2 (-8.8%, 6.4%) | 1.64 -> 1.49 | 1.76 -> 1.64 | 4.9 -> 6.5 | 18 -> 17 |
| ... `crowd-slow` | 31.4 -> 33.6 (+6.9%, 6.9%) | 53.0 -> 50.1 (-5.5%, 8.1%) | 1.51 -> 1.44 | 1.86 -> 1.63 | 3.9 -> 5.1 | 19 -> 20 |

(One base run of the first map fell out of the map, none since.) On Tree Dungeon she lived 1.2 minutes longer a run

minute alive on `crowd`, 0.28 -> 0.36 on `crowd-slow` (standard error 0.06 each). The simulator is far from the game
there (her kills take 15-23 stars to the game's 3-4, she's touched 5 times as often, and nearly every run dies), and
seed to seed it swings by a third: the 20 seeds there don't tell a few percent either way.

The golden traces of the 19 hunt-loop scenarios were recorded again (`loop_*` on the first map, West Rocky Mountain II
and Tree Dungeon, and `feed_loop_*`); each still shows what it's there for, and the other 95 match. The slices' 23 ms
each on the simulator's clock move every look after the first one (`loop_basin` first differs at 5.255 s -> 5.278 s),
and the memory changes rule 7, the fast exit and the guards by piles; `feed_loop_basin` and `feed_loop_rival` (no
