479 -> 547, `loop_run` 1937 both), as the harness's runs above swing from seed to seed.

**Merged with positioning, the escorted trips and the capture checks** (2026-10-24): her fighting trips keep clear too
of the monsters it has where a chain goes that the look couldn't have found, last seen off its view across within 2 s
(`Navigator.remembered`, `TRIP_MEMORY_S`; Control). Taking every one it had there that the look didn't find, in view
too, the hunt loop on Tree Dungeon (`crowd`, zero-walls, the regular attack, 8 seeds of 10 minutes) was hurt 29.5 times
a run on its trips against 28.3 without, lost as much HP on them, and spent 12% longer on them; off the view only, the
runs were the same as without, seed for seed (in the simulator a chain's platforms are in view). Tree Dungeon's 14
golden traces: the 9 trips and tours match; the 5 hunt-loop ones were recorded again, `loop_tree_crowd` and
`loop_tree_walls` for the sweep's slices alone (with the sweep off they match but for the memory's line at the end),
`loop_tree_run` and `loop_tree_wait` for the memory's rules too (in `loop_tree_wait` at 42.4 s 20 isn't judged empty
while the memory holds the Horny Mushroom she's shooting, killed 0.25 s later), `loop_tree_23` for those and its seed;
`loop_tree_grind` recorded for the first time.
Two of Tree Dungeon's checks are one run's luck and went on other seeds: `loop_tree_23`'s twenty kills (met on 5 of
seeds 1-12, 4 before; seed 3), and the rope test (`test_ropes.py`), met on 5 of 12 against 10: with the sweep she
waits less (the rule 'wait' 23 times on those 12 runs against 111) for as many kills (14.2 a run against 14.0); seed 3.

**To check in game** (no keys, no attack): `watch --minutes 3 --map 10002073` with her standing on 23 or 27, then
`memory` on its telemetry file and `minimap --replay` on what it writes: rings where the monsters are, none piling up
on 19, 20 or 30, and the counts steadier than the looks'. Needs a hunt (later): the sweep's cost live (`bench-look
--live` has its row; a hunt loop's looks a second, and the touches she takes in close quarters, against the hunts of
2026-10-24), what the 'memory' records of a live hunt hold against its whole-screen looks (`eval_live.py` on it), and
whether a slice comes as often as the simulator says (the end of the hunt's log: "the sweep: N slices, held M times").

Not done: the memory doesn't learn its own numbers (how fast others take each kind, how full each platform is: the
choice of ground's belief); monsters seen while she's in the air are placed by the spawn rule and never missed; with
the feed it takes the feed's scenes as they are (a band look's band, its whole-screen searches); a kind read wrong for
a look (a Horny taken for a Green) is a new monster, and the other one is forgotten by looks; the replay doesn't have
her throws, so a monster she shot at is missed by looks as any other there.

## Grinding

`tour --loop --grind` (`behavior/grind.py`, `behavior/patrol.py`; simulator-tested only, off by default): the hunt loop
going round the map as an experienced player grinds, not hunting one ground and waiting there for its respawns. On
2026-10-24 the user played 4.5 minutes on Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II while `watch` recorded it
(`lab/telemetry/20261024-170558-watch.jsonl`) and, set against her hunts of that day, said she should hunt their way:
"this would how an experience player will grind to get EXP". By `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/compare/compare.py` (moving:
faster than 30 units/s between two looks under 2 s apart; path: |dx| + |dy| between them; kill distance: across from
her to where her kill died) and the stays as the harness counts them below (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/exp-grounds/grind/
user_moves.py`: a run of looks on one platform, one in the air or on a rope not ending it):

| | the user | one ground (14:01) | blind rotation (15:16) | positioning (16:54) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute | 484 | 283 | 204 | 215 |
| kills a minute | 14.8 | 9.2 | 6.7 | 7.2 |
| HP lost a minute (bars) | 0.33 | 0.31 | 0.33 | 0.06 |
| time moving | 59% | 43% | 42% | 31% |
| path a minute (units) | 8210 | 2020 | 1868 | 1247 |
| stays on a platform, median / longest | 1.0 / 11 s | 2.0 / 69 s | 0.8 / 29 s | 2.4 / 98 s |
| kill distance, median | 126 | 176 | 171 | 158 |

The user never camps: a circuit up the tower to the top cluster (8, 6, 9, 7) and back down (12, 16, 19, 21, 23, 24,
20), killing what's in reach on the way and closing in, for about her one-ground damage (14 of their 18 hits came while
moving, the pet drinking potions). The blind rotation lost because it travelled without fighting, to grounds others had
emptied.

**The map** (`patrol.Patrol`, once per map). Its spawn points of the kinds she hunts (`Point`: platform, x, kind, the
kind's EXP), and on each platform from which she gets at any, a stop (`Stop`): the x that reaches the most spawn EXP,
her own platform's points whole (she walks up to them), another platform's as much of it as a star reaches from there
(`spots.coverage`), less what touches there cost her while she shoots it (the danger hook's, below: `STOP_DANGER`, 0.149
of the EXP a unit of danger by the guesses that make it), so of places reaching as much, the safest rather than the
middle. With the positioning model's danger (as grinding runs, `Hunter.danger`) 3 of Tree Dungeon's 26 stops move by
it (11, 18, 22, by 20-77 units to places 0.003-0.014 safer); 23's stays at x 8, between its two Horny Mushroom points
(0.29 there, 0.15 at -147), for the Green Mushroom point on 24 a star reaches from there (positioning's own weight,
`spots.DANGER_WEIGHT`, keeps it there too); waiting at a stop she goes to the safest place along it that keeps its
spawn points in reach (the hunt loop's rule 9). Route times between stops from the planner (`Planner.arrive`): up Tree
Dungeon's tower is slow (24 to 6, 21 s by its ropes), down quick (6 to 24, 8 s). 31 points and 26 stops there.

**The belief** (`patrol.Belief`, at every look, from her memory of the monsters). Each point's monsters now or at any
time ahead (`stock`): those her memory has, each at the nearest point of its kind on its platform; else, for a point her
kill emptied or a look saw empty (a look that searched a body of its kind standing at the point: `Memory.holds_body` on
the looks' covers, `Memory.covers`), a refill clock: nothing for 5 s, then back with time constant 12.8 s times the
platform's points (`not_back`, fitted on the live respawn report: a platform's first monster back a median 13.9 s after
her kill emptied it, 0.74 not back at 8 s, 0.55 at 12.56 s), given that it wasn't back at the latest look that saw it
empty (past the 5 s the clock has no memory: a point watched empty is due 8.9 s after the latest look, however long it
has been empty); else, never seen, 0.35 (the uncamped 19-21 had a monster 31-39% of the time live). A point is emptied
once and full again once a monster is seen there after that, so another player's kill of a monster she had seen is
never read as a respawn (by the clock of an earlier miss, it had been, on 14-35% of the points emptied in the day's
replays). The clock's scale is learned from the refills she watches (a point her kill emptied, seen empty within 2 s
before the look that sees it back), against the model's mean (against its median, a world refilling as the model says
came out at 1.25), 5 refills' worth of the model. What of a point is hers (`share`): all of it, but with another
player's minimap dot by it (200 across, 100 up or down) within 20 s, what others leave of its kind (`take`), learned
from her memory: of the monsters she saw by another player, those looks forgot without a kill of hers (not those
forgotten by age: she never looked again) against those she killed or saw there 10 s after she first saw them, 0.5
counting as 4 monsters. The crowding study saw others take Green Mushrooms within about 4 s and leave Horny Mushrooms;
replayed on the user's recording it learns 0.50 and 0.33 (0.25 and 0.10 had counted every monster she passed and never
saw again as left to her).

**The loop** (`Patrol.best`): a round of 2 to 12 stops, by its EXP a minute in the long run. Going round every `cycle` s,
she catches at a point 1 / sum over k of not_back(k cycle) monsters a round (`caught`: after a kill it's back R s later,
and she kills it at her first round after that); the cycle is the round's route times, 1 s a stop, and her shooting:
0.085 s a point of EXP (Lucky Seven live on Tree Dungeon: 3.85 throws a Horny Mushroom's kill, 2.95 a Green's, a throw
every 0.84 s), learned from her throws and the EXP they gave (300 EXP's worth of the prior: with the regular attack it's
about three times as long). Less what touches cost (`HP_PRICE`, 100 EXP a bar of HP, a guess) by the danger hook. Grown
from each of the 8 stops worth the most on their own, adding the stop that raises the rate most at its cheapest place in
the round. Camping, a round of one stop (`Patrol.camping`), isn't taken up: it's logged with each loop to compare. A new
round replaces hers only when it's 15% better by the same belief and 60 s after the last change, looked for every 20 s
at her next-stop decisions: no flip-flopping. On Tree Dungeon before she has seen anything (`python -m gamelab patrol
--map 10002073`): 10, 7 (the top cluster), 463 EXP a minute, a round of 22 s (5 of travel, 15 of shooting, 8 points);
14, 10, 8, 12 461; 19-24 round by the safe platforms, 347; camping at 23, 228, where her hunt loop camping there made
283 live (with her kills on 22 and 24 from 23 and 24). Rounds that close are a coin toss: what she sees decides.

**The next stop** (`Patrol.choose`, when she leaves a stop, and every second of a trip to one): along the loop from the
last loop stop she was at, the first expected to hold a quarter of what it holds full by the time she gets there
(`SKIP_FULL`, under the 0.35 of a point never seen: an emptied stretch is skipped until it's due, an unseen one never),
or on a trip the stop she's on her way to; and a stop whose monsters she has seen (her memory's, not the belief's
guesses) pulls her there when they give 1.5 times the EXP a second of that one (each counting its travel and kills),
from one monster at even odds (with two kills' worth asked, 18 of 45 moments of the 14:01 hunt with two monsters or
more on another platform, and 22 of 34 of the user's play, went elsewhere: on 22, two seen on 19 2.6 s away at 7.1
EXP/s lost to 10, 15 s up the tower and never seen, at 2.2). What a stop is expected to give (`Patrol.expect`) counts a
remembered monster only where she gets at it from there (on its platform, or in a star's reach from somewhere along it:
`Patrol.gets`), and not one she left that stop with nothing in reach since (`left_at`): a pile on the part of a platform
no star reached had pulled her to a stop that couldn't hit it, and she bounced between the two.

**At a stop and on the way** (`grind.Grind`, the hunt loop with these in place of its grounds). At a stop she stays while
what's there gives as much EXP a second as going on would (the marginal value theorem: a patch is left when its rate
falls under the habitat's): going on gives the loop's long-run rate or, if more, the next stop's (`Patrol.choose`,
looked at every second, about 1 ms). A monster in a star's reach now is shot at once: at her kill time (0.085 s a
point of EXP) that's 11.8 EXP a second, more than any loop (Tree Dungeon's best is 7.7). Else the monsters her memory
holds, seen within 3.5 s, that she can get at from her platform (on it, stance closing in; or in reach from somewhere
along it, which she walks to: the rule 'reach'), count by their EXP over their kill time and the walk; with none, the
respawns expected in reach within 2 s (`Patrol.wait_gain`: 1-2 EXP a second at a point watched empty), so she doesn't
wait out respawns. Under it, she leaves 0.3 s after (a hit monster hides under its damage numbers: fight's hidden targets
keep it a target meanwhile); 40 s at most while nothing's in reach. The respawn rule before it (stay while a respawn in
reach is due within 2 s) had no lower bound: a respawn late by the model held her until the 40 s backstop. No better
spot (rule 7), no lull loot (8), no waiting for respawns (9 only for those 0.3 s), no rate rule, no trip back for the
piles of a stop she leaves (10). On the legs the hunt loop's rule 4 shoots what a star reaches on any platform (15 s a
platform), rule 4b finishes what she hurt, 5 and 5b pick up the piles on her platform and on the way, and every second
a pile seen elsewhere worth 1.5 times the stop she's heading to takes her there instead. After a rest the next stop is
chosen from where she rested. Kills are credited to the platform they died on (the end of the log: "kills by
platform"); the value map (`lab/grounds.json`) isn't written: its EXP a visit credits a ground with its neighbours'
kills (48% of 23's live), and a stop's visit is a few seconds.

**The danger hook** (`Patrol.danger(platform, x)`): how badly she can be touched fighting at a place, in the positioning
model's unit (`attack.danger.Danger.at`: the share of her HP touches would take there, each monster or spawn point
weighed by how soon it gets to her, Positioning), and `Grind` takes the hunter's (`Hunter.danger`), as the offline
`patrol` does; she loses 0.0175 of her HP a second for each unit of it (camping 23 she lost 0.28-0.31 bars a minute
live, 0.005 a second, where `Danger.at` is 0.285). With it the loop is 10, 7 at 468 EXP a minute (463 with the
default). The default, for a hunter without one: 0 where no monster can touch her (`MapModel.safe`), else the touch
shares of the platform's spawn points of kinds whose touch takes 5% of her HP or more (`touch_shares`: the positioning
model's, `Danger.share`, from the map's touch damage at her level: 16.1% for a Horny Mushroom, 7.2% a Green, 1.2% a
Slime, which doesn't count; no kind by name). Blended with the HP she has seen go on each platform (`hp_rate`, the hook
counting as 60 s there): a cost, never a ban.

**While looks fail their checks** (Capture checks) she stands still as the hunt loop does (`Grind` is a `Hunt`: its
LookLost pause), and grinding's clocks move on by the pause as the hunt loop's do: the stop's stay (`STOP_MAX_S`,
`LEAVE_CONFIRM_S`), her EXP a second over the run, where she'd go instead (looked at afresh), and the HP gone over it
isn't charged to a platform; the belief's refill clocks and `left_at` are the game's and ran on (`test_grind.py`: looks
failing from 20 to 35 s of `loop_tree_grind`, no plan sent in the pause, no stop left for it after).

**What it says** (the log, and telemetry 'grind'): each loop taken up ("loop: [10, 7]: 463 EXP a minute (a round 22 s:
5 s of travel, 15 of shooting; 8 spawn points, 181 EXP) (the first; camping at 7 would give 403)"), each next stop
("next: stop 23 at x 8, 1.6 s away: 77 EXP expected (77 seen), 6.8 EXP/s with its kills; pulled (stop 7's 2.5 EXP/s)",
with the stops skipped and when each is due), each stop left ("leaving stop 24: nothing left in reach (16.0 s there,
108 EXP)", or "... 4.1 EXP/s here, going on gives 7.7"), every 60 s and at the end what it did and learned: loops, stops, stays, pulls, skips, the refill scale,
her kill time, what others take, HP a minute by platform, kills by platform. `python -m gamelab patrol FILE` runs a
recorded run through her memory and the belief and prints, every few seconds, the next stop grinding would have taken
from where she was (a look, not a choice: her place in the loop doesn't move; it had rotated the loop by itself every 5 s,
10, 8, 12, 14, read as choices). On the user's recording 43 of 50 choices pull her to the monsters in view (19-24, 15
and 16 on the way down), 7 go by the loop (to 7 and 13).

**What the harness says** (Tree Dungeon, `--world zero-walls,lucky-seven`: the hunt loop as `tour --loop --skill`
throws, 10 seeds of 20 minutes, the policies `loop` at bc036b9 (base), `loop` here (the memory, half (V)), `loop-grind`
as first built (4f0401c) and `loop-grind` now; per seed in `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/exp-grounds/grind/runs` (`base-`,
`v-`, `g5-`, `fix2-10x20` and their `-slow-` twins), the moves measured as the user's above, from the runs' looks and
glances):

| `crowd` | base | memory | grinding first | grinding |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute (while alive) | 136.9 (163.4) | 137.6 (177.2) | 143.9 (178.4) | 167.2 (191.3) |
| kills a minute alive | 4.68 | 4.96 | 5.08 | 5.72 |
| HP lost a minute alive (bars) | 1.29 | 1.40 | 1.38 | 1.22 |
| rests a run, deaths | 6.0, 6 | 5.9, 9 | 6.6, 8 | 7.6, 4 |
| time moving, waiting | 45%, 1% | 46%, 1% | 44%, 0% | 42%, 2% |
| path a minute | 4011 | 4309 | 4164 | 3934 |
| stays median / longest | 1.0 / 109 s | 1.0 / 106 s | 1.0 / 105 s | 1.1 / 88 s |

| `crowd-slow` (30 s respawns) | base | memory | grinding first | grinding |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute (while alive) | 111.3 (149.2) | 118.0 (151.2) | 117.2 (155.3) | 154.7 (185.7) |
| kills a minute alive | 4.22 | 4.24 | 4.44 | 5.16 |

Seed by seed, grinding against the memory alone: +25.1% EXP a minute on `crowd` (standard error 10.7%), +35.9% on
`crowd-slow` (12.1%); while alive +13.3% (10.1%) and +25.7% (6.9%). As first built it was +7.3% (9.0%) and +2.2%
(8.0%); with the belief's and the next stop's fixes alone (pulls from one monster, stops counting only what they get
at), +8.0% (10.4%) on `crowd`, so most of the gain is the stays (left as soon as staying gives less than going on) and
the pulls on the way. It dies less (4 runs of 10 against 9): pulled to what she sees, she hunts the stops round 19-24
by the safe platforms more and climbs the tower less. (The base matches the positioning branch's own run of the same
10 seeds, 136.9.) What the simulator still can't say:
- Her world there is denser and harder than the game. Every spawn point is filled from the start and back 8.8 s (or
  30 s) after her kill, nobody hunts the tower's top or the Horny Mushrooms, so a stop or a leg always has monsters in
  reach, and a monster in reach is always worth shooting: she stays a median 15-100 s at a stop, fighting what keeps
  coming. Live a point had a monster 28% of the time, and the user moved on after a median 1 s.
- She's touched about four times as often as live (1.2 bars a minute alive, the game 0.3), so every policy goes
  through its 60 potions and rests 4-8 times a run: how long she lives weighs as much as where she hunts.
- The moves' measures don't tell the policies apart there (stays a median 1 s, 42-48% moving, 3900-5400 units a
  minute for all): knockbacks and jump throws move her as much as the patrol does.
So her simulated patrol doesn't look like the user's (long stays where monsters keep coming), where the live numbers it
plans by (the loop's 463 EXP a minute, camping 23 at 228 against 283 live) do. Judging that needs the simulator's crowd
brought to the game's (respawn delays spread about 14 s, points 28% full, the upper tower contested, touches as often as
live: the gaps in `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/wf-prior/wf_a8ab59d8-9df__understand_sim.md`), or the game.

**Merged** (2026-10-24, over positioning, the escorted trips and the capture checks; Tree Dungeon, `crowd`, zero-walls,
seed by seed against the hunt loop as merged, which positioning made much stronger there: its grounds and spots by the
danger shoot 23 from 24, where grinding's loop goes up the tower to 10 and 7):

| | the regular attack, 4 seeds of 5 min | the regular attack, 8 of 10 min | Lucky Seven, 8 of 10 min |
|---|---|---|---|
| EXP a minute, the hunt loop | 85.6 | 74.5 | 228.3 |
| ... grinding | 78.4 (-4.6%, standard error 17.2%; better on 2) | 64.6 (+0.1%, 20.7%; better on 3) | 187.5 (-12.9%, 11.2%; better on 2) |
| kills a run | 13.0 / 12.0 | 22.3 / 20.6 | 66.6 / 54.9 |
| HP lost a run (bars) | 5.8 / 6.4 | 10.7 / 10.6 | 11.2 / 9.7 |
| touches that hurt on trips | 17.0 / 10.3 | 28.3 / 21.4 | 29.1 / 29.3 |
| rests a run; deaths | 0.8 / 1.0; 0 | 2.6 / 3.3; 0 | 3.1 / 3.8; 0 |

Grinding as it was measured above (167 EXP a minute, 20-minute runs, against the memory alone's 138) makes 188 on the
merged stack, where the hunt loop makes 228: it no longer beats it in the simulator. With the regular attack (as live
now) the two are level within the runs' spread, and her kills take about three times as long as the loop's model
starts from (`KILL_S_PER_EXP` is Lucky Seven's, learned from her throws as she goes).

**To check in game.** Without attacking: `watch --minutes 5 --map 10002073` while the user plays or she stands on 23,
then `python -m gamelab patrol <its telemetry file>`: the belief's stops and the choices it would make (each pull should
name monsters in view; no stop should be skipped unless she or someone emptied it within its refill), and `memory` on it
for the mini-map. Supervised, attacking (once the user allows it): `tour --loop --grind --minutes 10 --map
10002073` from 23 or 24 (Lucky Seven but to finish and while her MP is under a cast: Attack), the user at the PC: the log's "loop:" line at the start, "next:" lines every few seconds to 30 s
(not minutes), "leaving stop N: nothing left in reach" within seconds of its last kill, never a stop held past 40 s
while nothing's in reach, kills credited across 6-10 platforms by the end, HP lost under half a bar a minute and no
rest, EXP a minute (the summary's, off her EXP number) against 283 camping and the user's 484; if she spends her time on
trips up the tower without fighting, the loop's travel is too dear and `patrol.STOP_S` or the kill time is off.

Not done: the HP price isn't fitted (100 EXP a bar is a guess) and doesn't count the trip to rest from far up the
tower; the stops are one per platform, at the place reaching the most less its danger's cost (not positioning's spots
for a ground, which can be on another platform: a stop on 24 gets at 23's points by a star anyway); a point she hasn't seen is
reached as a share of its platform (a monster she has seen, by where it is); the loop is never re-chosen in the
simulator's runs (the first one stays 15% better or the belief doesn't change enough); the learned refill scale, kill
time and takes start again each run (not saved across runs, as the value map is); grinding with the feed (the
default since 2026-10-24) is measured only in the simulator (Continuous perception: The feed by default); `nav`/`hunt`
don't grind; `tour --loop` without `--grind` still hunts one ground at a time, crediting a visit's every kill to its ground
(on Tree Dungeon, 23 alone): which is the default is the user's call, after a supervised hunt.

## Her EXP number

Her EXP number in the bottom bar, "13,549[67.02%]" (the EXP she has into her level, and its share of what the level needs), is read glyph by glyph whenever it changes (`perception/exp_number.py`), and each gain's size says what it was (`localization/gains.py`).

**How the game draws it.** At its own 1366×768, with the status bar's number pictures (`UI.wz` `StatusBar.img/gauge/number`, read by `gamedata.canvas.status_numbers`: the digits, comma, point and percent white, a digit 5×7 game px, and the brackets green), each glyph its picture's width and a blank column after the one before, the first with its top left at game px (749, 740): the bar's left (279) plus `posNumberExp` (470). The screen shows the whole picture stretched to 1920×1080 with bilinear filtering, game px (x, y)'s middle at screen ((x + 0.5) × 1920/1366 − 0.5, (y + 0.5) × 1080/768 − 0.5). Across that's 1920/1366, not the 1.40625 the minimap's markers were fitted with: drawn so over each row's background, the number on `calibrate.png` comes out within 1.9 levels of the screen at every pixel of the box read (0.30 on average), and with 1.40625 across 11.2 levels off on average (79 at most), the number half a pixel to the right. (At the minimap, the screen's left, the two differ by 0.1 px at most.)

**How it's read** (`read`). Every glyph, and a blank game column, is drawn as the game draws it at every game x the number can put one at, over the box read (`READ_BOX`: 139×16 px, from the first column past the "EXP." label's outline to the bar's right edge, two rows of plain bar above the glyphs and three below). Glyphs never share a game column (each has its blank one), so the box's screen columns fall into cells of one glyph each (a column goes to the cell of the game column its sample falls before), and a reading is a path of cells across the box: the best one, the least squared difference from the screen, is found exactly by dynamic programming. The background is each row's own (the bar's is the same along a row), its darkest tenth of columns: the text only brightens a row, and covers 59% of its columns at most in "13,549[67.02%]" (75% in "888,888[88.88%]"). A reading is kept only if:
- every cell is within `FIT`, 4 levels (root mean square over the cell), of its glyph as the game draws it: on the saved frames every cell was within 0.68 (`lie_detector_004720.png`; 0.66 and 0.65 on two others);
- every other path through each cell's first column (another glyph there, or the cells cut another way) is worse by `LEAD`, 10 levels (as a root mean square over the cell): on the saved frames the nearest was 14.1, a '[' against a ']' (16.9 a ',' against a '.', 21.6 an '8' against a '6' or a '9');
- the glyphs spell an EXP number (digits with a comma every 3 from the right and no leading zero, then the percent with two decimals in brackets) and nothing but blank cells follows it to the bar's end: a digit-like cell right after the number means one was missed;
- the percent is what the EXP is of some level's requirement, to the hundredth: the game rounds (2,613 at level 19 shows 15.67%, 15.6655). The requirements are Classic World's table from meowdb.com (`maplehelper/ocr/exp_table.py`: the game data hasn't it); its levels 19 (16,680) and 20 (20,216) fit every saved frame. Under about 1% several levels can fit (0.01% at 3 EXP fits levels 20-26): the reading says which, and her level is the one that follows the last reading.

**What it read** (`tests/test_exp_number.py`). All 32 saved 1920×1080 frames read as checked by eye on crops of each enlarged 4 times (17 values: 421 [2.52%] to 13,549 [67.02%], levels 19 and 20), and so do the 19 saved crops of the bar (16 `exp_before*`/`exp_after*` of 2026-10-19, `hud_exp`, `hud_end`, `rest_hud`), 17 of them enlarged 2 or 3 times with PIL's bicubic: re-enlarging the screen's own pixels so gives the crops back exactly, and undoing it by least squares gives the screen back to about a level. The game's glyphs drawn by OpenCV's bilinear warp (not the reader's own drawing) over the bar read right for 304 values of levels 1-60 up to 888,888. Over 11 other backgrounds (black, white, grey, the gauge's green, sky blue, gradients down and across, noise, dark noise, stripes, blobs) at full, 75% and 50% opacity, no value came out wrong in 825 tries; it read only over the plain dark ones at full opacity (and 15 of 25 over grey 128, where the brackets' green is close to grey). With a block of the cursor's or a window's colours dropped anywhere on or by the box, none of the 183 that fell on it was read, and the 17 that missed it read right. A digit after the number, the number one game px off its place, a leading zero, a comma out of place, a percent that fits no level: never read.

**Its cost:** 8.6 ms (median; p90 11.2, the CPU shared with other runs, when `Eyes.look` took 13.5 ms against 9.2 quiet), on the 17 bench frames × 5 (`bench-look`'s "EXP number read"), all 85 read. Drawing every glyph at every place takes 0.2 s, once. It's read only when 3 or more of the number's text pixels change (`hud.exp_changed`, 0.1 ms a look as before), about once a kill, so under 1 ms a second of hunting; a look keeps the box (6.7 KB) for it. While it has no value (none read yet, or after a change it couldn't read, or a jump to confirm) a look reads it anyway 0.5 s after the last try (`RETRY_S`), then twice as long after each that fails, up to 8 s (`RETRY_MAX_S`): a cursor left over the number, or a percent no level's requirement fits, costs a reading every 8 s (about 1 ms a second), where retrying every 0.5 s cost 17-20 ms a second, in the hunter and in the feed alike.

**What a gain was** (`ExpMeter`: the hunter's, from her looks, and the feed step's, from its frames, for its tracker). Each change is the number read less the last one, and across a level-up what was left of the level plus what's into the next. Its size names the kind killed: each kind's EXP per kill is the game data's (on this map a snail 2, a slime 12, a pig 13, an Orange Mushroom 15), and in the saved frames her gains were exactly those (`z2_shoot1` to `6`: 453, 466, 478, 490, 503, 516, a pig, two slimes and two pigs; `z1_hop` to `z1_after`: 451, 453, a snail). A gain that's several kills (two between two looks) is split into the fewest kills that add up to it (4 at most: `SPLIT_KILLS`), by kind when only one set of kinds does (39 is three pigs, or two slimes and an Orange Mushroom: three kills of kinds not known). A size no kills add up to (a party member's share, a quest's EXP) is logged ("no kill of this map's monsters gives that") and isn't a kill. A gain worth more than 4 of the map's best kills (60 EXP: `JUMP_KILLS`) or the number going down (a death) waits for the next look to read the same; one that doesn't agree is dropped as a misreading. A gain that's levels that don't follow (two levels up at once, or down) waits for a second reading too, and isn't counted ("EXP read at a level that doesn't follow from the last").
- *Changes it can't read* (the cursor, a window over the number) wait for the next reading to say what they were. The cursor over the number changes its pixels as it comes and as it goes, so before, a pass of the mouse over the bar was two kills; now the reading after it says no EXP came, and it's none. The changes it couldn't read come in bursts (`ALONE_S`, 0.5 s apart: a cursor moving over it changes it at every look, her kills come seconds apart), the first of them the cover coming: the reading after them is so many kills as there were bursts after the first (or with the first, if that reading is no change), split as above (`RUN_KILLS`, 12 at most), the kills at the times of the latest bursts. A burst of one change that no reading has explained 1.5 s later (`UNREAD_WAIT_S`: a cursor passing over it is gone by then) is one kill of a kind not known at its time, as every change was before; the first burst never is. So a cursor left over the number, or a level-up into a level the table has wrong (never read again), still counts the kills under it, one a change, and the reading when it goes takes the rest from its size; one counted that its size doesn't explain can't be taken back (`over`, counted). A kill in the very look the cover comes or goes in is taken with it: its EXP counts, the kill not. Before the first reading, a change is a kill as before.
- *Another EXP rate* (an event, a coupon) would make every size another kind's: at rate 2 a pig's 26 is two pigs, at 1.5 a slime's 18 four kills and a snail's 3 none, and 6 kills had counted 12-14. Each kind's EXP is the data's times `gains.EXP_RATE` (1), and the sizes are checked: of the last 8 gains of one change each (`RATE_GAINS`), fewer than 4 one kill (`RATE_OFF`) turns them off, every change one kill of a kind not known (as before, nothing split or flagged), logged with the rate the gains fit if one of 0.5-3 does ("they fit rate 2 (gains.EXP_RATE)"), and 6 of 8 (`RATE_ON`) turns them on again; until 6 of the first 8 have been one kill, a gain is named when it's one kill but not split (at rate 2, 6 kills now count 6). In the simulator every one of the harness's 3,719 gains (preset `game`, tour and loop, 10 seeds of 20 minutes) was one kill, and the runs were the same. `gains.NAME_KINDS` off is the same as the sizes off, for good: each change one kill of a kind not known, as before the number was read (its EXP a minute still from the number).

**What uses it.**
- *Her kills.* The hunter counts the kills each gain was, two in one gain as two, and none for a gain no kill explains. A fight puts each on a monster of the kind named among those she threw at (`monsters.dead_one`): before, a gain went on one of those she had thrown at, whatever its kind, and in the golden traces of `nav_fight_basin`, `nav_fight_short` and `nav_fight_up` a slime, a slime and an Orange Mushroom killed in the first seconds had been put on a pig, a snail and a pig, the live one then left alone as dying. A kill of a kind she wasn't throwing at counts by its kind, with no place. `kill()` takes a gain only of its target's kind (or one not known).
- *The ledger* (`ledger.py`) puts a kill on a monster of the kind named in her throws' paths, else leaves it unplaced (`KIND_ANY` off; on, on the likeliest of any kind). Over the three basin scenarios' seeds 1-10 (30 runs, 1,111 kills): 90% of the kills placed, against 99.9% without the kinds, and 96% of those right (kind and platform, within 2.5 s of the world's kill), against 70%: 86% of all the kills placed right, against 70%. With `KIND_ANY` on, 99.5% placed and 86% of those right (85% of all).
- *The tracker* (the feed): a gain's monster is one of the kind named. Over the tracker table's seven runs (Continuous perception), her kills judged hers went from 266 of 277, 257 right, to 283 of 299, all right; someone else's kills judged, 3 (all right, named) and 3 (2 right, named). (The runs differ: her fights go by the kinds too.) On the three tracks together: 290 of 304 hers, all right, and 7 someone else's, all right and named.
- *Her EXP a minute,* from the number itself (the EXP gained since its first reading): the hunter's and `watch`'s 'exp' telemetry record at each gain (her EXP and level, the gain and its kinds, the total, EXP a minute), on the mini-map's line and the phone page; the hunt loop's summary ("off her EXP number +812, 269.6 a minute" in `loop_basin`'s 180 s); `watch`'s line every 10 s. `hunt look` prints the number as read, with its worst cell's fit and lead.

**In the simulator** `FakeEyes` draws her number with the game's glyphs (`exp_number.draw`: the reader's own drawing, over the bar's colours, `BAR`) for level 19 at 421 EXP (`fakes.EXP_START`, the saved frames') and what she has gained, carrying each level's requirement over, and the stack reads it as in game; `Noise.exp_hidden` covers it for a while, its text pixels changing as the cover comes and goes as in game (`tests/test_gains.py`: in `hunt_clear_basin` the hunter's EXP off the number is the world's, every kill named right; covered for 18.5 s, each of the 5 kills under it is one kill of a kind not known within a look of when it came, the cover's coming and going none, and the EXP adds up; before, those two had been kills too, but the simulator's cover didn't change the pixels, so it couldn't show it). With the fights, the ledger and the tracker going by the kinds, 28 of the 86 golden traces changed, each from the first kill a fight put on another kind than before (the three above, `hunt_clear_basin`, `fight_snail_short_platform`, the tours', the hunt loop's and the feed's); with the number read but not yet its kinds, every run had been the same, the hunt loop's 12 scenarios and the feed's 5 changing only in their summaries and the feed step's counts; three scenarios no longer showed what they're there for and moved seed (each says why): `nav_fight_up_runs_out` 2 -> 6 and `nav_fight_across_runs_out` 9 -> 6 (on their seeds she now arrives), `loop_lull_loot` 4 -> 1 (no lull came; over 16 seeds 11 meet all of it, 8 before). Harness (preset `game`, 20 minutes), against the code before it (the other players' work, `exp-base-*`, 10 seeds): the hunt loop 129.2 -> 129.3 EXP a minute (+0.7% paired, standard error 1.9%; better on 6 of 10, the worst seed 13% down: 148.4 -> 129.6, 146.0 -> 146.3, 103.0 -> 101.3, 165.5 -> 161.9, 122.3 -> 130.3, 148.6 -> 143.4, 94.2 -> 101.3, 128.3 -> 133.7, 109.0 -> 113.9, 126.3 -> 130.8), drops picked up 82.6% -> 83.7%, HP lost 2.62 -> 2.61 bars; the tour 92.2 -> 90.8 (−0.8%, standard error 4.0%; better on 6 of 10: 103.2 -> 103.9, 101.3 -> 102.2, 82.8 -> 88.8, 103.0 -> 101.5, 102.7 -> 72.5, 92.3 -> 93.4, 78.6 -> 71.8, 91.0 -> 97.0, 73.1 -> 87.8, 93.8 -> 89.5; seed 5 29% down: at 45 s an Orange Mushroom killed on 12 was no longer put on a live pig, which she then fought, and from there the run went another way and killed 56 snails where it had killed 4), drops 94.7% -> 94.2%, HP lost 3.14 -> 2.82. Over 20 seeds, against the same code with `NAME_KINDS` off (on seeds 1-10 exactly the starting commit's runs): the hunt loop 130.0 -> 131.2 (+1.0%, standard error 1.3%; better on 11 of 20, the worst 13% down), drops 83.8% -> 82.7%, HP lost 2.73 -> 2.72; the tour 91.4 -> 88.9 (better on 11 of 20), seed 11 falling out of the map at 298 s (a corner of the simulator's walls, see Found offline): without it 91.3 -> 92.8 (+2.1%, standard error 2.9%), drops 94.3% -> 93.9%, HP lost 3.02 -> 2.96. None passes the bar (8 of 10 seeds better), nor what was asked of this step, the harness unchanged or better seed by seed: each run is worse on 4 of 10 seeds. `NAME_KINDS` is on against that, for what the kinds do to where her kills are put (the ledger's 70% placed right to 86%, the tracker's 257 of 277 to 283 of 299, all right): what changes is which monster each kill is put on, and from the first such kill the runs go other ways, the means within their standard errors. The game decides it (to check in game, below: the kinds the log names against what she killed, and the summary's EXP a minute against the bar's).

**To check in game:** `hunt look` (no keys) with the bar in view: the number it prints is the bar's, every glyph within about a level and every other reading 14 or more off (on the saved frames 0.68 and 14.1), and at a level other than 19 or 20 that the percent fits the table's requirement (if it doesn't, nothing is ever read: "fits no level's requirement"); the mouse over the number: not read (`hunt look`), and read again once it moves away. With `watch --minutes 2` running and her standing still (no kills), pass the mouse over the number a few times and leave it there for 10 s once: no kill counted for any of it (its telemetry's 'exp' records, and the hunter's count in a supervised `tour --loop` doing the same while she fights: every kill once, none for the mouse). In a supervised `tour --loop`: no "no kill of this map's monsters gives that" line while she hunts alone, no "aren't the game data's EXP a kill" line, and the kinds the log names are the ones she killed (a slime 12, a pig 13, a snail 2); under an EXP event or a coupon, that the line comes within 8 kills and names the rate (then `gains.EXP_RATE` to it, and the kinds are named again); the summary's EXP a minute against the bar's EXP before and after it. With another player hitting the monsters she kills, and in a party: what a shared kill gives her (a share of its EXP is taken for snails when it's 2, 4, 6 or 8). A level-up during a hunt (the next level's requirement, 21's 24,402 from the table, not seen yet).

## Her heading

Which way she faces is read off every look's frame (`perception/heading.py`, from 2026-10-24). Until then it was dead reckoning alone: `Actions.facing`, set by the arrows her plans pressed and unknown after every move (the mover's and the navigator's), so in the 12 tour telemetry files of 2026-10-24 (13:49-20:43) it was unknown on 38.6% of looks (3,438 of 8,915), 258 of 2,206 throws tapped the arrow first for want of it (80 ms each, 205 on a jump throw, and a few units walked), and anything that turned her without a key the stack sent (her player's own arrow, a plan cut short, a knock if the game turns her on one) would have left it wrong with nothing to see it: the next throw that didn't tap went the wrong way. (No such throw was found in that day's logs, which couldn't have shown one.)

**What turns her.** The game draws each of her poses facing left and mirrors it when she faces right, her name tag centred under her either way. By HeavenClient (`Character/Player.cpp`, `PlayerStates.cpp`) only arrows turn her: standing, walking, in the air with one held, prone, sitting; off a rope by a jump she faces right if right is held, else left; `Player::set_direction` is ignored while she attacks, and `Player::damage` sets the knock's speed and never calls it; landing, grabbing a rope and leaving it don't turn her (on a rope only her back shows). In 30 of the old recordings, read with the method below, every turn came 60-110 ms after an arrow went down, 50 ms taps too (`do_turn` right to left at 104 ms, `star_right` left to right at 60, `jump_throw_1` at 75; the toward-taps of `jb_k165`, `jb_k190`, `jb_k290`, `jb_k450` and `jump_back_land` in mid-air 70-80 ms after the press), and she never turned without one: through throws, climbs, ropes, the chair, pick-ups and `knockback`. Not settled: a knock with no arrow held (`knockback` holds right all through). In that day's telemetry 214 throws went out within 1.5 s of a hit with no tap: if a knock turns her, they all went the wrong way.

**How it's read** (`Heading.read`, through `Eyes.heading`). Her box above her name tag's centre, 71 to 11 px up and 23 either side at 1366×768 (50 to 8 and 16 at 1920×1080 half size), looked for 4 px (3) around where the tag puts it, is matched in colour (normalised correlation) against her pictures facing left, as it is and mirrored, the best of each over the pictures. The better way is hers when it leads by 0.08 (`SURE`) and matches 0.3 or better (`LOW`); else it isn't told ("close", "low"), nor where her tag wasn't seen (where the camera puts her), her box is off the frame or under the minimap (drawn over her), or she's on a rope (the hunter doesn't ask there). Her pictures (`REFERENCES`, 8 at most) are the medians of k-means clusters of a pool of her boxes turned to face left, so a pose after a throw, on the chair or in the air has its own; the one that matched best last is tried first, and one matching 0.4 and leading by 0.2 decides alone (`DECISIVE`).

**Where her pictures come from.** She learns them as she plays (`Heading.learn`, from the hunter): a look whose keys say beyond doubt which way she faces (`Actions.keys_say`: her last arrow 0.25 s or more before the frame, no hit since, no navigator chain playing, no frame having told otherwise) adds its box, one a second at most, 300 kept (the newest); the pictures are made again every 30 new boxes on a thread of their own (65 ms on a full pool at 1366×768; not a daemon, so a run that ends meanwhile waits for it) and kept with the pool in `lab/heading_1366x768.npz` (`lab/heading.npz` at 1920×1080), as her name tag is, with the window size and box they're for. The file is written whole and then put in its place (`os.replace`: a run cut off leaves the last whole one; refused while a reader holds it, the next save tries again); one that can't be read, or is of another box than `heading.py`'s, is set aside with a line in the log and the seed stands in until the next save writes it anew; pictures a thread made from a pool dropped meanwhile are dropped too. A look the pictures read the other way from such keys isn't learnt, and 12 such of the last 20 drop the pool (a new outfit: `OUTFIT`). Until she has pictures of her own the seed stands in (`gamelab/assets/heading_1366x768.npz`, in git): 16 frames at 1366×768 in her outfit of 2026-10-24, 4 pictures, all of her standing still (Tree Dungeon's `map3/native`, `lab/shots/hp0_*`: hit, on her chair, and `nm_*`: by the portal), her tag where the 2026-10-24 study put it (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/heading/explore/tags.json`), made by `python -m gamelab heading --seed --fresh --tags TAGS --learn left FRAMES` and `--seed --tags TAGS --learn right FRAMES`. West Rocky Mountain II's four (`newmap/native/n28_*`) are left out, for the seed's test to read a scene it wasn't made from; and `nm_pet7`, whose "tag" is the Character Info window's label under her portrait (the first seed had two pictures of that portrait: a look's search for her tag took the window's "BugBaby" on `nm_pet1` and `nm_pet7`, which is why `--tags` is there). `python -m gamelab heading FRAMES` reads saved frames with her pictures (lab's, else the seed); `--learn` without `--seed` teaches lab's, `--fresh` drops them first.

**What it read** (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/heading/eval`: `eval_old.py`, `eval_native.py`, `attack_margins.py`; `tests/test_heading.py` on crops of the same frames, `tests/fixtures/heading`):
- The old recordings (the first map, 1920×1080 looks at half size, her outfit of 2026-10-18 to 21): 1,249 frames whose facing is known, 788 by the keys of 19 recordings (walks, the jump backs' turns in mid-air, taps, throws, `knockback`, chains; frames 130 ms or more after the last arrow) and 461 of 13 recordings with none pressed (throws, Lucky Seven, the chair, pick-ups), each recording read with pictures learnt from the others: 1,232 told, every one right, and 17 not told (1.4%); the right way led by 0.29 or more on 95% of them. 131 more were of her under the minimap, where 4 had been read the wrong way (`walk_snail`). With one picture (the pool's median) 15.9% weren't told and 10 frames of throws were read the wrong way.
- With pictures learnt from walks and jumps alone (none of a throw), 9 of the 426 frames of throws told were the wrong way, all one pose matching 0.31, 0.24 ahead. So while her attack plays (until `Actions.arrow_until`, 0.8 s after its key) a frame counts only matching 0.4 and leading by 0.2 (`Reading.strong`): 377 of the 461 do, none of the 9.
- 20 frames at 1366×768 in her outfit of 2026-10-24 (15 facing left, 5 right), of 5 scenes, all of her standing still: West Rocky Mountain II's chair (`n28_*`, 4), Tree Dungeon's (3), hit on her chair (`hp0_1353*`, 5), hit (`hp0_1648*`, 2) and by the portal, behind its glow (`nm_*`, 6). Each scene read with pictures of the other four (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/heading/fix/scenes.py`): 19 right, 15 of them strongly, one not told (`hp0_135320`, neither way like her: 0.21), none wrong; by scene, 4 of 4, 3 of 3, 4 of 5, 2 of 2, 6 of 6. (A frame's mirror image only swaps the two ways' scores, so it adds nothing to these; frames of one scene are near alike, so none is read with pictures of its own scene.) The seed reads every one of West Rocky Mountain II's 30 frames, and their mirror images, right and strongly. On `nm_equip`, `nm_keys` and `nm_verify` the labels by eye had been wrong and the reading right: her eye patch is on the right there.
- Not measured at 1366×768: walking, jumping and throwing, of which no frames were saved at that size, nor her standing anywhere but those 5 places. Where the frame and her keys disagree a crop is kept (below), and her pictures learn those poses as she plays. Until then the seed may read them weakly or not at all; a weak reading can't take a throw's tap away (below), and a pose the seed reads the wrong way isn't learnt (`learn` takes no box its pictures read the other way) unless 12 of 20 such looks drop the pool.

**Its cost:** 0.2 ms a read at half size (p95 0.5), where every one of 8 pictures each time took 1.4 ms; at 1366×768 with the seed (4 pictures) 0.4 ms (p95 1.5, at most 4.2) over the 2026-10-24 study's 92 frames, each read 20 times on one thread with the live hunt running on the same PC (`D:/MapleHelper-scratch/heading/fix2/cost.py`).

**What uses it** (`Actions.see`, from the hunter's `_heading` after every look). A frame sure of her facing is her `heading` unless it can't show what her last arrow did (before it and `HEADING_SETTLE_S`, 0.2 s, after: `hold_back` keeps when every plan's last arrow goes), a navigator chain is playing (its arrows go out around `play`; frames count again once the navigator says it's over by setting `facing`), or her attack plays and it isn't strong. One that can't tell leaves it, but after a hit since it was last set makes it unknown. Whether a throw taps the arrow to turn her first goes by `heading` (`Actions.faces`, `_turn`): after a move, a knock, a chain cut short or her player's own keys it's the frame's, where it was unknown or wrong; unknown, the throw taps (the safe choice). A frame takes a tap away only where it's strong (`Reading.strong`: matching 0.4, 0.2 ahead), and one that isn't changes her heading only where it tells otherwise, so it can add a tap but never take one away: none of the frames read the wrong way above was strong, and a star the wrong way costs a throw where a tap costs 80 ms. With her own pictures that costs little: on the old recordings 787 of the 788 keys' frames told right were strong, and 418 of the 444 frames of her attack. The decisions (`attack.stance`: the side she keeps working, the close monster she jumps away from; `behavior.probe`'s stand) go by `facing`, her keys', as before: with them going by the frame's too (the first build of this), which knows her facing after a move where her keys don't, so she kept working the side she faced after every trip, the simulator's hunt loop made 12% less EXP a minute than with her keys' (paired over 24 seeds, standard error 6.5%) and the tour 3% (3.0%). A throw sets `facing` to its way, as before; one that taps counts in `Actions.turns` and `turn_ms`.

**What's logged.** Each scene (telemetry) carries her `facing` (the heading, which the mini-map's arrow and reach colours show), `facing_by` ("frame" or "keys"), `facing_keys`, `facing_seen` (the frame's alone) and `facing_strong` (whether that frame was strong enough to take a throw's tap away; `watch --heading`'s scenes carry the last two). A frame telling the other way from her last arrow is an event 'heading' (once an arrow) with how long after the arrow and the last hit it came, logged ("the frame shows her facing left, her last arrow right (1.8 s after it, no hit before; matched 0.60, 0.40 ahead of right)"), and her crop is kept in `lab/heading` (200 at most), for a look by eye. The hunter counts what came of every look's reading (`heading_counts`). In live runs the events say how often something turns her without a key, and a short `since_hit` says it was a knock.

**In the simulator** `FakeEyes` reads the world's facing (`Screen.heading`, as matching 0.6 and 0.4 ahead), not on a rope, nor on `Noise.heading_unsure`'s share of frames (preset `game`: 1.4%, as on the old recordings), and the wrong way on `Noise.heading_wrong`'s (share, score, margin, and whether in runs of one pose of hers for a second, `HEADING_SPELL_S`, or frame by frame). `Noise.turned` turns her from outside at given times; `Realism.knock_turns` has a touch's knock turn her to face the monster (not the game's, by HeavenClient; harness `--world knock-turns`). The world counts the attacks that go the other way from where the stack threw them (`World.wrong_way`, by her facing as each starts: the Sim hands it each throw's way and its key's time), and the harness records her throws, those that tapped first, those attacks and the frames against her last arrow (`turning`); `--world heading-wrong` reads 1% of her frames the wrong way in runs, as weakly as every frame read the wrong way above (0.31, 0.24 ahead), and `heading-wrong-sure` as surely as the right ones (0.6, 0.4: the worst case).
- `tests/test_heading.py`: on Tree Dungeon, turned away twice while she shoots a Horny Mushroom, only the two throws decided before each turn (it came while they waited out her attack lock) go the wrong way and the second turn is logged, where eyes that can't read her send 5 the wrong way; in a world where knocks turn her, over seeds 1-8, 4 attacks go the wrong way for 10 knocks that turned her, against 16 for 19 without the frame; facing away from the mushroom with every frame read the wrong way, weakly, she kills it with no attack the wrong way, where with the frames strong, or with a weak frame let take a tap away, every one goes the wrong way.

Elsewhere `nav_fight_up_runs_out` (seed 4), `tour_hunt_basin` (5), `tour_hunt_other_spot` (the other pig at 1260), `loop_tree_lie_detector` (7) and `loop_tree_lie_detector_28` (2: her course before the sighting goes otherwise) moved, each explained there, and `tests/test_gains.py`'s cover, `test_sim.py`'s jumps onto 14 after a kill and `test_respawn.py`'s hunt with the feed (75 s, not 45) say what they show now.

Per run, its means (HP lost in bars of her HP; "in taps": what the taps added to her throws, 80 ms each, 205 on a jump throw). In the game's world her keys were never wrong, so all the frame can do there is save the taps where she already faced the target: 26% of them in the loop, 21% in the tour, 2 s of every 10 minutes; the EXP a minute moved within the harness's noise on this map, down in the loop and up in the tour. (The one attack the wrong way in each is a jump back whose turn in mid-air came after its attack started, its star still hitting the mushroom 4 units off: on both sides.) Where a knock turns her (`--world knock-turns`; for these runs the same knock patched into both sides' worlds), half her attacks that went the wrong way go the right way; those left were decided before a knock (it came while they waited out her attack lock), and her EXP a minute is as it was: the collapse 8 seeds showed before the rebase (seeds 4, 5 and 8 at a third of master's) isn't there on 24 (64.9, 101.1 and 87.1 against 74.8, 90.3 and 69.2), and the stretches of a minute or more without a kill are rests on 25 on both sides (every seed with any, 12 at master, 13 here). Frames read the wrong way as weakly as every one read the wrong way on 2026-10-24 cost nothing: a weak frame can add a tap but never take one away (`Actions.faces`), and the taps they add are 1 in 40. As surely as the right ones (which none of those was), 1% of frames the wrong way send 0.5-0.8 attacks the wrong way every 10 minutes, against 15-16 taps saved.
- What the frames came to on seeds 1-8 of the game's world (loop and tour, 160 minutes; `D:/MapleHelper-scratch/heading/fix2/counts.py`): 98.5% of her 14,490 looks told her facing (195 "close", 21 on a rope: `Noise.heading_unsure` and the ropes); her keys took 1,012 of them as told otherwise (the frame knowing after a move what her keys didn't), let 1,399 by as too soon after an arrow, 22 as of a chain playing and 123 as of her attack playing; her heading went unknown after a hit 4 times in all (the next throw tapping, 80-205 ms); and 842 throws tapped first, 106.1 s in all, of 5,981 attacks, none the wrong way.

**To check in game.**
- *No attacks:* `python -m gamelab watch --heading --minutes 3` with her standing where she can turn (no monster near), the game at 1366×768: turn her with the arrows by hand, now and then on her chair and back. Every turn should be said within a frame or two ("her frame: faces left (matched 0.62, 0.40 ahead)"), the status line's "her facing told on N of M frames" near all of them, and nothing said while she stands still. The seed has her standing, on the chair and hit; walking, jumping and in the air may be "can't tell (close)" until she has pictures of her own from a hunt. If she's said the wrong way standing still, her outfit isn't the seed's: `heading --fresh` on frames of her now, or let a hunt teach her pictures.
- *In a hunt, later* (an attack test: when they're allowed again): a supervised `tour --loop` of 10 minutes or more. In its telemetry: the scenes' `facing_by` "frame" after moves and `facing_seen` told on most looks, and `facing_strong` on most of those once her pictures are her own (until then the seed's weak readings keep the taps: a weak frame can't take one away); the 'heading' events and `lab/heading`'s crops, how often the frame told against her last arrow and how soon after a hit (a crop the wrong way is a reading to fix; one the right way is a turn without a key, and with a short `since_hit`, a knock that turns her); `lab/heading_1366x768.npz` written within the first minutes (her pictures, learnt); fewer throws that tap first than the day's 12% (258 of 2,206), and none thrown away from its target.

## Motion planning

| Module | Does |
|---|---|
| `body.py` | How the game moves her: the one movement engine. One step on the ground and in the air (`ground_step`, `air_step`), the jump's kick (`takeoff`), the game's 30 ms step on which it acts on the keys, sets her speed on the ground and lifts her off (`STEP`; landing between two, she waits for the next: `LANDING_ENDS_STEP`), the lags (`Lags`: on average, the wait for the step included), closed forms on flat ground for planning, the mean over where the steps fall (`run_x`, `run_time`, `stop_distance`, `hold_for`), the chance a jump from standing leaves short (`short_takeoff_chance`, `jump_window_ok`, `blind_ends_ms`), flights over a map (`fly`, with the arrow held and her speed as she lands) and a key timeline replayed on flat ground, at any phase of the steps (`replay`). From the game's `Physics.img` and her stats (`config.toml` `[character]`); see below. |
| `physics.py` | Her jump (`jumpSpeed` 555, gravity 2000: 77 units high, 555 ms in the air) and fall, and where a jump or fall lands, flown by the engine (`body.fly`, as the simulator flies her) against the map's platforms and the walls of the foothold group she leaves (`group`: below), with the arrow held the way she leaves and her sideways speed as she lands (`flight`). Her movement, from the engine (`body.py`, below) with the median lags: walking at 149 units/s (`WALK_SPEED`, 125 × SPEED/100), a standing start 119 ms behind (`RUN_LAG`: the arrow acted on 66 ms after the key, then 1400 units/s² up to full speed), a jump lifting off 65 ms after its key (`JUMP_LAG`), 24.0 units of slide after the arrow is let go, standing still 0.25 s later (`STOP_RUN`, `STOP_S`), 13.9 on landing with no arrow (`LAND_SLIDE`), how long to hold an arrow to walk a given distance (`hold_ms`, the engine's over the game's steps: 46 ms for 5 units and 104 for 20; 39 and 90 on the 5 ms engine, where the old straight line, 8.5 + 0.149 t, held 30 and 77), and when to press jump on a run from standing (`run_ms`, `sure_jump_ms`: never in the blind spot, so a run-up under about 14 units is keyed 150 ms in (`BLIND_ENDS_MS`, from the jump sweeps: below) and lifts her off 14 units along at full speed; 40-52 ms after the arrow leaves at full speed by the kick most of the time, not reliably enough to plan on, `KICK_JUMPS`, off). A route jump has to land on its platform at 0.85 × 149 too (`SLOW_TAKEOFF`: a takeoff three of the game's steps into a run, 126, is 0.846 × 149); every one on this map already does. When an attack from a jump comes (`JUMP_ATTACK_MS`, `JUMP_BACK_ATTACK_MS`: the key plans use them, and `attack.reach` works out from them how high she is when the attack starts, with `jump_rise`). Landings are cached, the 20,000 last used (`FLY_CACHE_SIZE`, about 9 MB when full; the planner's own jumps and falls on this map are 7,973 of them, about 3.6 MB, with the ropes in mid-air (5,726 since its jumps' takeoffs are placed for room, 3,128 before, 1.4 MB, and the 8-minute `loop_run` added none); 2,718 when the cache was bounded, before the foothold groups and the walk-offs' room rule). Each is worked out from its start rounded as its key is (to 0.01 units, and 0.1 units/s), so what is cached never changes an answer. Worked out from the exact start, a landing cached by one call went to every later one that rounded the same: the simulator's scenarios each had to start from the same cache to give the same trace. A landing not cached is flown by the engine's step in C (`gamelab/native.c`: `body.fly` as `flight` calls it, the arrow's steering and the air's drag, the fall cap, the walls of her group, a wall at her start, the corner landing, the same arithmetic in the same order) when it's built: 0.019 ms a flight instead of 0.85-0.95 in Python on this map, with the same platform, x, time and speed at landing to the bit and the type on 172,000 random flights on this map and the tests' (with and without arrows, by every wall and by group, 48,000 of them aimed through a platform's corner; tests compare 6,000). Building the map's moves, which every hunting command does as it starts, takes 0.25 s instead of 3.3 (204 moves, 7,973 landings flown; 0.07 s for the 226 moves before the takeoffs were placed for room and the ropes in mid-air), and in the simulator's tours (`sim.latency`, 5 seeds of 10 minutes) the stack's computing before a plan went out, a chain planned on the move among it, was at most 6.1 ms instead of 164 (the 99th percentile 2.2 instead of 5.5), the same hunts. The C is built with MSVC 2022's Build Tools the first time it's needed, into `gamelab/build/` (git-ignored; `python -m gamelab.native` builds it now), and without them it's all done in Python as before. The elevated key player never loads it. |
| `route.py` | Every move the physics allows: rope climbs and climb-downs (and a running jump at a rope hanging just past a platform's end, `_past_end`), jumps off a rope sideways onto a platform or onto another rope and up it (`rope_jump`, `side_jump`: the mover does them, closed loop), jumping up through a platform overhead, running jumps (tried from every 10 units along each platform: every 20 missed the 30-unit window of takeoffs from platform 4 up onto 2, so 2 had no route), walking off an end and dropping through (↓ + jump; not where the foothold forbids it), from the middle of where her platform overlaps the one she lands on, and never within 70 units of the basin's cliffs (`route.KNOCK_CARRY`, `void_ends`: 36's ends, the only ones here with nothing under them, which a knock as she comes down from another block can carry her through; Found offline and Fixed since). Up to 3 takeoffs are kept per landing platform. A jump is kept only if it still lands on the same platform when she leaves the ground 12 units early or late, or 10% slower: a jump meant for the basin once landed on a platform whose edge its arc passed close by. Each then takes off where it lands with the most room, near where it was found (`_placed`): up a staircase step 15 units from the end, not 33, so that taking off flow's 10 units earlier, or 12 early or late, still lands 24 and 12 in (Found offline: Jumps up a step taking off too early), its run-up from 20 units before it (`RUN_UP`). A jump off an end is looked for from 33 units back (`JUMP_FROM`) and, if none surely lands from there, a unit nearer at a time down to 15 (`END_LOOK`): on West Rocky Mountain II five land only from the last 15-33 units of a platform, which weren't found (17 onto 16, 64 up and 25 across, the way up the steps on the right: from 17 to 10 in 3.4 s now, 22.2 before, round by 22 and the rope to 14); none on the first map. A jump off an end and one found along the platform that take off at the same spot are one move. Walking off wins over a running jump down to the platform it reaches anyway. A walk-off is kept only if she can stop where it lands: she comes down at full speed, so her slide (13.9 units) and 9 more must fit before the landing platform's far end. A twin (Localization) is the platform it lies on: no move goes from or to one. Where the sure moves leave places with no way back to the rest of the map (a trap), a running jump out that lands only from a short stretch of takeoffs is kept (`_ways_out`: takeoffs tried every unit at full speed, the jump taken from the middle of the longest stretch landing on a place with a way back, its chance that she leaves the ground within half of it, her takeoff off by 5.5 units (sd: half a minimap pixel of the game's from her dot alone, the game's step and the lags); kept if over 0.5, and costing its misses at 3 s each): on West Rocky Mountain II the jump from 27 onto 25 out of the pit of stairs, 0.90; none on the first map, where every place gets to every other. Off platform 10's right end she came down 10 units from 15's end and slid off it onto 18, every time in the simulator. An end with a cliff dropping from it (platform 34 down to 36) can be walked off; the simulation used to start just inside the end and bump into the cliff's top, which left out 42 of the map's 63 walk-offs. Dijkstra finds the quickest route; a move's cost includes a small allowance for misses, measured on chained moves. With `route.ROUTES` over 1 (`--ways`) a trip chooses among that many distinct ways instead, the best route through each move, by a softmax over their times (`alternatives`, `through`, `chances`: Route diversity). How long the trip to each platform takes from where she is (`travel`, for the hunt loop's and the tour's choices) comes from one search to every platform (`times`: 1.6 ms, against 11 for a search to each of the 16 platforms monsters spawn on, the same seconds), kept for the last 256 places she was asked about. How long to stand at an x on a platform (`arrive`, for the hunt loop's piles on the way) is the quickest of the routes landing anywhere on it with the walk along it from where each lands, from the same search with every landing kept (about 210 on this map), kept as `times`' are. |
| `bodies.py` | What a monster touches her by, for the planner's touches (`Flow.danger`) and the danger of a place (`attack/danger.py`, a fight's escape and a wait's walk): her box, one definition for the stack, the v83 client's for her standing, 22 either side of her feet and 65 tall (`HER_HALF`, `HER_TALL`: RustMS's port of the decompiled client, `MobAttackRules.h` `user_body`, from FUN_0045183b; her drawn sprites agree, standing -22 to 13 and 64 tall; HeavenClient's own test takes her as a line 50 tall, and the simulator's world has her 15 by 55, the client's box with `--world client-body`; the hop stays timed with the 15 it was checked with in game, `flow.HOP_HALF`), against a monster's, the widest and tallest of its kind's frames (`localization.monsters.touch_box`: a Horny or Green Mushroom 28 either side and 56 tall as it flinches, a pig 34 and 54; the first map's tables for their kinds), overlapping strictly as the client's rectangles do (`touches`); and her feet along a flight (`Arc`: gravity, the fall cap, never under its landing on the way down) or a climb (`Climb`). Nothing logged tells 50, 55 and 65 apart (a MISS leaves no trace); the one touch on a rope, climbing 11 -> 10 on Tree Dungeon, came with her feet 41-58 under 10's surface by her dot. |
| `flow.py` | Turns several route moves into one continuous key timeline, predicted with the game's physics and her measured movement. She keeps running through landings, jumps on the way, and grabs ropes on the run. Each move has a few variants (keep running on landing, land and stop, jump straight up from anywhere under the platform above, or step back for a run-up), and the chain picks the variant the next move can follow on from. Each leg keeps the whole way her feet go, her runs, her flights (`bodies.Arc`), her climbs (`bodies.Climb`) and where she stands meanwhile (a jump's lift-off, lying down to drop, a landing's slide, a rope's climb-over), and `Flow.danger` checks her body's box against each monster's all along it, every 0.05 s, wherever the monster can be by then on its own platform, whichever that is (`bodies.py`, below); it used to check her runs alone, against the monsters of the platform she ran on, 15 units either side of her. A monster seen walking turns back at its platform's end (`MonsterTrack.at`), where it used to stop. A monster in the way of a run is hopped over: by the body sizes in the game data her jump can't clear a pig or slime outright, so the hop is timed so she can't touch it on the way up, and any contact comes past its middle, where the knockback pushes her on. Timed this way, 7 hops over monsters standing or walking at her took no damage. Monsters walking away from her aren't hopped: she closes on them too slowly. Climbing over a rope's top is an animation of about 0.35 s; the next move's arrow goes down just before the top if it starts a run, and when the plan has her standing there if it's a step (held 250 ms or less, `STEP_EARLY_MAX_MS`: pressed early, the step back for the jump 32 -> 29 went too far in game, and the jumps after it came down in the basin). A running jump takes off only where it lands with room to spare from where she really leaves the ground (`Flow.sure_takeoff`: 9 units in from the ends of the platform it lands on, and still on it if she leaves 8 units early or late, 10% slower or at 0.85 × 149, `TAKEOFF_SLOP`), else from another of its takeoffs (every 2 units, nearest the one picked first); a hop lands so on her own platform. Pick-up is pressed every 0.15 s through every run. Her body in a plan has a speed along the ground, and it is moved by the engine's closed forms (`body.py`, with the median lags): a standing start, a run from whatever speed she lands with, a release a stop's length early, a step from standing held as long as `hold_for` says (one under 8 units isn't taken), each jump's takeoff speed by the kick (passed to `physics.flight`), and a stop on landing that slides from the landing speed. |

**How the game moves her** (`body.py`; `python -m gamelab.analysis.physics_check` replays the recordings through it). The constants are the game's own, in its `Physics.img` (forces over a mass of 100), scaled by her SPEED and JUMP stats (`config.toml` `[character]`: 119.2 and 100, from what's measured; her stat window would say exactly). How the client applies them step by step comes from a decompile of an older client (v83, ported by the RustMS project). That is secondhand, so it's kept where the recordings agree:
- **An arrow speeds her up; it doesn't switch her to full speed.** From standing she speeds up at 1400 units/s² (walkForce 140000) to 149 (walkSpeed 125 × 1.192): full speed 0.106 s and 7.9 units after the game acts on the arrow, 66 ms after the key on average. Five standing starts at 60 fps fit 1400 (free fits 950-1500), and so do the 18 starts of the jump sweeps, step by step (below). Let go, she brakes at 800 (walkDrag), from 68 ms after the key comes up on average: 24.0 units on from full speed (18 stops at 30 fps: 24.0 ± 2.0; seven at 60 fps before: 23.4-28.1), 13.9 of them braking, which is also her slide on landing.
- **The jump's kick.** Leaving the ground with an arrow held, below 0.8 × 149 that way she gets 0.8 × 149 more, up to 149. On the game's steps (below) she runs 42, 84, 126 and 149 units/s after one to four steps of the arrow, so a jump lifted off by the arrow's own step leaves at 119, one after one or two steps at full speed (the kick), one after three at 126 (too fast for the kick, short of full speed), one after four or more at full speed. A flat jump at 126 lands 13 units short. The jump-backs keyed it 80 ms after the arrow: of the five recorded at 60 fps, three left at 121-127 units/s, one at 142 (the arrow seen late, so it kicked) and one at 146. `body.short_takeoff_chance` gives the chance that a jump keyed a given gap after the arrow leaves short (the steps between the arrow's and the jump's are k or k + 1 as the steps fall, spread by the lags), `jump_window_ok` which gaps are safe, and no key plan jumps in between (below).
- **In the air** the arrow she runs with does nothing (it only steers toward 8.9 units/s), and the opposite one brakes her at 200 units/s² (five 40 ms holds in the jump-backs: -4 to -15 units/s). With no arrow the air drags at 1 unit/s², or 100 once she falls at the cap (670), so a long fall with the arrow let go lands shorter: 17 units over a 500-unit drop.
- **In the air only walls of her own foothold group stop her** (`body.OWN_GROUP_WALLS`): the group of the foothold she last stood on, kept through a jump or a fall through her platform (off a rope, none). Floors of every group land her. A reconstruction of the client's code has its airborne collision skip the walls of every other group (ThatMapleDev's "Player Physics" gist), and the live runs agree. Six flights in the telemetry of 2026-10-19 and 20 went across another group's wall (15-37 units inside its span; one just under the floor of the block it hangs from), and each came down within 13 units of where the rule puts it (her minimap dot fitted to the engine's arcs), against 54-142 units off, or on another platform, with every wall stopping her:
  - three walk-offs off platform 3's right end, planned for the basin, went through platform 1's wall 12 units on and landed on 17, at 1,005-1,015 (the rule: 1,004-1,008; stopped by the wall: the basin, or 869 round its foot);
  - two jumps from 19 that came in under 18's floor went through its wall onto 24, at 198 (the rule: 185-198; stopped: 140-144);
  - one from 22 went through 21's wall onto 34, at -138 (the rule: -138; stopped: -56).

  None was seen stopped by one. The planner's moves go from 224 to 226. walk_off 3->17 (1.4 s) replaces walk_off 3->36 (2.7 s). Running jumps 4->3 (a second takeoff), 13->20, 19->24 and 18->32 are new, and the walk-offs 13->20 and 19->24 are gone: through the wall they come down 10 units from the far end at full speed, with no room to stop. Six jumps from along a platform can take off 20 units nearer the end now: their arc goes up through the wall hanging under the next block's end, where it used to bump into it. In the simulator (`sim/world.py`) her group goes with her, and of the 2,559 flights in the 62 scenarios 19 land further on, 2 of them on another platform: a knock off 18 carried her through 16's wall onto 32, not down it into the basin. Of the golden traces 16 changed and one was replaced. Trips without monsters make the same moves, 0-0.1 s apart, but for `nav_drops`, which with its route weights drawn anew drops 2->12->21 again, 1.4 s sooner. `move_walk_off_long_fall` (off 3 for the basin, 1,500 units down, with `Mover.settle` giving up on her still falling) is `move_walk_off_through_wall` now: no move on this map falls that long. Harness, preset `game`, 20 seeds of 20 minutes: the hunt loop 120.5 -> 123.3 EXP a minute (11 seeds the same, 7 better, 2 worse), drops 86.9% -> 88.1%; the tour 88.9 -> 88.6 (9 better, 10 worse), drops 93.5% -> 91.7%, HP lost 2.66 -> 2.52 bars.
- **A step lands her if it crosses a floor's surface by either end's reading** (`body._lands_at`, and `native.c`'s twin): from at or above the surface under where the 5 ms step starts, or under where it ends, to at or below it where it ends. HeavenClient (`FootholdTree::limit_movement`, read 2026-10-24) judges a falling step by the start's height against the surface under the start (`crnt_y <= ground(crnt_x) && next_y >= ground(next_x)`); the engine had weighed it against the surface under the end alone, so a step onto a floor rising the way she flew, starting above the surface under her but less than the rise below the surface at its end, went through. On West Rocky Mountain II the jump from 13 up onto 10 (rising 0.15 a unit) went through on 3 of 6 seeds; on Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II, whose floors slope 0.1 a unit, she came down through the floor itself and out of the map (In game, there). No landing of before is lost: the first map's moves and traces are as they were; West Rocky Mountain II's walk-off off 8's left end lands on 10 now, not through it onto 17, and its graph has 179 moves (181).
- **A rope beyond a sure grab** (`route.GRAB_CHANCE`): a rope whose bottom is above `physics.GRAB_SURE` (82: HeavenClient grabs one when a point 5 above her feet is on it, `Ladder::inrange`, at the top of her 77-unit jump) but within `ROPE_REACH` (95, a guess: the highest grab tried in game was 69 up) may or may not be grabbed; its climb is kept as landing half the time, each miss 3 s, and so is a rope jump that starts by grabbing it from below (none on Tree Dungeon, Forest Up North II, which has no rope jumps). A jump at a rope past a platform's end is kept only if she grabs it with her feet at its bottom taking off on time, a sure grab by either reach. One climb on the three maps: Tree Dungeon's from 30 up the rope at -8 (94.3), which the ways up now go round by 29.
- **Lags,** from a key going out to the game acting on it, on average (medians of the recordings, the wait for the game's next step included): an arrow pressed 66 ms, let go 68 ms, a jump 65 ms. The jump sweeps' 18 jumps at 30 fps had 66, 68 (from how far she went on) and 64, with sd 12, 13 and 11; the 60 fps recordings before, 60, 72 and 67 (10 starts, 7 stops, 18 jumps). The jump's 65 is between its two sets, and follows both sets' arcs best. The engine has the game see each key half a step sooner (`Lags.seen`), spread by 7.7 ms, and act at its next step: the wait spreads it by 8.7 ms more (30/√12), which makes the 11-13 measured. So a hold is seen 2 ms longer than it is, a whole number of steps one way or the other: an arrow held t ms from standing moves her, on average over the steps, along the line between 1.8, 7.0, 15.7 and 23.7 units (one to four steps of it), and 6.2 + 0.149 t from 118 ms on. One tap at a time it's one or the other: a 50 ms tap moves her 1.8 or 7.0 units; of three 50 ms taps in game, two moved her 0 and one 7.1.
- **The step is 30 ms** (`body.STEP`). The game acts on what it has seen of the keys, sets her speed on the ground and lifts her off once every 30 ms. In the jump sweeps (below) her run when she lifted off sat in five tight groups, 0.45-0.96, 2.34-2.79, 5.19-6.54, 10.09-10.68 and 14.04-15.23 units, which are one to five steps of 30 ms at 1400 units/s² (0.63, 2.52, 5.67, 9.80, 14.27), nothing between them; and every jump that lifted off three steps after the arrow left at 124.7-126.9 units/s (five of them: 3 × 42), the only speed below full one left with from a run. With a 5 ms step those would spread over 119-149 by when the jump came (the 5 ms engine, given each jump's own lags, had three of the five leave at 133, 133 and 149, landing 4-14 units off); the game's 16.7 ms frame has 117 and 140 there, and is ruled out too. Between its steps she moves evenly, as the frames at 60 fps show her. In the air the engine still flies 5 ms steps: the arc is the same at the game's steps, and the planner's landings by platform edges stay as they were. Landing between two steps, she stands where she lands until the next (`body.LANDING_ENDS_STEP`): between landing and letting go of the arrow she ran 3.3 ± 0.8 units less than the 5 ms engine had her in all 18 jumps of the sweeps (1.6-4.8), which this makes 2.2 on average (it ends the sweeps' runs 0.65 ± 1.9 units on from where she stopped, against 2.6 running on at once); the 60 fps recordings that land and run on fit it as well either way (two better, two worse). The planner's run on from a landing starts half a step later (`body.landing_wait`). `replay` takes the steps' phase against the keys (`phase`); the closed forms are the mean over it, and `physics_check` compares that mean (or, with `--fit`, the phase fitted with the lags).
- **Fit:** with these fixed lags the engine (its path the mean over the steps' phase) follows the 18 tracked recordings at 60 fps to 1.94 units RMS in x and 1.67 in y (medians 1.61 and 1.17), without `do_ledge2` 1.82 and 1.57; with lags and the phase fitted to each, 0.89 and 0.75. The gain over the 5 ms engine below is the jump-backs keyed 80 ms after the arrow, 0.9-1.7 in x where they were 4.3-5.6: on the steps they are kicked or not by where the steps fall, half and half, where the median lags had them all go one way. The sweeps' 18 jumps at 30 fps, each with its own lags, leave as fast as she did to 4 units/s and land within 2 units of where she did (`tests/test_recordings_physics.py`, with the sweeps as a fixture).
