**The kernel's page zeroing** ("System" in Task Manager, which the table above leaves out). Each fresh page a process touches is a page fault, and Windows hands it a page zeroed by its zero-page threads (12 threads of the System process, pid 4, at priority 0, one start address: `review-numbers/systhreads.py`). Over 43 minutes with the game closed (gamelab at 0.02 cores), System was 6.5 cores on average and 11 at the peak, following the page faults (r = 0.91 over 12 windows of 5 s, about a core for 100,000 faults a second: `corr.py`; a controlled test, a fresh 6 MB array 100 times a second against one reused, three paired 10 s phases: +1.39 cores, `zerotest.py`; measured on a busy PC, maybe less on a quiet one). Most of it was the background jobs' (a `pytest` worker faults 74,000-333,000 pages a second: a `-n 4` job is about 3-7 System cores, on any CPU, which idle priority and the CPU affinity don't hold: D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/APPLY-ops.txt caps the workers while she plays). Of gamelab's, faults a second (from outside the process, psutil; `review-numbers/faultrun.py` around each bench):

| | master | this branch |
|---|---|---|
| the player's watch (4 looks a second, `apply/ld/wd_paced.py`; live a grab's 4 MB more a look, 4,100 a second more either way) | 137,000 | 18,500-19,400 |
| `perceive` hunting at 30 (`apply/perc/prof_service.py`) | 25,700-34,700 | 27,700-37,000 (the same frame work) |
| `perceive` between hunts (master 10 a second, the branch 5) | 13,000 | 4,700-5,100 |
| the recorder's frames (`fix/pack_bench.py`: a fresh grab a frame, packed, 10 a second) | 24,900 | 17,200-17,600 |

At about a core for 100,000, that's about 2.0 System cores of gamelab's while hunting on master (1.8 between hunts) and 0.7 on the branch (0.45), besides the table above.

**The player's Lie Detector look** (`perception/lie_detector.py`, `control/player.py` Watchdog). Each form has always shown at one place (its template's top-left at 1920x1080: the countdown at (910,323) and its second window's title at (1648,589), the notice at (1458,680), the board at (432,124), the quiz's warning at (598,566), the quiz at (604,470), the Truth Booth's timer at (776,40); on every recording and fixture of 2026-10-18 to 26 that showed it, both sizes, the same pixel). So 7 looks in 8 match each form only within 48 px of its places (`near`: on the very pictures the whole frame's look matches, the frame stretched to 1920x1080, halved, grey), and every 8th look (`WHOLE_EVERY`, the first too) the whole frame (`scores`, as before), for a window anywhere else: seen within 8 looks of its first showing, 2 s (the notice comes 10 s before the mini-game, the quiz's warning 30 s before the quiz). `near` is asked only of a frame of the game's own size, 1366x768, with its picture where it belongs: either capture anchor in its place (the minimap's title strip, the HUD's border: `framecheck.anchors_off`, 0.04 ms; `Watchdog.in_place`). Every other look is of the whole frame: while a cycle is in progress (what it records is as ever), on a frame of any other size (a client of 1920x1080, or of another size brought to it: no anchors are measured there), and with neither anchor in its place, as when the game draws its picture off its client (2026-10-25 23:40 and 2026-10-26 00:29: 18 px left and 85 up), where every form is off its places too. The watch's thread sets OpenCV to one thread (it had that only from ultralytics' import, the YOLO shadow's, seconds after the start: its 24 threads cost 0.17-0.24 cores more with the whole frame's look, 0.13 with this one, for no faster a look), the countdown's yellow gate counts its pixels (`cv2.countNonZero`: 0.26 ms at 1920x1080 against the sum's 1.3; the same count), and the stretched frame, its half and its grey are made into pictures the spotter keeps from look to look (`LieDetectorSpotter._kept`, a thread's own: 8 MB of fresh memory a look before, 2,000 page faults). Measured:
- the watchdog's own tick at 4 looks a second on 120 saved hunt frames, OpenCV on one thread (`apply/ld/wd_paced.py`, run through `review-numbers/faultrun.py`, 25 s after 12, two rounds alternated): master 0.55 and 0.58 cores, 137,000 page faults a second; the branch 0.07 and 0.12 cores, 18,500 and 19,400 (before the pictures were kept 0.13 cores, 27,600-28,200);
- every saved frame that can show a form (16,062: the 5 recordings, the last 90 s of the 4 before/ folders, the 6 rolling saves, the 2 alarm clips, the fixtures and the alarm screenshots; the safety review's `review-safety/ld/scan_all.py`): on the 3,821 that show one by master's look, `near` saw it, as the same form; on the 12,241 without, `near` scored at most 0.736 of a threshold (the quiz) and never above the whole frame. The kept pictures change nothing: the scores are master's to the bit (316 frames, both sizes, `near` and `scores` in turn);
- the game drawing off its client: every one of the 16,050 frames at 1366x768, drawn so (18 px left, 85 up, black below), has neither anchor in its place, and every one of the 3,809 showing a form plain has one (`fix/anchors_scan.py`). Before the review `near` saw none of the 2,230 such frames the whole frame sees (its highest 0.58 of a threshold), and the alarm came 1.10 s after a form's first frame on average, 2.10 at the worst;
- from each form's first frame to the alarm, the real `Watchdog.tick` on the 12 saved first showings (8 places in the round times 5 phases each, `review-safety/ld/latency.py`): as they were, 0.125 s on average (0.119-0.130 by showing), the worst 0.392, against master's look's 0.265 (0.416); every frame drawn off the client, 0.248 (0.389) against 0.253 (0.383), the whole frame each look as master's (the two countdowns' first showings, drawn so, are seen within the 3 s replayed by neither look); no run missed one (`fix/out/lat_down.json`, `lat_drawn.json`).
- Tests: `near` scores every saved Lie Detector screen as `scores` does, at both sizes, and the scenes without one no higher; a form pasted somewhere it never showed is unseen by `near` and alarmed at the 9th look; a form on a picture drawn off the client alarmed at its first look; the whole frame every 8th look, in a cycle, at another size, with neither anchor in place, and when the check fails; one anchor is enough.

**The player's key thread** (`Player.run`'s `wait`). It waited for a key's time in 15 ms steps (the cancel file and a retreat's window looked at between), each `_sleep_until`'s, which wakes 2 ms early and spins its last 4 ms: about 3 ms of spinning every 15 ms while a plan ran. Now the steps are slept on the alarm itself (`halt.wait`: it ends at once when the Lie Detector is seen) and only the last 20 ms before a key are `_sleep_until`'s (`FINAL_S`, more than such a wait's lateness: 1-2 ms with the 1 ms timer serve asks for, a tick of 15.6 ms without). Offline, plans back to back (`apply/keywait.py`, 15 s, the 1 ms timer): 0.116 cores against 0.015 (the numbers review: 0.124 against 0.012); the keys as late as before (0.003 ms median, 0.09 ms at the 99th percentile against 0.17); the alarm to the wait's end 0.9 ms (median; 1.7 at most) against 5.3 (11.1). Tests: a plan's keys at their times and not before; the alarm ends a wait for a key 3 s off at once.

**Perception's frame loop** (`perception/nametag.py`). Her name tag is looked for within 80 px across and 40 down of where she was first (`NEAR_FRAME`: about a frame's move; then within `NEAR`'s 240 and 120): 1.60 ms a find against 3.63, the same answers (D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/perception/tag_ab.py, 3,000 finds); still in the whole play area every 30th look (1 s hunting, 6 s between hunts). Every frame's state as master's (her place, her tag, the monsters seen, the tracks and their states, the events: `review-safety/perc/accuracy.py`, the step with its searches inline at a fake 30 a second, seeded throws, `compare.py`) on 3,300 saved frames of four hunts: 600 each of 2026-10-24 23:51, 10-25 00:55, 10-26 00:25 (two stretches, one of them a crowd: 2,387 monsters seen in 600 frames) and 01:01, and 300 of 01:01's crowd. On that crowd, alternated, 131.8 and 139.4 ms of CPU a frame for master, 133.2 and 113.3 for the branch (its searches inline; `fix/out2`).

**Perception's pace** (`localization/service.py`). With a command that hunts, 30 frames a second as before (`ACTIVE_HZ`). Between hunts, 10 a second as before, with `watch`'s searches (`IDLE_HZ`, `IDLE`: 5 a second, her band every second, the screen every 4 s and the drops only when asked made 0.20 cores against 0.83, and were set back the same day: the user, "Don't reduce perception fps please"); a command that hunts attaching wakes the frame loop, and its rate takes from the next frame (`feed.run` brings that frame's due forward). The drops' search runs on 3 threads (`loot.THREADS`, 8 before; on 100 saved frames, `apply/perc/thread_sweep.py`: every platform in view 294 ms of CPU against 431 in the same wall time, 111 ms against 115; her platform 21 against 27, 8.6 ms against 8.2; the same drops found). The service on the saved frames (`apply/perc/prof_service.py`: its real frame loop, a pipe of its own, a minute after 8 s, a fake tour asking as the tours of 2026-10-26 did, 2.2 looks a second and 0.6 throws; two rounds alternated):

**The web page** (`ui/sources.py`, `telemetry.newest`). It looks for a newer command's run once a second, and asked the last write of every file the directory said was written in the last 7 days (`RECENT_S`); now of the last day's (`COMMANDS_RECENT_S`; the service's files keep 7: it runs for days). On the live folder (340 files, 20 of the last day; `apply/newestbench.py`, read only): 1.5 ms of CPU a scan against 14.3, the same file found.

**The recorder** (`control/rolling.py`). A frame's BGR picture, compressed into the ring, is made into one the recorder keeps from frame to frame (`pack`; a thread's own, made again only when the size changes): a new one a frame was 3 MB of fresh memory, 770 page faults. On the bench (`fix/pack_bench.py`: a fresh BGRA picture a frame as mss's grab hands one over, packed at 10 a second into a ring of 3,000, OpenCV on one thread as the recorder runs; 25 s after 8, two rounds alternated): 17,200 and 17,600 page faults a second against 24,900, its CPU the same (0.09-0.10 cores either way); the same bytes packed (22 frames, two sizes). The alarm's clip is written as PNG without its row filters (`PNG_PARAMS`): 31.0 ms of CPU a frame against 54.6, 8% bigger, the same pixels read back (`apply/pngbench.py`, 80 frames of the two clips of 2026-10-26): a clip's 700 frames 17 s of CPU less, in the 4-core burst after an alarm.

**Background work.** A harness worker (`feed.quiet`) set itself to below normal: started at idle, as a background job's should be, it went up. Now one started at idle stays there. The rest is outside the repo (D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/APPLY-ops.txt): every background job started through `bg_quiet.cmd` (idle priority and logical CPUs 12-23, inherited by every child), `-p quietplug` on every pytest run (OpenCV and torch on one thread a worker: 356 perception tests 51 -> 30 CPU-s, the peak 8-11 -> 5 cores), while she plays one such job of 2 workers at most (their page faults: above); and the babysitter without WMI queries (`babysit-new.ps1`: one process list a loop, 0.083 -> 0.005 cores).

**After the review** (two reviewers on the branch as first built, ef2ba91; their scripts and numbers in D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/review-safety and review-numbers, the fixes measured with `fix/run_all.sh` and `run2.sh`):
- *The Lie Detector look was blind to a picture drawn off the client.* Shifted as the game drew it at 23:40 and 00:29, 2,230 frames that master's look sees showed nothing to `near`, and the alarm came 1.1 s late on average, 2.1 at the worst. Now such a frame (neither capture anchor in its place) is looked at whole, every look (above). A form at a place never seen, on a frame in its place, is still seen at the 8th look at the latest (2 s): the quiz and its warning were seen at one showing, the countdown and the Truth Booth at two.
- *A hidden track re-found with its last picture only changed a crowd's tracks.* On 600 frames of 2026-10-26 00:25 the reviewer's run had 142 frames' states different and 2 kills more hers, given to tracks left hidden 10-20 s at one x; the cut had been measured on the frames it was tuned on. Taken back (`MobDetector.refind` tries the animation's next frames for a hidden track as before): every frame as master's on all the stretches (above).
- *20 frames a second hunting* (as first built) is back to 30. In the harness (Tree Dungeon, the hunt loop, `crowd`, zero-walls, the attack choice, costs measured, the simulator's feed at the rate) EXP a minute was 252.4 against 238.4 at 20 on the first 8 seeds: 5.5% less, not the "+5.0% seed by seed" first written (a mean of per-seed ratios two outliers pulled up; the median seed -15%, 3 of 8 better); with 16 more (`review-numbers/hz`) -7.7% over 24 (by 17.5 a seed, standard error 18.5; 10 of 24 better), kills 12.8% fewer, HP lost 3.2% more. And live it would have saved little: 30 asked made 15.1 a second (2026-10-25 and 26, the service's 470 status lines while hunting: 16% of its 10 s windows above 20), so a cap of 20 would have cut 1-3 frames a second, 0.03-0.15 cores. At 20 a glance (63 ms after the last) also asked a frame of its own just after the regular one and waited behind its step (median 27 ms, 70 at the 90th percentile, against 22 and 59 at 30).
- *Her band's search on 3 threads* is back to 6: at 30 frames a second it came 6-7 ms later (median 29 ms against 22-23) for 0.02-0.03 cores (`fix/out/hunt-*.txt`); the harness's feed.COSTS.band is 6 threads' 26 ms.
- *Her tag's whole-area search* is every 30th look again: every 150th (as first built) was 5 s hunting but 30 s between hunts, how long a wrong match could stick.
- *The numbers.* The crowd's 134.7 -> 93.2 ms a frame was one run; alternated, three each, the first build's was 105.4 -> 96.1 (-9%). The table counted the babysitter's cut, which is outside the repo, in the branch's totals: they're apart now. And the page faults' System time wasn't counted at all (above): the player's watch's 137,000 a second on master were about another core and a third, more than its own 0.55-0.58.

**Not done.** The YOLO shadow's threads (torch at 2 threads and KMP_BLOCKTIME 0 on the CPU, where a cycle's checks would take 6.4-6.7 cores; on the GPU 0.09-0.10): `lie_detector_yolo.py` has another session's work in progress, so it's a patch to apply once that's in (D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/apply/8b/8b-yolo-threads.patch; it applies to both); the player's log says now which device the shadow checks on. Cheaper re-finds of hidden tracks with the same answers (their next frames are 4 masked matches of about 2 ms each a track and frame: in a crowd 25 ms of a frame's step), and the service's other fresh pictures a frame (26,000-35,000 faults a second hunting): for later. The hunt's foothold bounds and memos (about 0.007 cores, though the 99th percentile before a key plan 44-53 -> 28-37 ms) and the pet's state in memory (0.001): small. Searching less often while hunting (the whole screen every 2 s, her band every 0.3 s: -0.13 and -0.11 cores) wants a harness A/B of EXP first. Left out, the risk more than the saving: the recorder and the player's watch reading the service's ring (they're the Lie Detector's lines of defence, and must work with it down), the watch at 1 a second between hunts, the YOLO checks gated, the recorder's frames packed as differences, a capture straight into the ring.

**Live checks** (read only, once the user plays): over one hunt and one wait between hunts, `python D:/MapleHelper-scratch/cpu/fix/live_check.py 600 --every 60` (one NtQuerySystemInformation at each end: every process's CPU and page faults, the elevated player's too, and System's): each process's cores and faults a second against the tables above, and System's with no background job running; the service's console line as before while hunting (30 asked, 15.1 a second made on 2026-10-25 and 26) and 5 a second between; and at the next Lie Detector its alarm as soon after its first frame as before (the recording's index: the first frame's time against the alarm's `at`), whatever form it comes as.

## Memory and disk

A hunt runs for hours, and perception is to run all the time, so what the stack keeps has to stay bounded.

- **Monster speeds:** `Tracker.speeds` (what `nav` sums up as the speeds seen) keeps the latest 400 of each kind (`SPEED_SAMPLES` in `localization/monsters.py`). Kept all, one a monster a look, a look every frame would add about 110,000 an hour.
- **The drop store** (`behavior/ledger.py`) drops a pile 5 minutes after looks have seen it gone (`GONE_KEEP_S`); kept for ever, every kill added one, and every look went through all of them. The hunt loop's count of the piles she went for that were seen gone (its progress, for the stuck guard) is kept apart (`DropStore.gone_tried`), so it decides exactly as before. In the simulator's hunt loop (preset `game`, seeds 0-2, 20 minutes each) the store ended with 77, 82 and 112 piles instead of 280, 257 and 338 (at most 90-123 at a time), with the same EXP and the same counts after every look. Piles never seen gone, on platforms she didn't look at again, stay: 6-20 at the end of those runs.
- **Telemetry** (`gamelab/telemetry.py`): live hunting wrote 72 KB a minute (4.2 MB an hour; the looks' `scene` records the most of it, 21 KB a minute at 257 bytes each), and a feed of 10 looks a second would write 10-15 MB an hour, in one file per run that was never deleted. A run's file now goes on in a new part past 64 MB (`MAX_BYTES`): a file of its own, named for the time the part starts and always later than the part before, so it sorts after it; its first record is the run's session record with `continues` (the part before's file name) and `part` (2, 3, ...). The part being written is the file written to last, so the mini-map window and the web page (`telemetry.newest`, by last write) go on to it, the rest of the part before read first, without starting over (`ui.sources.LiveSource`: a file whose first record `continues` the one followed isn't a new run). They had started over at each part, as at a new run, and lost what a run sends once: the tour's and the hunt loop's safe platforms and grounds, coloured on the page, with her trail and the log. On NTFS the last write os.scandir reads is the directory's copy, brought up to date only when a file is opened or closed: a part still being written that nothing else opens shows as written when it was made, 0 bytes long. A tour that finished after `watch` began so stayed the newest until `watch`'s part closed, hours later, the page frozen on it. `newest` asks os.stat of the files the directory says were written in the last 7 days (`RECENT_S`; a part is open for 15 hours at most, hunting), and `prune` of every file, which had counted such a part as empty. When a run starts, and again each time a run goes on in a new part, the oldest telemetry files are deleted until the rest take at most 2 GB (`KEEP_BYTES`; `prune`, never the part being written); it says so when it deletes any. None is deleted for its age (`KEEP_DAYS` is None; a number of days would delete files last written longer ago too): live telemetry is evidence that later fits go back to (the terrain and geometry fits of 2026-10-20 were made from the live runs of 2026-10-19 and 20), and small old files cost next to no disk (the first two days of live runs were 38 files and 2.9 MB). The size cap alone bounds the disk. Pruning only at the start had let a run left going for days, such as `watch`, grow the folder without bound. A file another run is still writing can't be deleted on Windows and is left.
- **The Lie Detector's recordings** (`lab/lie_detector`, a folder a cycle: see The Lie Detector's recordings): about 175 MB a cycle answered in 2.5 minutes, at most 700 MB (the 10-minute cap), at 1366×768; half as much again at 1920×1080. When one starts, the oldest go until the rest take 10 GB (`lie_recorder.KEEP_BYTES`; about 58 such cycles), never the one being written: the folder holds 10 GB and the one being written at most. With the rolling recorder's 5 minutes before each (`before/`), about 0.8 GB a cycle answered and 12 of them: the recorder prunes to the 10 GB too, the parts counted, whenever it writes one, as a player older than 2026-10-24 doesn't count them.
- **The rolling recording** (`control/rolling.py`; see The rolling recording): at most 4 GB of frames in memory (`MAX_BYTES`; 2.2 GB for its 5 minutes at 1366×768, kept losslessly since 2026-10-25; 1 GB, 650 MB, a 712 MB process, as JPEG before), and a save being written holds the frames it took until they're written. The alarms' clips, PNG, keep 30 GB in `lab/alarm_clips`. On disk, `lab/recent` keeps 5 GB besides the newest save (`RECENT_KEEP_BYTES`), and the part before each Lie Detector counts in the Lie Detector's 10 GB, pruned to by the recorder as it writes one (and by the player as a recording starts, if its code is new enough to count the parts).
- **The player's log** (`lab/player.log`): about 40 bytes a plan, some 150 KB an hour of hunting, never cut. When the player starts, a log past 5 MB is moved to `player.log.1` (the one before goes) and a new one begins (`LOG_MAX_BYTES` in `control/player.py`; the standard library only, so the elevated player loads nothing more).
- **Cancel files** (`lab/inbox/<plan id>.cancel`): a plan cut short as it ended left its cancel file for good (25 had piled up, the player deleting them only when it starts). Now no cancel file is made for a plan that's over (the player renames a plan to `.running` when it takes it and deletes that only once its keys are let go of), and reading a plan's result deletes its cancel file (`control/client.py`).
- **What's there:** `python -m gamelab lab-usage` sizes each kind. On 2026-10-20 the main `gamelab/lab` held 1.5 GB: recordings 1.2 GB (64, made only on request: `--record`, `combo`, `calibrate`), screenshots 277 MB (255), golden traces 2.2 MB, telemetry 2.8 MB (38 files), harness runs 0.5 MB, the rest 0.4 MB. Recordings and screenshots are measurement evidence and are never deleted for you (the Lie Detector's recordings past their 10 GB, when one starts).
- **Soak runs** (`python -m gamelab.sim.soak frames|sim [--minutes 10] [--no-trace]`; minutes long, so not in pytest): the stack kept going offline while Python's allocations (tracemalloc) and the process's private bytes are sampled every 10 s; after a warm-up (the first fifth) it gives the growth an hour on the samples' line and what grew, by line and by part of the code. Telemetry goes to a temp folder in parts of 1 MB, so rotation runs too. Measured on 2026-10-20, other work sharing the CPU:
  - `frames`, 10 minutes: 5,900 real looks at the 17 saved frames (each frame 30 looks in a row; every 5th look with the drops on her platform, every 30th the whole screen; after each the hunter's listeners, the monster tracker, a glance and telemetry). Python's memory was 152.8 MB after the warm-up and 153.0 at the end, +0.4 MB an hour on the line. Of the 198 KB the stack gained, 145 KB was one line of the monster search's spot lists, the same after 1 minute as after 10 (freed tuples Python keeps for reuse), and 14 KB the ledger's 151 kills (about 100 bytes each, kept for the whole hunt). The private bytes moved between 230 and 262 MB as the big looks came and went.
  - `frames --no-trace`, 30 minutes: 29,184 looks (16 a second). Private bytes 197-237 MB, the first third's mean 219.0 and the last's 221.9: +8 MB an hour on the line, inside that swing. A run of hours would tell a slow creep in OpenCV or numpy from noise.
  - `sim`, 10 minutes: 5.6 simulated hours of the hunt loop (seed 0, preset `game`; 1,197 kills of hers). The stack grew 100 KB in the last 4.5 hours, the ledger's kills; the simulator's own lists of every plan and log line grew 8.9 MB, which a real run doesn't keep. At the end the drop store held 112 piles (7 not gone), the landing cache 2,721 entries (2,718 once the planner is built), and telemetry had gone through 30 parts.
- **Still kept for the whole run:** the ledger's kills (about 100 bytes each, 200-400 an hour; `hunt.py` counts them by their index), and piles never seen gone.
- **The continuous feed** (see Continuous perception) holds a fixed amount: 4 frames in shared memory, the last 8 states, at most 10,000 events not drained, only monsters seen in the last 10 s. `soak feed` (the real child on the saved frames, the stack's side asking as a fight does, 15 minutes): the stack's side's Python memory 17.7 MB after the warm-up and 17.9 at the end, its private bytes 66.5 and 67.2; the child's private bytes swung between 156 and 197 MB; 6 minutes more after her band's search went by time, 66.5 and 67.3 MB on the stack's side, and the child's last third's mean 175.3 MB against its first's 181.4 (162-196). The step itself under tracemalloc for 8 minutes in one process: Python's memory 2.0 MB throughout, the private bytes' last third 183.9 MB against the first's 193.1 (161-199 as the big searches came and went). `watch` writes about 16 MB of telemetry an hour (see Continuous perception). With the searches on a thread of the child's own (2026-10-21), 15 minutes of the same with the CPU busy with other work: the stack's side's Python memory 11.8 MB after the warm-up and 12.9 at the end (0.9 MB of it the soak's own record of each state's times, kept for its report), its private bytes 58.4 and 63.4; the child's private bytes 180.5 and 182.0 (165.9-186.3; the first third's mean 179.9, the last's 182.1).

## Limits

- **Recalibrate** after moving the game window, changing maps or changing resolution (each client size keeps its own calibration). Her name-tag template is kept between calibrations; `calibrate --fresh` rebuilds it, and should be run where no rope crosses her name (at 1366x768 on West Rocky Mountain II the name tag finder doesn't find her tag over the clouds: pass `--tag X,Y`; see Screen size).
- **`hunt` needs a 1920×1080 or 1366×768 game window** with a calibration at that size, and the size it had when the command started (see Screen size). The combo analysis on recordings (`track.airborne`'s lift, the claw's swing boxes, `scroll`'s strip and patches, `analyze.UP_PX`) converts its px, measured on the 1920×1080 window's half-size recordings, by the recording's geometry; no recording at 1366×768 has been made to check them on.
- **Each new monster type needs a colour gate** before its game sprites are used: a body-colour test in `PALETTES` in `perception/mobs.py` (the first map's four), or one learned against its map's background colours, which `learn-colours` measures on saved frames of the map (see Perception: learned gates). Its templates come from the game data automatically.
- **Her name tag can be hidden** behind screen widgets at the map's edge, like the party HP box. She's then placed on screen from the minimap dot and the camera, which is only good to about 11 map units.
- **Camera correction for recordings needs ground with a pattern** under her, like a girder or tiles. Plain ground can't be tracked.
- **Swing detection is tuned to the claw's white/lavender swing effect.** Other weapons or skills may need `SWING_MIN_PIXELS` in `analysis/track.py` adjusted.
- **Lucky Seven costs 11 MP a cast at her skill level 10** (the game data; config.toml `[character] lucky_seven`). A 32-shot test uses about 350 MP. Use `--regular-attack` for long runs. Hunting throws Lucky Seven but to finish a monster and while her MP is under a cast (`--attack choice`, the default); `--attack regular` throws the regular attack only.
- **How far above or below a star reaches** beyond 60 units isn't measured. `hunt shots` sums up the shot log.
- **Other players** hunt this map too. Platforms are often empty, and stops then end with no kills. Their minimap dots are seen (Other players), but only inside `MINIMAP_BOX` (300 px wide: this map's minimap; Kerning City's runs past it), and two within 4 game px (46 map units) of each other read as one. Nothing yet knows how much of a ground they take, so the hunt loop doesn't steer clear of them (`hunt.PLAYER_DISCOUNT` off), nor leave the piles by them (`hunt.PLAYER_PILES` off). Their dots are followed from look to look; which marker the game draws over which (hers or theirs) isn't known.
- **EXP from anything else** isn't a kill when no kill of the map's monsters gives that much (Her EXP number), but a share of 2, 4, 6 or 8 is taken for snails, and one that adds up to kills for those kills. Kinds that give the same EXP can't be told apart by it. The EXP table (meowdb.com's) is checked at levels 19 and 20 only. At another EXP rate (an event, a coupon) the sizes go off after 8 kills and each change is one kill, kinds not named, until `gains.EXP_RATE` is set to it. A kill in the very look the cursor comes over the number or leaves it is taken with it (its EXP counts, the kill not).
- **Loot templates cover the drops of the maps `perception/drops.py` knows** (the first map's, Tree Dungeon's and West Rocky Mountain II's: Perception, drops of each map); another map's monsters need their drops added there from their meowdb pages, and the drops' prices to `looting.PRICES` (a test checks them against the game data). Equipment, scrolls, and bills and sacks of mesos aren't looked for; she still picks them up when she walks over them. Nor do they have a price in the hunt loop's table: a kind the detector named that isn't there would count as worth the first map's dearest drop, 50.
- **The full-inventory message** is "You can't get anymore items." It's in the client, not the data files, so `loot.py` matches that and the usual other wordings. Without the OCR packages, only the 3-in-a-row rule tells.
- **The simulator is a model:** its timings and physics are fitted to gamelab's own measurements, its monsters are pushed back by a hit as far as one recording measured and never leave their platform, and its perception is exact unless a scenario adds noise. Use it to check that a change does what was meant, then confirm in game.
